IMPLEMENTATION AUTHORITY AND READING MAP

This file contains the complete requirements, mathematics, algorithms, data
contracts, interfaces, defaults, test fixtures, and five-part execution plan.
Implement the behavior defined here. Every instructional cross-reference names
a section inside this file. No earlier script, separate plan, website, paper,
conversation, or separately written specification is an implementation input.

The instructions start with the complete P50 and risk-envelope specification,
followed by the complete two QQQ data contracts, then the application work plan.
The P50 reference solver and offline/fast inference details are colocated in the
P50 portion; they are not deferred to a later code-inspection task.

Internal identifiers:
  SRC-E = P50 engine objectives, campaign state, risk envelope and build/query rules.
  SRC-D = P50 deadline mathematics, data quality, reporting and reference behavior.
  P50-Rxx = complete numerical reference-engine algorithms and validation fixtures.
  P50-Mxx = complete managed-policy, offline pack and fast-inference algorithms.
  SRC-A = retained terminal navigation/reporting requirements, adapted below.
  SRC-C = complete QQQ raw_theta options data contract.
  SRC-B = complete QQQ underlying OHLCV data contract.
  APP Sections 00-31 = integrated desktop implementation, in five work parts.
  APP-Cxx = concrete app/data/account/lifecycle/UI algorithms in APP Section 30.

A bare chapter number inside SRC-E or SRC-D refers to that same block. In the
application work plan, a bare Section number refers to APP. An E/D prefix or
SRC-E/SRC-D explicitly names the matching P50 block. All identifiers resolve here.

Binding decisions for this revision:
  1. Build one historical QQQ desktop simulator. Local data rules SRC-B/C apply.
  2. Build the reference P50 engine from SRC-D plus P50-R; qualify fast HOLD and
     managed operators using SRC-E plus P50-M. No unavailable legacy code is needed.
  3. P50-R/P50-M supply the concrete algorithms wherever an earlier paragraph
     described a choice generally. APP-C supplies the concrete simulator profiles.
     Explicit scope limits and unsupported-data states remain binding.
  4. New input snapshots reuse one validated pack. Expensive numerical work stays
     in explicit offline build/validation commands. Preserve the frozen campaign
     dollar target and original benchmarks through every adjustment and roll.
  5. Finite numerical accuracy, finite action coverage and simulated execution
     are stated explicitly. No claim of proprietary broker-algorithm identity,
     exact historical counterfactual fills, or guaranteed market outcomes follows.
  6. Live broker/SPX/Termux material describes conditional compatibility interfaces.
     The QQQ replay, its P50 engine, and all release gates work without broker login.
     Unsupported optional adapters do not block the defined historical simulator.

Quotes, actual QQQ archive files, contract records and explicit scenario values
are runtime data. Python packages are implementation dependencies. Model packs,
tests, caches and application code are outputs built by the instructions here.
None of these is a missing source of mathematical or procedural instructions.
Do not substitute invented historical data for missing runtime observations.

Status: implementation specification. This revision does not claim that the app
or its model pack has already been implemented, calibrated, or benchmarked.

BEGIN SRC-E — UNIVERSAL P50 AND RISK-ENVELOPE CONTRACT
===============================================================================

UNIVERSAL P50 / STRATEGY RISK-ENVELOPE ENGINE
DETAILED IMPLEMENTATION PLAN FOR AN LLM SOFTWARE ENGINEER
Specification version: 1.0
Prepared: 2026-11-08
Status: DESIGN SPECIFICATION ONLY. No code has been written, executed, or validated by this document.

===============================================================================
00. READ FIRST: THE TASK, THE CONTRACT, AND THE ARCHITECTURE
===============================================================================

YOU ARE IMPLEMENTING ONE SYSTEM, NOT A COLLECTION OF ALTERNATIVE VERSIONS.

Goal: Build one reusable Python engine that (A) rapidly estimates the probability
that an arbitrary supported SPY option position reaches its ORIGINAL 50%-of-max-
profit target by a specified New York deadline when held unchanged, and (B)
rapidly computes an attainable, risk-constrained ENVELOPE of desirable future
position characteristics that guides the human trader through changing market
conditions and optional rolls. The user chooses actual trades and adjustments.

The fundamental performance requirement is AMORTIZATION:
  * BUILD, OFFLINE, ONCE PER APPROVED MODEL SPECIFICATION: perform as much costly
    Monte Carlo, high-resolution option pricing, first-hit region construction,
    policy iteration, fitting, stress testing, and validation as required.
  * QUERY, REPEATEDLY: accept fresh market prices, IV/skew, time, existing legs,
    cash/P&L history, limits, and objectives; reuse the SAME stored model pack.
    Do NOT rerun high-cost Monte Carlo, full price-region root solving, global
    policy iteration, or model training during a normal query.
  * Changing ordinary market conditions MUST be treated as a new INPUT to the
    existing model, not a reason to retrain. An unsupported market state must
    return OUTSIDE_MODEL_DOMAIN; no silent extrapolation or heavy rebuild.
  * Small, deterministic, vectorized calculations at query time ARE allowed:
    obtain quotes, resolve contracts, calculate exact payoff extrema, reprice
    with reusable bases, calculate Greeks, interpolate, evaluate the surrogate,
    search a bounded candidate set, and verify a proposed trade's constraints.

The output is GUIDANCE, not an auto-trading order:
  * Explain WHERE the portfolio is now.
  * Explain the feasible range of WHERE its exposures/payoff should be next.
  * Show the resulting probability/risk trade-offs and target feasibility.
  * Permit the user to choose HOW to construct the recommended risk profile.
  * Fast-check each user-proposed concrete adjustment; do not assume identical
    Delta/Gamma/Vega implies identical P50 or maximum loss.
  * NO order submission, position modification, trading automation or access to
    unrelated sensitive broker account identity information is required.

Fundamental honesty:
  * A finite model cannot exactly cover every position, every future IV process,
    every strike/expiration, and every possible execution at arbitrary accuracy.
    This is a parameterized approximation with a validated operating domain.
  * No future market outcome or 50% profit success probability can be guaranteed.
  * The initial P50 cannot always be restored after adverse moves; the model
    MUST identify infeasibility instead of suggesting increased risk as a cure.
  * Probabilities are CONDITIONAL ON A STATED MARKET MODEL, not empirical or
    guaranteed real-world success rates. This P50 model is NOT proven to be
    numerically identical to tastytrade's proprietary implementation.
  * Without historical data, IV-factor transitions, jump behavior, and future
    fills cannot be uniquely inferred from a single current option chain.

IMPLEMENTER HANDOFF / READING ORDER:
  Read 00-03 first (problem, event definitions, persistent state); then 04-09
  (architecture, offline build, live query, risk envelope); then 12-18
  (APIs, code layout, tests, delivery). M0 implements the complete P50-R
  reference algorithm and fixtures written in this document. P50-M supplies
  the complete reusable-pack, training, managed-model and query algorithms.
  Treat M0-M6 as the execution checklist; no prior script is required.

Priority of authority:
  1. These requirements for the NEW reusable engine.
  2. The complete P50-R mathematical/accounting/data-quality contracts and
     P50-M implementation algorithms included in this same document.
  3. The corresponding in-document fixtures, measured validation, and release
     gates for the implementation being built.
  4. No speculative API fields, broker semantics, model accuracy or capabilities.

All implementation instructions are contained here. The complete deadline
calculation, terminal input workflow, QQQ data contracts, reference solver,
fast-pack construction, managed policy, and simulator integration follow in
this same file. The integrated application uses historical QQQ replay as its
default market source; the mathematical core accepts the normalized snapshot
contract and does not require a live broker or an earlier calculator.

===============================================================================
01. SUCCESS CRITERIA AND NON-NEGOTIABLE INVARIANTS
===============================================================================

SC-01. A user can build and validate a reusable model pack offline, save it to
       disk, then run hundreds of different position/strategy queries without
       rerunning heavy offline numerical work.
SC-02. Query results respond to changing SPY spot/forward, IV level/skew,
       current time, expiration, rates/carry, transaction costs, position legs,
       target deadline, initial cash/P&L and risk limits.
SC-03. Both HOLD-UNCHANGED P50 and MANAGED/POLICY SUCCESS PROBABILITY are
       available, clearly separated and not mislabeled as the same event.
SC-04. An original-dollar target from the INITIAL position persists across
       holds, closures, reentries, rolls and adjustments. It is never reset
       because the new spread has a different maximum profit.
SC-05. Cumulative gross/net P&L, fees, remaining downside capacity, and broker
       buying power are evaluated at the CAMPAIGN level, not trade by trade.
SC-06. A maintained probability benchmark is a DESIRED THRESHOLD subject to
       feasibility. If unreachable, show the maximum attainable modeled
       probability and the deficit relative to the initial benchmark.
SC-07. The envelope is of REACHABLE/IMPLEMENTABLE risk profiles, not free
       floating ideal Greeks or structures with fictitious fills.
SC-08. Every exact user-proposed adjustment is validated on its full payoff,
       attainable execution assumptions, BP, cumulative risk and estimated
       continuation probability. Greeks alone cannot authorize an adjustment.
SC-09. The same evaluated model pack drives P50, optimization and proposal
       checking. No disconnected proxy probabilities with incompatible math.
SC-10. Implement and regression-test the high-fidelity P50-R reference in this
       document and its independent analytical fixtures; preserve its event
       definition in the fast operator. No prior calculator is required.
SC-11. No look-ahead, false precision, silently invented IV/rates/quotes or
       misidentified expirations; use as-of market snapshot semantics.
SC-12. Read-only tastytrade connectivity. Secrets are never included in result
       files, diagnostics or model-pack artifacts.
SC-13. All outputs include uncertainty/quality diagnostics and validity range.
SC-14. No lengthy simulation/repricing task is triggered by an ordinary QUERY
       just because the new snapshot differs from the offline build snapshot.
SC-15. Separate versioned MODEL_BUILD and FAST_QUERY code paths; hard fail if
       a query attempts to enter an offline-only expensive routine.

Recommended numerical QUALITY TARGETS, subject to empirical demonstration:
  * Reference hold P50: aim for <= 1.0 percentage point absolute error on at
    least 99% of representative, supported out-of-sample test cases; investigate
    and disclose all material worst-case errors. Tighten near policy decisions.
  * Managed-policy probability: aim for <= 2.0 percentage points error versus
    independent rollouts with the actual returned policy, plus measured regret
    versus a more expensive offline control benchmark where feasible.
  * Risk/BP constraints: zero accepted known violations in deterministic tests.
  * Query latency: design for seconds, not minutes, on the user's Windows PC.
    Benchmark separately for single-position P50, envelope, and proposal checks.
  * These are development acceptance targets, NOT claimed measured results.
  * If tolerances cannot be met, mark the failing domain unsupported; never
    silently lower precision or fabricate a pass.

===============================================================================
02. TWO DIFFERENT PROBABILITY OBJECTIVES: DO NOT CONFLATE THEM
===============================================================================

2.1 HOLD-UNCHANGED P50 (legacy-compatible metric)

Definition: If the currently specified collection of option legs is left
unchanged, what is the model probability that its theoretical closing P&L
FIRST reaches the specified target by a chosen eligible NY-time deadline?
For an expiry reference, keep terminal settlement success separately labeled.
This is a first-hit event, not an endpoint-only probability.

Required semantics, fully defined by the P50 instructions in this document:
  - Coherent snapshot with actual t0; actual option expiration/fixing T.
  - A separate deadline t_star, NEVER substituted for actual expiry in pricing.
  - Current path model: GBM with specified r-q/forward anchor and path IV.
  - Frozen per-leg IV marks for future option valuations in that profile.
  - Supported American ETF pricing / European index pricing, respectively.
  - Eligible option-observation sessions, closed-period treatment, expiry
    terminal settlement convention, and first-hit detection.
  - Original entry cash vs hypothetical entry quotes explicitly distinguished.
  - Finite max profit as a prerequisite for 50%-of-max-profit target.
  - Model-quality/sampling/grid uncertainty, quote timestamps and provenance.

2.2 MANAGED STRATEGY / EXECUTED-TARGET SUCCESS PROBABILITY

Definition: Given the current campaign state, which probability of REALIZING the
fixed target by the allowed deadline is attainable under a stated set of future
feasible actions, execution-cost assumptions, market dynamics and constraints?
A policy must actually make a closing/realization decision when the target is
achieved. Merely touching a theoretical mid price is not a guaranteed fill.

Model the trade campaign as a controlled process:
  state -> choose feasible hold/adjust/close/roll -> market transition ->
  next state -> ... -> realized success OR deadline/failure.

Important: "managed P50" is NOT the same numerical quantity as legacy P50.
Use distinct names, event definitions and assumptions in outputs; show a
mark-touch analogue only as a diagnostic where helpful.

2.3 BASELINE PROBABILITY TO MAINTAIN

At opening, freeze:
  initial_position_id, original net entry basis, original maximum expiry profit
  M0, original dollar target H0 = 0.50*M0, original requested deadline T0,
  permitted roll/extension rules, ultimate campaign cutoff T_hard,
  initial benchmark p0 and its model/provenance identifier.

Make p0 explicitly one of:
  p0_hold = original HOLD P50 by the declared deadline, OR
  p0_policy = original optimized EXECUTED-TARGET probability under declared
              policy constraints and horizon.
Default presentation: show BOTH and compare future policy values consistently
against p0_policy; show p0_hold for continuity with the original user's P50.
Do not compare unlike targets or silently switch deadlines.

If an existing campaign has no trustworthy saved original entry snapshot or
initial p0, the initial probability benchmark is UNKNOWN; do not fabricate it.
Calculate current conditional metrics normally and offer a baseline comparison
only when the original terms and model basis are recoverable or user supplied.


A roll may move individual option expiries. It does NOT quietly move the fixed
campaign cutoff or reset H0. If the system permits a formally declared deadline
extension, report the revised horizon AND resulting changed probability basis
alongside the original benchmark. Never use an unbounded rolling horizon.

2.4 CONDITIONAL PROBABILITY, NOT RETROACTIVE HISTORY

At later time t, output a conditional forward probability GIVEN the current
state and any confirmed target-achieved status. If the campaign is already
completed, show achieved/closed; if it is still active, condition on being
uncompleted. Do not count an unobserved past theoretical mark touch as a
realized profit. Preserve actual realized entries in the cash ledger.

===============================================================================
03. DEFINE THE CAMPAIGN STATE (MARKOV SUFFICIENCY)
===============================================================================

Current state contains MORE than Delta and IV. Use typed structures.

State components:
  A. Market state:
     symbol/product adapter; actual NY as-of timestamp/session status;
     current underlying spot or valid implied forward; r/carry/dividend proxy;
     full relevant IV surface / expiry term structure; ATM IV, skew and curvature;
     actual option bid/ask and source timestamps; model factor coordinates;
     liquidity/spread/quote-coverage diagnostics.
  B. Open portfolio:
     exact contract identifiers, P/C, strikes, expiries, deliverables,
     multipliers, signed quantities, current quote marks, option style;
     current theoretical liquidation surface; Delta/Gamma/Vega/Theta and
     optionally higher-order sensitivity; payoff shape/tail bounds.
  C. Campaign ledger:
     original M0/H0/p0, initial and hard deadlines, original entry basis;
     all executed cash flows and fees, past closes/rolls, realized net P&L;
     modeled cost of liquidating all open legs; successful-exit flag;
     total consumed/reserved risk budget and current buying-power usage.
  D. Constraints:
     BP_CAP = $5,000, LOSS_CAP = $1,000 (current project defaults);
     applicable per-position/aggregate limits; tradable contract universe;
     time constraints, feasible strikes/quantities, spread/slippage model;
     allowable action/roll types, cumulative transaction costs.
  E. Numerical/method identity:
     model_id, dynamics/scenario specification, uncertainty bounds, validity
     domain, current quote-quality state and interpolator coverage.

NOT ACCEPTABLE as a state representation: {net Delta, ATM IV, DTE} alone.
Different portfolios sharing those numbers can have disjoint profit regions,
different assignment risks and radically different worst-case losses.

Use a TWO-LAYER state representation:
  1. Canonical exact state: ALL legs + ledger + market data (authoritative).
  2. Compressed surrogate input: cash/risk features AND a normalized multi-node
     value/payoff shape across spot and IV shocks, plus expiry structure.
     This avoids forcing arbitrarily many option legs into a fixed number of
     fields while preserving meaningful nonlinear payoff information.

Do not claim the compressed representation is perfectly sufficient for every
multi-expiration, early-assignment or path-dependent position. Out-of-coverage
portfolios must be detected and not assigned false high-confidence probabilities.

===============================================================================
04. ONE RECOMMENDED ARCHITECTURE
===============================================================================

NAME: Amortized Stochastic-Control Engine (ASCE), exposing the Probability-
Preserving Risk Envelope (PPRE) to the user.

One implementation, internally separated into layers:

  [LIVE BROKER SNAPSHOT] --- [EXACT POSITION + CAMPAIGN LEDGER]
                       \ /
                    [NORMALIZER]
                         |
                 [OFFLINE MODEL PACK]
                         |
             +-----------+------------+
             |                        |
      [FAST HOLD-P50]         [FAST CONTROL/VALUE]
             |                        |
             +----------+-------------+
                        |
             [FEASIBILITY / ENVELOPE]
                        |
                [EXPLAIN / VERIFY]

OFFLINE MODEL PACK contains:
  1. Parameterized normalized underlying/state transition operators.
  2. Parameterized reusable option-pricing bases/kernels and interpolation data,
     including an American-exercise-aware ETF component when supported.
  3. First-hit probability operator learned or tabulated from high-fidelity
     offline oracle solutions over many arbitrary payoff/position shapes.
  4. A constrained future-value/control-policy model, trained offline using
     the SAME market/pricing/accounting specification and feasible actions.
  5. Calibration/quality/error surfaces and validity-domain detectors.
  6. Calendar/product rule version and model-assumption manifest.

The query engine is NOT a literal lookup table of specific SPY prices/strikes.
Inputs are normalized to relative moneyness, relative returns, IV factors,
remaining time, cash target vs current liquidation value, and risk budgets.
Thus a different SPY level or IV today maps to another point in the SAME
continuous parameter domain rather than demanding a new simulation.

SELECTED numerical approach:
  * Build precise offline oracle labels using pricing grids, region checking,
    deterministic probability transport/first-hit solvers where robust, and
    high-quality independent Monte Carlo for comparison and validation.
  * Fit an amortized probability operator for HOLD-P50 that accepts a compact
    representation of each actual portfolio and current market state.
  * Train one approximate dynamic-programming value/policy model for feasible
    campaign decisions, with state-action values rather than blindly issuing
    particular trades.
  * At query time use the trained operators and cheap exact checks. In borderline
    cases, optionally use a BOUNDED fast deterministic kernel sweep that reuses
    the precomputed transition/pricing bases. This is not Monte Carlo or global
    price-region recomputation; its runtime budget must be measured and capped.
  * No automatic online training or expensive oracle fallback.

This is a chosen engineering architecture, not a list of models to choose from.
Different numerical routines serve construction, production inference and
independent verification inside the SAME system.

===============================================================================
05. MARKET MODEL: USE CURRENT CONDITIONS WITHOUT REBUILDING
===============================================================================

5.1 CURRENT DEFAULT CONSISTENCY

The reference HOLD profile in this document is a conditional GBM path model with a chosen
path IV and frozen individual leg IVs for repricing. Preserve this as the
benchmark definition for a legacy-comparable HOLD P50 output. American ETF
option marks require an exercise-aware numerical method. Index products need
forward/fixing adapters where relevant. Do not claim this is tastytrade's
undisclosed exact engine or a full dynamic arbitrage-free volatility surface.

5.2 NORMALIZATION (KEY TO REUSABILITY)

Use coordinates such as:
  x = log(S / S_reference) or log(forward-relative-moneyness);
  k_i = log(K_i / S_reference) or log(K_i / forward);
  tau = ACT/365F remaining actual seconds converted to years;
  sigma_path, ATM IV level, term slope, put/call skew, smile curvature;
  r, q (or forward + discount factor); model option/product type;
  normalized target gap and P&L relative to original H0 and loss budget.

Absolute spot changes should be handled by repricing in normalized coordinates,
not by constructing new SPY-specific simulation paths or quote-specific tables.
Include option expirations as actual timestamps for pricing/observation.
Preserve exact USD multiplication, dollar targets and quantity-dependent BP.

5.3 FUTURE IV: THE UNSOLVED OBSERVABILITY ISSUE

A single present option chain does NOT uniquely reveal the stochastic law of
future ATM IV, skew and term structure. The baseline HOLD-P50 result uses the
existing frozen-IV convention. For the control model, define and version a
single explicit, conservative joint spot/IV-factor transition specification,
with named ASSUMPTIONS and stress sensitivities. It may include bounded assumed
IV-factor innovations and spot/IV dependence; parameters cannot be advertised
as empirically calibrated without suitable data.

Feed CURRENT IV/skew/term into that precomputed model at query time. The
estimated probability for a future policy is CONDITIONAL ON the assumed IV
transitions. Include adverse IV/skew stress diagnostics and uncertainty bands.
Do not infer a real-world IV mean-reversion rate from an instantaneous quote.
If sufficiently defensible IV-factor transitions cannot be specified, restrict
policy numbers to a stated frozen-IV/sensitivity scenario, mark dynamic-IV
probabilities as unsupported, and do not pretend they are unconditional.

5.4 PRODUCT AND SESSION MODEL

In each step, option exercise/price behavior and possibility of taking an action
depend on the actual product and NY session calendar. Spot may diffuse during
weekends/nights under the approved clock while closes and rolls are allowed ONLY
at eligible option-trading times. Expiration fixing and terminal exercise are
handled separately. Extended-hours eligibility must be verified per product.

5.5 MARKET INPUT CHANGES

Inside the supported pack domain, all the following are ordinary query inputs:
  - SPY price changes; spreads/IV changes; current option quotes;
  - realized time passage and DTE changes; changes in r/carry;
  - changed positions, strikes, quantities or campaign realized P&L;
  - changed target deadline WITHIN fixed approved horizon configuration.
None of these is permission to retrain. Outside the validated model assumptions
or numerical domain: return diagnostic and require an explicit new OFFLINE build
for extended coverage. Do not extrapolate a high-confidence recommendation.

===============================================================================
06. PERSISTENT CAMPAIGN ACCOUNTING, COSTS, AND HARD CONSTRAINTS
===============================================================================

6.1 ORIGINAL TARGET

At entry, compute exact maximum expiration profit M0 for the initial structure
under the selected, validated entry basis; H0 = 0.50 * M0. Require 0 < M0 < INF.
For existing positions, original fill/cash history must be supplied or fetched
from authorized READ-ONLY transaction/position information. A new hypothetical
trade may use broker bid/ask midpoint only if labeled hypothetical.

Example (illustrative, not market data): initial max profit $200 -> original
target H0=$100. After a later loss or roll, H0 remains $100 total campaign
profit, not half of the new spread's potential profit.

6.2 LEDGER

Maintain immutable executed events:
  timestamp, transaction/action ID, exact contract, signed quantity, execution
  cash flow, commissions/fees, exchange/assignment effects where applicable,
  realized stock/option position changes, broker source/provenance.
Aggregate by campaign, not by trade ticket. Current liquidation P&L is:
  sum of all known signed net cash flows + current market liquidation value of
  all remaining exposures - prospective liquidation costs (when net modeled).

Bid/ask-based executable-side estimates and midpoint theoretical P&L must be
separate fields. Do not imply a theoretical mid-price threshold was filled.
Track current realized P&L independently of unrealized mark; assignment must
produce stock/cash legs or an explicit UNSUPPORTED_EXPOSURE state.

6.3 MAX LOSS VS BP

Enforce both:
  BP_used_after_action <= $5,000 (or the user-configured cap);
  total campaign loss exposure <= $1,000 under the DECLARED risk convention.

For a truly HARD maximum dollar-loss claim, require a conservative, verified
bound on cumulative losses for all permitted resulting portfolios and actions,
including realized losses and liquidation/assignment exposure. A stop-loss
threshold or modeled quantile does NOT guarantee a pathwise $1,000 maximum;
execution gaps, early assignment and slippage may violate it. Distinguish:
  structural defined-risk bound (if actually verifiable),
  model stress loss / CVaR (probabilistic guardrail), and
  realized loss-to-date.
No unsupported claim that a broker's BP charge is a guaranteed dollar loss.

Use a broker-verified buying-power figure when provided; otherwise calculate a
conservative product/strategy-specific estimate and mark the provenance. On
actual candidate checks, use the stricter verified applicable limit. Reject
naked or assignment-exposed structures when hard bounds are unavailable.

6.4 ROLLING AND HORIZON

A roll is a sequence of actual closes/opens with real credit/debit and fees, not
just moving a strike marker. Future expirations may move; account for the cost
and new max downside. Every campaign must have a FINITE hard cutoff. Define
and persist T_hard and the maximum allowable roll-extension rule at opening;
no infinite roll that makes eventual success probability artificially approach
100%. If an authorized extension changes T_hard, treat it as an explicit revised
objective, show before/after results and do not rewrite the original benchmark.

6.5 FEASIBILITY FIRST

Check every candidate action for:
  contract validity, quote freshness, session eligibility, quantity/increment,
  realistic execution-side prices, commissions/slippage assumptions, buying
  power, cumulative max loss, product exercise/assignment risk, deadline and
  allowed roll rules. Reject violations BEFORE ranking probabilities.

===============================================================================
07. HEAVY OFFLINE BUILD: THE ONLY PLACE FOR EXPENSIVE COMPUTATION
===============================================================================

The offline build is deliberately allowed to take substantial compute and to
perform high-precision iterative work. It is run once for a specification/domain
and repeated ONLY when explicitly rebuilding a new validated pack. Use user's
Windows PC CPU/RAM fully when safe: i7-12700F (12 cores / 20 threads), 32 GiB,
NVMe SSD, optionally RTX 3080 if a measured accelerator implementation helps.
Do not require GPU for production inference. Pin BLAS thread counts to prevent
nested thread oversubscription. Partition tasks independently and checkpoint.

OFFLINE BUILD ORDER:

B0. FREEZE SPECIFICATION
  - Version product adapters, dynamics, calendars, trading cost model, allowed
    actions, model features, domain, observation rules and random seeds.
  - Create reproducible build manifest. No live broker snapshot is frozen as a
    permanent market assumption. Live chain can be used only for examples/tests,
    not as a necessary absolute-price anchor.

B1. GENERATE PARAMETER-SPACE DESIGN
  - Stratified/adaptive samples over remaining time (dense near expiry),
    moneyness/strike distances, ATM IV, skew, IV term, r/carry, spot/vol factors,
    payoff morphologies, original target gap, BP/loss remaining and deadlines.
  - Include ATM, near-short-strike, deep tails, near-target, near-loss-cap,
    near-expiration, closed-market gaps, spreads and asymmetric condors.
  - Build data-driven COVERAGE METADATA from samples, but do not claim universal
    bounds. Avoid a uniform full Cartesian grid of all variables; it explodes.
  - Reserve held-out states and entirely held-out POSITION SHAPES/STRIKE SETS.

B2. PRICE BASIS / OPTION SURFACE LIBRARY
  - European: implement verified vectorized analytical Black-Scholes/forward
    marks (cheap enough for online use) and normalized basis tables.
  - American SPY: implement verified exercise-aware PDE/finite difference or
    accurately fitted solver surrogate over normalized (moneyness, time, IV,
    r, q, product conventions) inputs; preserve strict pricing-error gates.
  - Allow strike/expiry interpolation ONLY inside verified support; log errors
    and arbitrage-related monotonicity/price-bound checks.
  - Price and Greeks at time/spot/IV scenario nodes; cache hash-addressed,
    immutable numerical tables. Do not solve an American PDE per simulated path.

B3. TRANSITION OPERATOR LIBRARY
  - Derive/exactly integrate 1D GBM log-return transitions conditional on path
    volatility and time interval; use sparse/banded transport or FFT/convolution
    where accurate and appropriate. Do not treat spot endpoints as independent
    across repeated time steps.
  - For dynamic IV factors under explicit assumed law, build/factor conditional
    transition operators for joint market factors and their correlations.
  - Encode market closures/session boundaries and actual terminal fixing.
  - Verify nonnegative transition probabilities and mass conservation to
    allocated tolerance; no artificial clipping of extreme path mass.

B4. HIGH-FIDELITY HOLD-P50 ORACLE
  - For many synthetic supported portfolios at each sampled parameter state:
    assemble theoretical portfolio marks from the basis; identify the FIRST-HIT
    event through all eligible observations, including disjoint/tangent regions;
    calculate hold P50 using a validated high-resolution method.
  - Use deterministic forward survival transport / backward hitting PDE where
    appropriate, independently check with the complete P50-R nested-grid
    Monte Carlo and analytical fixtures. The P50-R/P50-M sections specify the
    boundary/domain checks, convergence tests, label construction and training.
  - The high-cost, precise region topology checks are OFFLINE ORACLE work only.
  - Save labeled examples, first-hit curves, deadline probabilities, terminal
    settlement reference, numerical uncertainty and representation fingerprints.
  - Do not train on unvalidated oracle results or misuse endpoint-only POP.

B5. TRAIN FAST HOLD-P50 OPERATOR
  - Fit ONE amortized probability operator mapping normalized market/campaign
    input + sampled portfolio value/payoff shape + deadline/horizon to P50.
  - Use the compact bounded discrete-survival network selected in P50-M10,
    with the exact feature/architecture/training/export rules in P50-M09 through
    P50-M11 and P50-M20. This is one selected implementation, not a deferred
    choice among unspecified neural or regression methods.
  - Enforce P50 in [0,1], nondecreasing cumulative deadline probability for
    the SAME event and consistent terminal convention. Also train/test full
    cumulative hit curve where supported, not a disjoint model per deadline.
  - Handle hard expiry/session discontinuities through explicit event flags,
    not naive smooth interpolation over trading/not-trading boundaries.
  - Save calibration/error estimates by regime and uncertainty diagnostics.

B6. GENERATE OFFLINE FEASIBLE CONTROL DATA
  - State includes market factors, payoff/portfolio shape, remaining budget,
    original target gap, costs and finite time-to-hard-deadline.
  - Generate feasible action families: hold, reduce quantity, partial close,
    close, rebalance directional/volatility exposure, add defined-risk hedge,
    roll strike(s), roll expiration within permitted horizon, stop campaign.
  - Derive the resulting full portfolio and ledger for EACH action in the
    oracle. Never represent an actual action by Greeks alone.
  - Use a finite but rich action generator and structural filters. Include
    transaction costs and avoid free/untraded gamma/vega targets.
  - Generate additional actions around near-optimal boundaries and failure
    scenarios rather than exhaustive enumeration of every strike combination.

B7. OFFLINE DYNAMIC OPTIMIZATION
  - Define success as a genuine campaign exit at or above H0 net/gross basis as
    declared, by T_hard, with BP/loss constraints respected.
  - Terminal state: success value 1, failure/deadline 0, with separate terminal
    payoff convention where applicable. During closed sessions no new trade is
    executable; market state can still evolve.
  - For each feasible action evaluate expected CONTINUATION VALUE over the
    shared transition model. Recursively optimize backward from T_hard with
    sufficiently fine time/state discretization and function approximation.
  - Train ONE state-action value estimator Q(state,action) and corresponding
    optimal/near-optimal value V(state); derive envelopes as high-value feasible
    action sets, not as unconstrained free Greek coordinates.
  - Preserve policy identity and actual action restrictions for forward rollout.
  - Penalize or constrain execution costs, structural downside, uncertain BP,
    stale quotes and high-risk regimes; no probability gain obtained by violating
    the hard constraints. If probabilities are tied within uncertainty, prefer
    lower cost / lower risk / simpler exposures rather than false precision.
  - Independently simulate POLICY ROLLOUTS on held-out market paths, including
    realistic redecision times, exits, adverse states and potentially missed
    fills; compare predicted value with observed success frequency under the
    SAME declared assumptions.

B8. ERROR CERTIFICATION, STRESS AND COVERAGE
  - Evaluate hold and policy estimators against independent precise reference
    cases across the whole declared parameter domain, particularly tails,
    narrow success regions, high gamma, skew changes and near constraints.
  - Measure numerical sampling error, price-grid error, surrogate approximation
    error, monitoring-grid error, quote/cost uncertainty and model-form stress
    SEPARATELY. Don't combine them into one fictitious confidence interval.
  - Establish out-of-distribution detector, abstention thresholds and known
    unsupported product/position states. Reject unvalidated domain cells.
  - Verify each candidate envelope contains at least one ACTUALLY IMPLEMENTABLE
    action according to tested product, strike/size, loss and BP conventions.
  - Use no temporal or state look-ahead when replaying synthetic scenarios.

B9. PACKAGE / RELEASE
  - Save immutable model pack, manifest, reproducible seeds, preprocessing
    schema, feature order, numerical precision, validation report, coverage
    limits, artifact hashes and dependency versions.
  - Reload pack in a new process; run official acceptance tests against its
    ACTUAL loaded bytes. Promote only if every release-critical test passes.
  - Archived packs may remain for reproducibility, but normal use has ONE
    selected validated production model, not competing user-facing versions.

===============================================================================
08. NORMAL QUERY PATH: NO EXPENSIVE REBUILD
===============================================================================

QUERY MODES:
  p50      - Evaluate a specified supported unchanged position at the current
             as-of snapshot for one/multiple deadlines.
  envelope - Compute campaign state, attainable modeled probability and target
             risk exposure ranges across near-optimal feasible next states.
  verify   - Fast-evaluate a user-specified exact adjustment/roll candidate.

EVERY QUERY:

Q0. Load/verify production model pack once into a process read-only. Manifest
    hash and product/session contract must match. No implicit rebuild.
Q1. Obtain a coherent as-of snapshot from the normalized HistoricalSnapshotProvider
    defined in SRC-D/P50-R and APP Sections 05-08. A saved snapshot preserves its
    original timestamp and coverage. An optional read-only live provider can use
    the same contract; it is not a dependency of historical queries.
Q2. Parse exact legs/expiries/quantities, original campaign and fee ledger.
    Calculate structural maximum profit/loss and dollar target exactly.
Q3. Normalize current underlying/forward, IV surface, time, strikes, payoff
    response and risk budgets into pack feature coordinates.
Q4. Check supported domain, quote quality, valid market assumptions and exact
    structural risk/BP. On failure, return a reason; no synthetic values.
Q5. Evaluate hold-P50 amortized operator. For one position, return cumulative
    hit curve and requested deadline(s); terminal settlement reference separate.
Q6. For envelope, evaluate constrained state-action values over a bounded,
    vectorized set of currently realizable adjustment families; compute the
    achievable probability ceiling and near-optimal feasible risk profiles.
Q7. For verify, convert exact proposed transactions to post-action portfolio,
    ledger, and BP; evaluate their continuation success value using the SAME
    model and compare against hold/current optimal feasible value.
Q8. Apply uncertainty and feasibility gates; serialize auditable JSON and clear
    terminal explanation. Display no guarantee and no trade execution command.

QUERY SHALL NOT CALL:
  raw Monte Carlo paths; high-resolution pricing-region root finding;
  exhaustive pathwise repricing; offline model training; backward global policy
  fitting; benchmark multi-grid convergence; whole-market brute-force search.

Bounded, cached deterministic inference and tested direct option mark/Greek
formulas are permitted. If a query needs prohibited heavy work for correctness,
return MODEL_COVERAGE_UNAVAILABLE and log the state for an OPTIONAL future
explicit offline rebuild. Do not quietly change the operating contract.

===============================================================================
09. THE PROBABILITY-PRESERVING RISK ENVELOPE
===============================================================================

9.1 CONCEPT

The envelope is the SET of next-position profiles that are simultaneously:
  - reachable by an eligible and affordable real trade/hold action;
  - consistent with position/campaign execution and loss constraints;
  - sufficiently close to the best conditional probability of eventual
    success, OR preserve the initial benchmark when feasible;
  - covered by the validated model with adequate decision confidence.

It is not the intersection of independently chosen Delta, Gamma and Vega
limits. Such a box can admit impossible or dangerously different portfolios.
Represent the JOINT set (possibly nonconvex/disconnected), preserving correlations
between Delta, Gamma, Vega, payoff tail, remaining DTE and cost.

9.2 CORE DECISION RULE (FOR IMPLEMENTER; USER UI NEEDS NO EQUATIONS)

Let s denote the exact state; A_feasible(s) the finite, dynamically generated
set of executable supported actions; Q(s,a) the model's conditional future
success probability after action a (including costs and next decisions).

    V_star(s) = max_{a in A_feasible(s)} Q(s,a)

    P_required = min(p0_policy, V_star(s))

    Envelope(s) = {
      post_action_profile(a):
        a in A_feasible(s),
        Q(s,a) >= P_required - epsilon_decision,
        all cumulative risk and BP constraints hold,
        model coverage and error gates pass
    }

Choose epsilon_decision based on validated probability model error (NOT an
arbitrary percentage). The formulation represents preserving p0_policy when
possible, otherwise showing the highest attainable probability. If multiple
actions have Q >= p0_policy, the envelope can include a range of quality/risk
trade-offs; rank additionally on lower loss risk and net expected profit.

A user may prefer an action outside the envelope; report its measured projected
cost to success probability, required capital, and constraints, not a forced
instruction. Do not silently redefine P_required to make an unsafe action pass.

9.3 OUTPUT CONTENT

For the CURRENT position:
  * Current hold-P50 to original deadline / hard deadline.
  * Current optimal attainable modeled campaign-success probability.
  * Frozen initial p0_hold and p0_policy benchmarks, with comparable horizons.
  * Probability shortfall/surplus; feasibility assessment.
  * Current exact and scenario-sensitive Greeks, key payoff/tail scenarios,
    realized/unrealized net P&L, BP used/remaining and loss budget remaining.

For the RECOMMENDED ENVELOPE:
  * JOINT acceptable exposure profiles/ranges (Delta, Gamma, Vega, Theta,
    downside/upside payoff sensitivities, contract/expiration constraints).
  * Relationship among exposures (e.g. increasing gamma requires lower delta
    or smaller quantity) rather than misleading independent target intervals.
  * Estimated success-probability range with uncertainty by region/profile.
  * Which market changes cause the current position to leave that envelope.
  * Indication whether HOLD is already acceptable.
  * At least one feasible representative construction used to establish that
    each reported envelope branch is actually reachable. Show examples as
    optional illustrations, NOT the one obligatory order.
  * Explicit 'NO FEASIBLE ACTION CAN RESTORE INITIAL P50' when appropriate.

For USER PROPOSED ADJUSTMENT:
  * Exact proposed legs and assumed fills/costs, changes in Greeks/payoff shape.
  * Expected hold-P50 after action (where its definition is applicable).
  * Managed continuation-success probability, vs current hold and best feasible.
  * Cumulative max-loss/BP checks including already realized losses.
  * Domain/precision/execution uncertainty and pass/fail/ambiguous disposition.

9.4 ROBUSTNESS VS A SINGLE HIGH P50 NUMBER

If two profiles differ by less than approximation or execution uncertainty, do
not declare an arbitrary winner. Prefer the lower-cost/lower-tail-risk profile
or show both as nearly equivalent. Expose substantial sensitivity to IV/skew,
price gaps, early assignment and liquidity, not only raw Delta.

9.5 GUIDANCE DOES NOT CONFER IMPLEMENTATION EQUIVALENCE

If the user adjusts Delta to the reported target by a different combination of
options, the model must RE-CHECK that concrete portfolio. Matching selected
Greeks alone is not proof it belongs to the envelope.

===============================================================================
10. POSITION/STRATEGY COVERAGE AND ACTION RESTRICTIONS
===============================================================================

Primary trading objective is SPY defined-risk short-dated options, especially
4-DTE positions. Define the validated production domain explicitly in the
build manifest; do not imply support for unlimited DTE, naked options or all
multi-expiry instruments from the mere presence of a parameter field.

Requirements for any supported candidate:
  - Listed exact contracts, validated expiry/fixing, contract multiplier,
    option style, deliverable, quote timing and eligible trading session.
  - Defined worst-case payoff under the supported product model; no naked short
    or unmodeled early assignment stock exposure accepted as a bounded-loss
    campaign without a verified corresponding risk treatment.
  - Compatible with the allowed adjustment grammar and finite campaign horizon.
  - Correct unit-size lots and financing/broker BP constraints.

Minimum action semantics that MUST exist in the SAME architecture:
  HOLD, FULL CLOSE, PARTIAL CLOSE, REDUCE RISK, ADD DEFINED-RISK HEDGE,
  REPOSITION STRIKES, ROLL EXPIRATION, and EXIT/STOP CAMPAIGN.
Do not create free, continuously fractional option quantities as executable
candidates. Abstract exposure-control targets are guidance only; exact actions
must be integer contract combinations on listed chains.

Cross-expiration exposure from overlapping rolls/calendars, early assignment
stock legs or complex deliverables require their own complete product/cost/
valuation/margin semantics before being included in the supported domain. A
close-then-open roll may be supported without holding overlapping expirations.
If a desired portfolio is unsupported, return a precise unsupported reason,
not a guessed probability.

===============================================================================
11. TASTYTRADE DATA, PRIVACY, AND TIME SEMANTICS
===============================================================================

Use read-only broker APIs for quotes, option chains, IV/Greeks and (if explicitly
authorized) position/trade cashflows. No placing/modifying/canceling orders.
No unnecessary account owner names, account identifiers or other PII exposed to
the model/report. Any internal account token/identifier required by the API is
used solely in the adapter, redacted from logs and result records.

Use the data/transport contracts and operational safeguards included here:
  - The desktop replay adapter consumes the normalized local historical schema
    in this document. The mathematics depends on that schema, not on obtaining
    a missing live broker implementation. Any optional read-only adapter must
    validate its mapped fields against the in-document schema and fail on
    unknown units or unavailable timestamps; it may not invent endpoint fields.
  - Exact contract root/expiration/fixing and style; robust chain pagination.
  - Timestamp-proven as-of snapshot, age/dispersion checks, source audit trail.
  - Actual NY timezone (America/New_York), DST-aware absolute deadlines.
  - Live RTH / eligible extended sessions / previous-session fallback clearly
    distinguished. A prior-session result is AS-OF that earlier time, not live.
  - Verified option trading times; no profit closing hits while options closed.
  - No fabricated missing rate, carry, IV or account fill.
  - Actual rates/carry conventions and ETF dividend approximation exposed.

If the broker chain is too large, fetch only required expirations/strikes and
cache contract metadata/quotes safely per as-of time; avoid broad repeated HTTP
calls. A different live snapshot may change marks and current IV while reusing
the identical offline pack.

===============================================================================
12. DATA CONTRACTS, ARTIFACT SCHEMAS, AND API SURFACE
===============================================================================

Implement Python dataclasses or typed Pydantic-equivalent models with explicit
schema versions. Keep core numerical functions independent of the broker.

Suggested immutable types:
  ContractKey(symbol, root, expiration, right, strike, multiplier, style, fixing)
  PositionLeg(contract_key, signed_quantity, quote_timestamp, bid, ask, iv)
  MarketSnapshot(asof, spot_or_forward, curve, carry, iv_surface, session, quotes)
  LedgerEvent(timestamp, contract, quantity, signed_cash, fees, kind, source)
  Campaign(id, original_M0, H0, p0_hold, p0_policy, T0, T_hard,
           events, opened_positions, target_realized, risk_policy)
  RiskLimits(bp_cap, loss_cap, max_horizon, supported_actions, costs)
  PortfolioShape(normalized_mark_curve, terminal_payoff_curve,
                 IV_shock_response, expiry_signature, exact_risk_features)
  ModelPack(manifest, transitions, price_basis, p50_operator,
            Q_value_operator, uncertainty, domain)
  HoldP50Result(probability_curve, deadline_values, settlement_reference,
                intervals, model_id, quote_basis, warnings)
  EnvelopeResult(current_state, probability_ceiling, target_gap,
                 feasible_profile_regions, representative_actions, confidence)
  CandidateCheck(feasible, reason_codes, post_action_risk, probabilities,
                 shortfall_vs_best, precision)

Avoid ambiguous percent/decimal fields: IV=0.25 means 25% annualized;
probability=0.75 means 75%; prices/strikes are in instrument units; cash is
signed USD. Preserve exact strike and cash inputs using Decimal where needed;
float64 for numerical arrays, with units metadata in outputs.

Minimal public Python API:
  build_model(build_config) -> ModelBuildReport
  load_model(pack_path) -> ValidatedModelPack
  evaluate_hold(snapshot, exact_position, campaign_or_entry, deadlines,
                model_pack) -> HoldP50Result
  optimize_envelope(snapshot, campaign, limits, model_pack) -> EnvelopeResult
  verify_adjustment(snapshot, campaign, proposed_trades, limits,
                    model_pack) -> CandidateCheck
  save_record(record, output_dir) -> atomic JSON path

Build and query must be physically separated: the production query module must
NOT import or invoke expensive offline trainers or high-precision oracle runners.
Use explicit method/endpoint-specific CPU-time and memory budgets.

Example OUTPUT JSON SHAPE (values here are field names, not a simulated result):

  {
    "schema_version": "...",
    "mode": "p50|envelope|verify",
    "model_pack_id": "...",
    "snapshot": {"asof_ny": "...", "kind": "...", "quality": "..."},
    "campaign": {"original_M0_usd": null, "target_H0_usd": null,
                 "initial_p0_hold": null, "initial_p0_policy": null,
                 "T0_ny": "...", "T_hard_ny": "..."},
    "position": {"legs": [], "pnl_realized_usd": null,
                 "pnl_mark_usd": null, "bp_used_usd": null},
    "results": {"hold_p50_by_deadline": null,
                "policy_success_probability": null,
                "optimal_attainable_probability": null,
                "target_preservable": null,
                "joint_risk_envelope": [],
                "candidate_comparisons": []},
    "quality": {"in_domain": null, "approximation_error": null,
                "model_stress": null, "fill_assumptions": "...",
                "warnings": []},
    "execution": {"orders_sent": false}
  }

Null means uncomputed with a reason; NEVER use 0 as an error sentinel. Store
exact normalized legs, hashes, source timestamps, fee basis, probability event
names and model assumptions, but NO credentials/private account identifiers.

NOTE: Paths/names above are output modules to implement from this document,
not existing dependencies. Implement the P50-R reference and preserved terminal
workflow directly; P50-M defines the fast implementation and all regression
comparisons. Every reference calculation is built from the instructions here.

===============================================================================
14. ORCHESTRATION PSEUDOCODE
===============================================================================

OFFLINE:

  def build_model(config):
      spec = freeze_versions_and_assumptions(config)
      grid_cases = adaptive_state_and_portfolio_design(spec)
      pricing_kernels = build_and_validate_price_bases(grid_cases, spec)
      transition_kernels = build_and_validate_transitions(grid_cases, spec)
      hold_labels = high_precision_first_hit_oracle(
          grid_cases, pricing_kernels, transition_kernels, spec)
      p50_operator = fit_and_calibrate_hold_operator(hold_labels, spec)
      action_data = generate_feasible_control_transitions(
          grid_cases, pricing_kernels, transition_kernels, spec)
      value_operator = solve_fit_constrained_control(action_data, spec)
      verification = independent_test_and_policy_rollouts(
          p50_operator, value_operator, grid_cases, spec)
      if not verification.release_pass:
          return FAILED_BUILD_WITH_REPORT
      return save_immutable_pack_and_manifest(...)

QUERY:

  def evaluate(request):
      pack = get_verified_loaded_pack()  # never train here
      snapshot = get_coherent_readonly_asof_snapshot(request)
      state = build_exact_state(snapshot, request.position, request.campaign)
      check_preconditions_and_exact_risk(state)
      features = normalize_and_check_pack_domain(state, pack)
      if request.mode == 'p50':
          return calibrated_hold_operator(pack, features, request.deadlines)
      if request.mode == 'envelope':
          candidates = feasible_action_generator(state, snapshot)
          poststates = exact_cost_margin_payoff_filter(candidates, state)
          q_values = batched_value_operator(pack, poststates)
          return form_reachable_joint_envelope(
              poststates, q_values, state.original_p0_policy, pack.errors)
      if request.mode == 'verify':
          proposal = apply_candidate_to_exact_ledger_and_legs(state, request)
          check_structural_risk_margin_and_execution(proposal)
          return evaluate_proposal_from_same_pack(pack, proposal)

ERROR POLICY:
  If market data missing, portfolio unsupported, model outside validated
  domain, probability uncertainty excessive, or complete risk bound cannot be
  established: return an explicit failure/indeterminate state. Do not fall back
  to slow live Monte Carlo, pretend a zero IV, or choose an arbitrary adjustment.

===============================================================================
15. NUMERICAL ACCURACY, ERROR REPORTING AND DECISION HYGIENE
===============================================================================

Separate uncertainty into:
  A. Monte Carlo LABEL error in offline reference runs (confidence interval).
  B. Time/space/discretization and target-region topology error.
  C. Surrogate hold-P50 error by state/position category.
  D. Continuation-value/policy-fit error and policy-evaluation uncertainty.
  E. Model-form risk: GBM vs actual jumps, assumed IV dynamics, dividend/early
     exercise assumptions; cannot be removed by increasing simulation N.
  F. Market snapshot/bid-ask/fill uncertainty; theoretical touch is not fill.

Report model point estimates with appropriately conservative validated ranges;
not all ranges are statistical confidence intervals. Represent action advantage
as QUALIFIED when approximation intervals do not materially overlap.

REQUIRED INVARIANTS:
  0 <= each probability <= 1;
  cumulative first-hit P50(deadline) nondecreasing with deadline for fixed state,
  target and observation convention;
  target achievement absorbing in a genuine managed-exit event;
  no closing hit during ineligible sessions;
  no endpoint/settlement confusion;
  no campaign target reset upon roll;
  exact max loss and cashflow check before probability ranking;
  no manufactured out-of-domain probabilities;
  no condition claiming a positive realized win from theoretical mid touch;
  probability benchmark is comparable under same objective and deadline;
  risk-envelope contains no action invalid under actual integer-lot constraints.

Decision guard:
  When model uncertainty is large relative to the estimated probability gain,
  state 'statistically/numerically indistinguishable' rather than asserting an
  optimal trade. If no covered feasible action improves the current state,
  report hold/close/no-supported-improvement according to measured values.

===============================================================================
16. TEST MATRIX / ACCEPTANCE PROTOCOL
===============================================================================

T01 - Original target immutability:
  Open $200-max-profit sample -> $100 campaign target. Lose $30, roll twice,
  pay fees: target remains $100 TOTAL net/gross basis as originally declared.
  A trade with a different max profit cannot reset it.

T02 - Historical/realized touch vs actually closed:
  Theoretical mid crosses target and reverses without a fill: HOLD-P50 counts
  a theoretical first touch; managed realized-success metric does not. Accurate
  user ledger and position flags are required.

T03 - First-hit vs endpoint:
  A path reaches target and later finishes below: counted in first-hit only;
  endpoint value computed separately. Compare expiry settlement rule.

T04 - Monotonic time:
  For fixed input state/event, cumulative P50 does not decrease when the
  deadline moves later. Validate around sessions, weekends and expiry.

T05 - Same-Greeks different payoff:
  Construct two near-equal Delta/Vega profiles with different tail losses or
  gamma. The system MUST NOT mark them automatically equivalent/feasible.

T06 - Delta/IV sensitivity:
  Change current spot, skew, path IV, DTE and risk budget within the domain,
  then query the SAME pack_id. Results must change where mathematically
  appropriate; pack must not rebuild.

T07 - Closed-market/timezone:
  Market closures, extended-hour eligibility and DST ambiguity must preserve
  exact NY timing, valuation vs execution distinction, and valid as-of quotes.

T08 - Risk correctness:
  Reject actions exceeding BP $5,000 or verified cumulative-loss $1,000;
  reject unknown/unbounded/assignment-exposed risk when hard bound required.
  Realized losses consume campaign budget before evaluating a replacement.

T09 - Overlap/roll mechanics:
  Closing old spread and opening new legs posts both cashflows plus fees;
  expiration roll alters options DTE but not H0 or T_hard; no implicit free fill.

T10 - Out-of-domain and missing data:
  Extreme untrained skew, exotic deliverable, missing quote, unsupported
  American exposure and uncalibrated IV process produce named diagnostics.
  No extrapolated confident percentage and no automatic heavy rebuild.

T11 - Hold oracle parity:
  Independently sampled supported positions are compared with the P50-R
  reference, deterministic/analytical/PDE fixtures and independent paths,
  using the same assumptions, dates, target and master observation grid.
  P50-M19 supplies the packed-price comparison. Account for sampling,
  monitoring and approximation uncertainty; do not claim tastytrade parity.

T12 - Managed-policy validity:
  Independently roll out the RETURNED policy (not an optimistic clairvoyant
  solver) on hidden synthetic paths; estimate probability of realized H0 and
  constraint violations under same assumed model and execution rules.

T13 - Envelope validity:
  Every displayed envelope branch has at least one feasible concrete example;
  every user-selected proposal is rechecked on full exact position/payoff.
  Near-equivalent probability cases are reported as ties within uncertainty.

T14 - No hidden heavy work in query:
  Instrument/call-count assert offline oracle/trainer/MC/region-root routines
  are never imported/called from query. Time 100 distinct-position queries
  across different snapshot/vol inputs on an installed pack. Save p50,
  envelope and verify latency percentiles and peak RAM; compare against original
  minutes-per-position baseline without inventing claimed speedups.

T15 - Reproducibility and privacy:
  Loading the same pack + exact frozen snapshot + same campaign + same config
  yields identical deterministic inference, saved hashes and outcomes;
  JSON and pack files have no broker secrets or sensitive account identity.

T16 - Domain pressure tests:
  Near 0 DTE, ATM/high gamma, tight butterflies, condor disjoint regions,
  extreme IV/skew and deep-tail payoff bounds; enforce abstention where the
  fit fails. Do not use a few easy spreads as universal accuracy evidence.

T17 - Value-model sanity:
  Verify that the policy's estimated success probability cannot exceed 1,
  does not increase just because already-incurred fees are ignored, and only
  improves over HOLD when a viable action/policy exists under constraints.
  A V_star computed by action maximization should be >= its own feasible HOLD
  action Q, within the numerical/error accounting. 

T18 - Empty/fully realized campaign:
  No open legs plus H0 reached => achieved; no open legs without H0 and no time
  left => failure; risk-cap exhausted => feasible set restricted as defined.

RELEASE GATE:
  All correctness tests pass; numerical threshold report passes inside the
  documented domain; policy rollouts validate outputs; query heavy-routine
  exclusion passes; measurable runtime/memory reported. Otherwise do not
  advertise a production-ready universal engine.

===============================================================================
17. DEVELOPMENT ORDER (ONE VERSION, SUCCESSIVE IMPLEMENTATION MILESTONES)
===============================================================================

M0. SELF-CONTAINED REFERENCE AND BASELINES
  - Implement the complete P50-R reference algorithm and the normalized market,
    clock, cash, portfolio and event contracts provided in this document.
  - Implement the specified analytical/deterministic fixtures and independent
    numerical comparisons; save reproducible reference outputs.
  - Measure this reference's runtime, peak RAM and numerical uncertainty.
  - Reuse only components that pass the specified regression gates; the fast
    implementation must not silently change its probability event.
  DONE WHEN: an independent input/cash/quote/first-hit regression corpus exists.
  No pre-existing source code or outside specification is an M0 prerequisite.

M1. EXACT CAMPAIGN & STATE CONTRACTS
  - Implement immutable original H0/p0/deadline, executed ledger, margin,
    exact payoff and risk classification, post-adjustment state transitions.
  - Build no-trade read-only data adapter, contracts, time/quality validation.
  DONE WHEN: T01/T02/T05/T07/T08/T09/T18 pass.

M2. PARAMETERIZED PRICING & TRANSITIONS
  - Implement model factor coordinates, validated fast price-basis evaluation,
    expiry/exercise behavior, normalization, and transition kernels.
  - Independent analytical and high-fidelity numerical tests.
  DONE WHEN: pricing/transition errors and domain limits are measured.

M3. OFFLINE HOLD-P50 AMORTIZATION
  - Generate high-fidelity oracle labels, train/validate reusable P50 operator,
    test first-hit and deadline invariants, save/reload pack.
  - Enforce fast query ONLY and no retraining for changed spot/IV/time.
  DONE WHEN: in-domain hold-P50 accuracy and fast-query gates pass.

M4. OFFLINE CONTROL VALUE & ENVELOPE
  - Implement feasible action model, cumulative risk/cost budget, backward
    control optimization, policy rollout evaluation, reachable joint envelopes.
  - Train continuation-value model and exact proposal verification.
  DONE WHEN: T12/T13/T17 pass and strategy probability basis is honest.

M5. CLI / RESULTS / OPERATIONS
  - Add interactive p50, envelope and verify paths in one CLI; preserve the
    three-stage terminal workflow fully specified in the P50 section here.
  - No more questions than necessary; clear indicators, archived audit records.
  - Atomic pack loading/saving, checkpoint resumable OFFLINE build, domain
    diagnostics and error reports.
  DONE WHEN: a clean Windows setup can build offline and query repeatedly.

M6. VALIDATE / FREEZE MODEL PACK
  - Complete all T01-T18 tests on independent data and held-out positions.
  - Tune offline numerical effort and pack capacity to meet precision gates.
  - Freeze one production pack version, document model assumptions/limitations,
    compare query cost vs original. No invented performance numbers.
  DONE WHEN: reproducible accepted report accompanies the released pack.

Milestones are tasks inside ONE deliverable, not user-facing model editions.
Do not skip a failing verification stage merely because the CLI prints numbers.

===============================================================================
18. PERFORMANCE ENGINEERING REQUIREMENTS
===============================================================================

OFFLINE:
  - Exploit 12 physical-core/20-thread desktop resources via independent work
    units and measured batch sizing. Control NumPy/BLAS nested threads.
  - Prefer vectorized float64 NumPy/SciPy kernels; use compiled numba/C++/GPU
    only after numerical parity testing and measurement. GPU optional.
  - Cache identical normalized pricing kernels and transition operators;
    content-address with assumptions, grid shape and numerical precision.
  - Store high-dimensional work as chunked data, not all Cartesian states in
    32 GiB memory. Use NVMe-backed streaming/chunks where necessary.
  - Make generation and training restartable by immutable random seeds,
    partition manifests and atomic checkpoints; never silently combine
    incompatible simulation parameters or random streams.
  - Adaptive sampling: allocate extra oracle work where the approximation
    has high error or near feasible/action boundaries, not uniform oversampling
    of easy states.

ONLINE:
  - Preload model pack once per process and memory-map large read-only arrays.
  - Reuse contract/spec metadata; do not reuse stale LIVE quotes as if current.
  - Cache normalized current option-value basis for multiple candidate actions
    on the SAME snapshot; batch the value model across all candidates.
  - Prune infeasible candidates using exact, inexpensive structural/payoff/
    margin tests BEFORE model evaluation. Prefer candidate generation from
    target exposure directions rather than combinatorial strike enumeration.
  - Use a configurable hard cap on candidate count/CPU work; disclose sampled
    search coverage. The resulting V_star is best among SEARCHED feasible
    actions, not the absolute global optimum over all conceivable trades.
  - Never use cached values from old IV/spot/time as live results without
    passing the new market state through the parametric model.
  - Record per-stage wall/CPU time, peak memory, pack load time and quote age.

DO NOT let the desire for a fast runtime change the actual definition of P50,
throw away narrow target regions without tests, clip tail losses, use an
endpoint approximation for a first-hit event, or downgrade an American ETF
option to European pricing silently.

===============================================================================
19. USER EXPERIENCE: A GUIDING INSTRUMENT, NOT AN ORDER GENERATOR
===============================================================================

Desired terminal presentation pattern:

  CAMPAIGN OBJECTIVE
  Original target: 50% of initial max profit = <H0 USD>
  Initial probability benchmark: <p0> [hold/policy, horizon]
  Current deadline / absolute hard cutoff: <NY timestamps>

  CURRENT STATE
  Hold-P50 to target deadline: <p_hold> [model + range]
  Best attainable managed success probability: <p_best> [coverage + range]
  Delta/Gamma/Vega/Theta and payoff-shape risk: <calculated values>
  Realized + mark P&L: <calculated values>
  BP used / available: <calculated values>
  Structural loss exposure / remaining risk: <calculated values>

  GUIDANCE
  Initial probability preserved? YES / NO / UNCERTAIN
  If NO: maximum attainable probability and shortfall to p0
  Acceptable JOINT risk-profile region(s): <linked ranges/conditions>
  Current position within envelope? YES / NO / UNCERTAIN
  Representative feasible implementations: <optional examples>
  Constraints and uncertainty that matter most: <specific warnings>

  OPTIONAL USER PROPOSAL VERIFICATION
  Proposed adjustment: <exact legs + assumed fills>
  Probability and exposure after adjustment: <calculated>
  Meets target envelope and BP/loss limits? <graded result>
  Why/why not: <brief explanation>

No exact percentage or Delta target may appear in a real result unless actually
computed. No suggestion that choosing an envelope point automatically ensures
restoration of the baseline probability. User retains execution choice, and
execution price/slippage can change the answer.

For future development, a compact plot of the nonconvex joint envelope can help,
but it is not a dependency of the terminal-first computational engine.

===============================================================================
20. DECISIONS LOCKED NOW; QUESTIONS TO RESOLVE FROM CODE/EVIDENCE
===============================================================================

LOCKED BY THIS SPECIFICATION:
  - One system with one reusable offline pack and fast query paths.
  - Universal only WITHIN explicitly tested, parameterized model support.
  - Separate hold first-hit P50 from controlled realized success probability.
  - Fixed original-dollar target across campaign adjustments.
  - Strict BP and defined loss constraint enforcement; never waive to restore p0.
  - Reachable joint exposure/payoff envelope rather than one mandated trade.
  - Recheck the user's proposed concrete implementation.
  - No query-time heavy simulations/region solvers/training.
  - Real current market conditions as input, with explicit modeling limits.
  - One version developed in successive milestones; independent validation.

NEED EVIDENCE DURING IMPLEMENTATION (NOT NEW USER-WIZARD QUESTIONS):
  - The P50-R implementation must demonstrate the behavior and fixtures
    specified here; no unavailable historical source code is required.
  - Normalized replay quote timestamps, units, contract metadata, parameter
    availability and ledger provenance must pass the in-document data gates.
  - The embedded fee/execution/session profiles and P50-M05/M06 operational
    assumptions must be implemented and surfaced under their exact identities.
  - The selected future law is P50-M04's frozen-known-surface GBM; dynamic-IV
    calibration is not an unresolved prerequisite of this initial model.
  - P50-M03/M07/M19/M20 define initial domains, actions, price bases and design;
    P50-M11 through M13 define the required measured accuracy/runtime gates.
  - The P50-M06 matched-delivery/expense certificate and application lifecycle
    rules determine when a declared-model $1,000 bound is valid or UNKNOWN.
  - The campaign input T_hard is finite and explicit; the fixed action/roll
    budgets in P50-M03 cannot silently extend it.

When an unspecified item can alter result correctness, use a conservative,
explicitly labeled engineering setting or return NEEDS_MODEL_INPUT / UNSUPPORTED;
do not invent a verified convention. Do not allow unsupported areas to contaminate
validated claims about covered states.

===============================================================================
21. FINAL DEFINITION OF DONE
===============================================================================

The system is finished only when an independent engineer can:

  1. Install it on the specified Windows desktop.
  2. Build ONE reproducible, extensively validated model pack using substantial
     offline compute; inspect the complete build/validation report.
  3. Restart the application and load that same pack with no recomputation.
  4. Enter a new SPY position, different strikes/expiry, live spot/IV and a NY
     deadline; obtain hold-P50 quickly using the stored pack.
  5. Enter an ongoing campaign with initial $ target, recorded transactions,
     realized losses, limits and exact open legs; obtain its CONDITIONAL
     current probability and an attainable joint risk-profile envelope.
  6. Advance to a different NY trading time or change the market state, and
     recalculate with the SAME pack_id and NO heavy Monte Carlo/region work.
  7. Compare several alternative user-selected, exact adjustments in seconds;
     each alternative is checked for cumulative BP/loss and payoff risk.
  8. See a clear explanation when maintaining the initial success probability
     is impossible under the current state and constraints.
  9. Confirm out-of-domain conditions are rejected rather than fabricated.
 10. Inspect independent validation error, model assumptions, sources,
     benchmarks, deterministic outputs, run-time metrics, and privacy safety.
 11. Confirm not a single order has been submitted or modified.

If an entire strategy optimizer cannot achieve these standards for a chosen
domain, report its limits. Never conflate a highly optimized software engine
with certainty about future options markets.

END OF IMPLEMENTATION PLAN

END SRC-E

===============================================================================
BEGIN SRC-D — COMPLETE DEADLINE P50 CONTRACT
===============================================================================

P50 BY A NEW YORK DEADLINE
FINAL IMPLEMENTATION PLAN - APPROVED-FALLBACK PROFILE
Revision 3 | Prepared 2026-10-12

STATUS
This is the completed software and mathematical specification, not an implemented
or tested trading calculator. The user has approved the proposed solutions to
U1-U19 from Revision 2. There are no remaining user decisions in this plan.
Vendor details that remain unverified have an explicit implementation decision;
accepting a fallback does not turn it into a verified tastytrade convention.

DELIVERABLE
One reference CLI, python -m qqq_sim p50-reference, with desktop and conditional Termux support.
The implementation includes the tests and reproducible records fully specified
in P50-R16 through P50-R23. This TXT is a planning deliverable and does not claim
that an older script was inspected, rebuilt or proven equivalent.

CONTENTS

===============================================================================
01. REQUIREMENTS, PRECEDENCE AND REPLICATION STATUS
===============================================================================

1.1 Priority

The latest user instructions take precedence over earlier proposed defaults:
- Keep the existing symbol selection and the same three trade-input stages.
- Put any extra user-supplied inputs in the reference CLI configuration as top-level globals.
- Fetch automatically rather than adding unnecessary settings or prompts.
- Match established tastytrade P50 conventions wherever they are known.
- Use the now-approved substitutes wherever the vendor detail is not established.
- Work while options are closed using the latest usable previous-session snapshot.
- Use live data for supported options that are actively trading outside RTH.
- Use tastytrade market data only; no historical calibration or backtesting.
- Produce first-hit probabilities, not just a deadline endpoint probability.

The original project specification is retained except where this revision
explicitly supersedes it. The QQQ historical OHLCV/raw_theta contracts continue to
govern those datasets, but this application does not open or modify them.

1.2 Objective

Calculate the theoretical probability that a specified, unchanged option position
reaches at least 50% of its maximum expiration profit at an eligible observation
time on or before a user-specified New York deadline. Also calculate an
expiration-horizon reference result and separate endpoint diagnostics.

The official P50 material establishes an at-any-time profit event and calls the
metric theoretical. It does not supply a full production-engine specification.
[SRC-D, Section 30] The numerical engine below is the user's approved implementation decision,
not a newly discovered specification of tastytrade's internal code.

1.3 Exactness and naming

Use three separate concepts:
A. Input agreement: same actual contracts, quotes, entry amount and valuation time.
B. Model agreement: same dynamics, volatility, repricing and observation rules.
C. Numerical agreement: same estimator, precision, sampling and display rules.

Current profile name:
    TT_ALIGNED_APPROVED_FALLBACK_V1

Required status in every result:
    Vendor equivalence: NOT VERIFIED
    Method: tastytrade-aligned inputs with approved modeling fallbacks

Do not use 'exact tastytrade P50', 'identical engine', 'physical probability', or
'guaranteed probability' as the name of the output. A subsequent vendor-confirmed
profile may replace specific conventions, but only in a versioned update. Never
change a running calculation automatically because a platform number differs.

1.4 Important mathematical qualification

The approved fallback uses one volatility for the underlying path distribution
and potentially different frozen IVs for individual option marks. It defines a
clear probability of a clear mark-based event. In general, those marks are not
all arbitrage-free prices under the same one-factor risk-neutral process.

Use Q_path for the specified path measure. Report:
    'GBM path probability with frozen-leg-IV repricing'
not:
    'a uniquely calibrated full-market risk-neutral P50'.

A common-volatility coherent model is an independent validation case, not an
unannounced replacement for the approved default. No historical calibration,
stochastic volatility parameters, estimated physical drift, jumps, or fitted
historical skew dynamics are introduced.

===============================================================================
02. SUPPORTED PRODUCTS AND STRATEGIES
===============================================================================

2.1 First-release support
