Support standard same-expiration vanilla options, with compatible deliverables,
exercise and fixing conventions, on:
- SPY and QQQ: American-style ETF adapters.
- SPX PM-settled series: European-index adapter; exact returned root and symbols.

The architecture permits XSP, RUT/RUTW and other equity/index products after their
product/session adapter and tests pass. Broker availability alone is not proof
that this calculator supports the product's pricing model. VIX and futures
options require separate underlying/fixing adapters and are not passed through a
stock GBM pricer. SPX has European exercise and cash settlement. [SRC-D, Section 30]

No blanket rule says that every non-SPX option is RTH-only. Session eligibility
must be resolved per product and broker, with effective dates. [SRC-D, Section 30]

2.2 Strategy scope

Support credit/debit verticals, iron condors, butterflies, iron butterflies and
other same-expiration combinations through signed-leg payoff accounting. Do not
select the mathematical procedure by a guessed strategy name.

Require finite, strictly positive maximum expiration profit for a percentage-of-
maximum-profit calculation. A long straddle/strangle with unlimited maximum
profit therefore cannot have this target definition, even though its options are
otherwise standard. Report the reason precisely.

Preserve the project's defined-risk restriction as an internal default. Compute
maximum loss correctly before rejecting an unbounded-risk strategy. Distinguish
'unlimited maximum profit' from 'unlimited loss'.

Unsupported: calendars, diagonals, mixed economic fixing times, adjusted or
nonstandard deliverables, FLEX, binary products, unsupported AM settlement, and
products needing futures/volatility-index modeling. Do not substitute neighboring
expirations, nearby strikes or a different settlement type.

2.3 Root choice without another prompt

For the normal SPX input, the shipped product preference is the PM series, normally
SPXW, and this preference is displayed before leg entry. It is an explicit
calculator selection convention, not a claim about tastytrade's default.

Resolve the actual returned root from the broker chain. If the preference is not
available or does not uniquely identify compatible contracts, reject that
selection with a diagnostic pointing to PRODUCT_OVERRIDES. Do not add a new
root-selection question. An explicitly requested unsupported AM root receives an
unsupported-product error, not a silent conversion to PM.

===============================================================================
03. UNCHANGED INTERACTIVE WORKFLOW
===============================================================================

3.1 Normal launch

    python -m qqq_sim p50-reference

If DEFAULT_SYMBOL is empty, ask for the symbol as existing startup context. If it
is filled, use it and print it without asking. This does not add a trade stage.

The trade stages remain exactly:

    Stage 1 - Expiration [YYYY-MM-DD]:
    > 2026-10-30

    Stage 2 - Enter p/c strike s/l; type done to continue:
    > p 767 s
    > p 765 l
    > done

    Stage 3 - Deadline, New York [YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM]:
    > 2026-10-23 15:30

    [automatic snapshot selection, validation and calculation]
    [results]

    Stage 2 - Enter another position for the same symbol and expiration:
    >

These dates/strikes illustrate syntax only, not listed-contract availability or
current market prices.

3.2 Commands and navigation

- e: move back one input stage. At the earliest available stage it does nothing.
- done: finish the current leg list.
- undo: remove the last entered leg before netting.
- q or quit: exit cleanly.
- EOF: exit cleanly.
- Ctrl-C during calculation: checkpoint completed work if possible and return to
  the input loop; Ctrl-C at a prompt exits cleanly.

Stage 3 -> Stage 2 preserves legs for editing.
Stage 2 -> Stage 1 invalidates resolved contracts if expiration changes.
Stage 1 -> symbol selection when the symbol is interactive.
After results -> Stage 2, preserving symbol and expiration and clearing the old
leg list. No 'continue?', confirmation or fourth input stage is added.

3.3 Parsing

p/c is case-insensitive. Strike is a positive Decimal matching a listed strike.
s means short and l means long. One line is one contract; repeated lines increase
quantity. Opposite entries for the same exact option net out. Preserve raw entry
history so undo remains intuitive. An empty net position is invalid.

The deadline is an absolute New York timestamp, not a DTE approximation. Use
America/New_York with ZoneInfo. Reject ambiguous/nonexistent local times with an
explanation and remain at Stage 3; do not choose a DST fold silently.

Optional extra target percentages and legacy day-offset reporting are globals,
not new prompts. The primary target is always 50% and the primary deadline always
comes from Stage 3.

3.4 No hidden questions

Missing rates, permissions, contract roots, snapshot data or credentials produce
actionable errors. Where a genuine manual override is needed, name the existing
top-of-file global and required normalized units. Do not ask for it interactively
halfway through a trade.

===============================================================================
04. MINIMAL TOP-OF-SCRIPT GLOBALS
===============================================================================

4.1 User-facing block

Place the configuration block immediately after imports, before functions and
classes. The following is configuration design, not a complete executable script:

    TT_CLIENT_SECRET = ""
    TT_REFRESH_TOKEN = ""
    DEFAULT_SYMBOL = ""                 # blank preserves the symbol prompt

    TRADE_OVERRIDES = {}                 # optional, exact-position keyed
    MARKET_INPUT_OVERRIDES = {}          # optional, dated and symbol scoped
    PRODUCT_OVERRIDES = {}               # optional, explicit root/session choice
    EXTRA_TARGET_PCTS = ()               # e.g. (25.0, 75.0); 50% always calculated
    EXTRA_DAY_OFFSETS = ()               # e.g. (1, 2, 3); no extra prompt

Authentication globals apply only to an enabled optional live provider;
historical/replay reference calculations require neither. DEFAULT_SYMBOL is
convenience only. All override collections default empty. The normalized
HistoricalSnapshotProvider resolves runtime inputs before a missing-input error.

An implementation may allow environment-variable values to populate these same
globals, but must not require a separate configuration file or environment setup
in addition to the global block. Never include credentials in logs, snapshots,
exceptions, example result files or the plan's test fixtures.

4.2 Exact-position trade overrides

Do not use a single reusable ENTRY_CREDIT global that accidentally applies to the
next position. TRADE_OVERRIDES is keyed by a canonical position identifier built
from underlying, expiration, actual root, sorted normalized legs and quantities.
Print this identifier in results/errors so it can be copied without guessing.

An override entry may contain:
    entry_cash_dollars: signed net cash, positive credit / negative debit
    entry_context: "hypothetical" or "existing"
    round_trip_cost_dollars: optional, total fixed cost for the whole position

If no entry override matches, the position is a hypothetical new position at the
snapshot's net leg midpoint. Unmatched overrides are ignored with a short
notice; they never carry over by approximate strike matching.

An existing-position override supplies original net entry cash. Its result is a
forward estimate from the current chosen snapshot. The application does not know
whether the original trade hit the target before that snapshot.

4.3 Market overrides

MARKET_INPUT_OVERRIDES entries specify the symbol, applicable expiration where
needed, effective-from/to timestamps, provenance text, and only missing or
intentionally overridden values. Supported values are normalized annual
continuously compounded r, continuous yield q, and, only if necessary, explicitly
identified path/contract IVs. IV and rates use decimals, not percentages.

A deliberate override takes precedence and is labeled USER_OVERRIDE. An override
must be valid at the snapshot time, not merely at the time the program starts.
No literal current market rate, dividend yield or IV is hardcoded as a default.
Missing data is never represented by an implicit zero.

4.4 Engineering defaults are not additional required user inputs

Keep one clearly separated top-level ENGINE_DEFAULTS structure with documented
shipped values. The user should not need to edit it for an ordinary calculation.
It contains model decisions, numerical controls and validation tolerances, not a
large menu of unanswered questions.

Shipped defaults:
    profile                       TT_ALIGNED_APPROVED_FALLBACK_V1
    target_fraction               0.50
    primary_profit_basis          gross, no premium interest accrual
    initial_monitor_step_seconds  60
    minimum_pilot_step_seconds    1.875
    production_paths              200000
    batch_size                    4096
    random_seed                   20261008
    pilot_paths                   20000, independent of production
    grid_tolerance_probability    0.0025   (0.25 percentage points)
    monitoring                    verified eligible option sessions
    terminal_settlement_reference included, separately labeled
    default_dividend_profile       continuous yield
    production_bridge_correction  disabled
    default_defined_risk_only      true
    snapshot_collection_seconds   15
    live_spot_max_age_seconds      2
    live_option_max_age_seconds    10
    live_quote_dispersion_seconds  5
    live_iv_max_age_seconds        60
    prior_quote_max_age_seconds    300, measured at snapshot t0
    prior_quote_dispersion_seconds 60
    prior_iv_max_age_seconds       300, measured at snapshot t0
    basis_tolerance_fraction       0.10 of the positive dollar target
    cache_session_retention        2 completed sessions, plus active state
    working_memory_budget         256 MiB for the numerical engine

These are approved implementation choices/quality thresholds, not measured
tastytrade production values. No hardcoded freshness rule can prove that a stale
quote is economically wrong; these rules choose which data the calculator will
accept. Full-session cache files have explicit coverage, not assumed completeness.

Automatically choose writable application-private cache/results directories;
never make Android Downloads access a startup prerequisite. Authentication and
cache permissions are not another trade-entry stage.

===============================================================================
05. SNAPSHOT PROVIDER CONTRACT AND OPTIONAL TRANSPORT SAFETY
===============================================================================

5.1 Required provider and numerical boundary

The required application provider is HistoricalSnapshotProvider. It reads the
canonical local QQQ contracts and returns the immutable normalized snapshot
specified in P50-R02, with availability semantics from the application data
section. The numerical core consumes these values and performs no network calls.
No broker SDK, live OAuth flow, earlier script, or online manual is required to
implement or run the historical simulator or its offline P50 build.

Required provider methods are:
  resolve_contracts(symbol, expiry, raw_legs, asof) -> exact ContractSpec list;
  session_intervals(contracts, start, end) -> ordered half-open intervals;
  snapshot(contracts, asof, quote_profile) -> MarketSnapshot;
  parameter_records(symbol, expiry, asof) -> normalized dated records;
  coverage(query) -> actual source/contract/time coverage and omissions.
Each call is read-only. The provider returns typed errors for unavailable data.
It must not submit real orders or use a brokerage account as a test facility.

5.2 Normalized transport/data schema

Every quote record contains contract_id, bid, ask, optional sizes, bid_event_time,
ask_event_time, known_at, received_at, source_precision, and provenance. Preserve
raw midpoint/mark/last/close separately; computed midpoint is (bid+ask)/2. A quote
without demonstrable event/availability semantics cannot satisfy strict as-of
reconstruction. An aggregate update timestamp is allowed only when the adapter's
schema declares that it updates both relevant quote sides, not merely a summary.
The effective quote age is the worst applicable side age at t0. Minute-precision
records retain their conservative uncertainty interval under the data contract;
no fictitious second/microsecond timestamp is created.

Contract records explicitly contain root, strike, right, multiplier, deliverable,
exercise style, physical/cash delivery convention, expiry date, economic fixing,
and exclusive stop-trading cutoff. AM/PM is a fixing convention, not a substitute
for the cash/physical field. Normalized rate/IV/dividend fields follow P50-R06;
unknown raw units are rejected instead of guessed from magnitude.

5.3 Parsing and bounded transport behavior

If an optional authorized read-only live provider is later implemented, it must
produce exactly the same normalized contract and pass the same tests. Its raw
wire schema is a separate adapter version; it cannot change the P50 mathematics
or create a missing historical snapshot. No live provider is a release dependency
of this local historical application. The complete mandatory parsing rules are:

- Reconcile requested and returned exact instrument IDs and reject omissions or
  duplicate conflicting records. Never silently choose an adjacent strike/root.
- Treat each response schema separately. An absent, null, or empty pagination
  field is valid for a declared nonpaginated schema.
- For a declared paginated schema, allow nonnegative integer or digit-string
  page counts only within that schema's declared bounds. Never convert None,
  an object, an empty string, or the string "null" to an integer.
- A declared multiple-page response must be fully reconciled; malformed positive
  page metadata is a structured error, not silent truncation.
- Partition quote requests at the adapter's declared maximum batch size; the
  retained compatibility ceiling is100 instruments per request.
- Read-only transient transport failures use at most4 attempts with delays
  0.5,1,2 seconds before successive retries; respect a server Retry-After up to
  30 seconds and return a rate-limit error when it exceeds that retry budget.
  A failure is never interpreted as market closure.
- Invalid JSON, unknown required schema, authentication, entitlement, missing
  instruments and stale/incoherent quotes have distinct typed errors.
- Any secrets used by an optional transport stay outside snapshots, diagnostics,
  result files and tests. Saved fixtures contain normalized nonsecret records.

5.4 Snapshot assembly

Resolve exact contract identity first. Select one t0, then backward-as-of join all
quotes, the authoritative underlying, IVs and dated parameter records, enforcing
known_at<=t0 and the applicable source availability cutoff. Store per-field ages,
quote dispersion, original timestamps and normalization choices. A recent receive
time or newly delivered old event does not rejuvenate a quote. Never synthesize
contemporaneous bid/ask/IV from unrelated last or closing prices. Use P50-R22 for
live/prior/replay mode selection and quality thresholds and P50-R06/R12 for the
complete normalized parameter/IV resolution algorithms.

===============================================================================
06. CONTRACT RESOLUTION AND MARKET SESSIONS
===============================================================================

6.1 Contract metadata

Retain exact underlying, root, OCC symbol, streamer symbol, right, Decimal strike,
integer quantity, multiplier/deliverable, exercise style, settlement convention,
expiration date, stop-trading time and economic fixing time. The equity-option
schema distinguishes exercise-style, settlement-type, expires-at and
stops-trading-at; settlement-type examples are AM/PM, not cash/physical. [SRC-D, Section 30]

Do not equate the active field with 'exchange is open now'. Do not let a stale
cached active flag resurrect a contract that has stopped trading or expired.

6.2 Separate times

    t_run     = actual launch/calculation wall-clock time
    t0        = frozen snapshot valuation time
    t_star    = user deadline
    t_last    = exclusive stop-trading cutoff for the whole entered strategy;
                every eligible closing observation strictly precedes this cutoff
    T         = economic terminal payoff/fixing time under the product adapter

Administrative expiry timestamps, last trading, exercise instruction deadlines
and cash settlement/payment dates are not interchangeable. A product adapter
must establish which field/rule supplies each time. Conflicts produce an error,
not a guess at 16:00.

Initial supported PM adapters must be validated against their applicable product
rules. AM SPX fixing is not approximated as the cash index at 09:30 in this version.

6.3 Option sessions, not underlying sessions

Use the intersection of:
    exchange/product sessions
    broker-supported sessions for those contracts/account entitlements
    contract-specific stop-trading cutoffs
    compatible trading times across all legs

Keep an effective-dated session-rule package and actual calendar dates. Handle
holidays, early closes, breaks and overnight sessions crossing civil midnight.
A trading-date label is not necessarily the New York calendar date of an event.

Current official tastytrade material confirms GTH support for SPX, XSP, VIX and
RUT. It identifies 20:15-09:25 ET GTH and 16:15-17:00 ET Curb, subject to product
and date eligibility. [SRC-D, Section 30] Cboe specifies that expiring SPXW ordinarily stops at
16:00 ET, or 13:00 on an applicable half day. Do not extend an expiring series
into Curb merely because the overall product has a Curb session. [SRC-D, Section 30]

The calendar implementation must not assume that a general Equity market-time
endpoint describes all options sessions. When the API provides an applicable
instrument collection, resolve its calendar; otherwise use the versioned,
validated product rules. Unknown eligibility is not automatically 'closed'.

6.4 Time arithmetic

Display and parse New York wall times, including their offsets. Calculate elapsed
seconds, ordering and ACT/365F fractions in UTC/epoch time, including DST changes.
Never use a fixed EST offset or mix 252-day volatility scaling with unconverted
calendar-year time. The interface remains YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM.

===============================================================================
07. SNAPSHOT MODES AND SELECTION ALGORITHM
===============================================================================

7.1 Mode definitions

LIVE_RTH / LIVE_EXTENDED:
The relevant option session is active, the selected contracts and reference
instruments have acceptable current data, and a coherent valuation anchor exists.
Use a current frozen snapshot. Extended includes supported GTH/Curb/pre/post
sessions; it is not synonymous with the underlying's extended stock session.

PREVIOUS_SESSION:
The relevant options session is closed. Use the latest complete acceptable
snapshot from the immediately preceding eligible session. Preserve its original
t0, input dates and price basis. The result is an as-of-t0 estimate.

UNAVAILABLE:
Session eligibility is unknown, a required active-session quote is unavailable,
or no acceptable prior-session snapshot can be constructed. Return a precise
reason, not an invented probability or a disguised stale live calculation.

7.2 Deterministic selection

After Stage 3:
1. Resolve all contracts, the actual product session and the economic horizon.
2. Validate the deadline against t_run and the available contract horizon.
3. If options are actively trading, collect current quotes and reference data.
4. If those data pass, freeze a live snapshot.
5. If active-session data do not pass after bounded collection/retry, return
   LIVE_DATA_UNAVAILABLE. Do not automatically switch to yesterday's data.
6. If the options session is verifiably closed, identify its predecessor session.
7. Attempt a timestamp-proven retained broker snapshot for that session.
8. Inspect the local cache for a complete snapshot for the same session/contracts.
9. Among valid candidates choose the greatest actual t0; prioritize freshness and
   completeness, not a fixed preference for REST over a newer valid local record.
10. If neither candidate qualifies, return PREVIOUS_SNAPSHOT_UNAVAILABLE.
11. Freeze the chosen state and run all calculations from that immutable state.

An intraday break uses the immediately preceding eligible session segment, which
may have ended on the same civil date. A Sunday-evening SPX session is live if the
selected contracts are active; it does not automatically use Friday because the
calendar day is Sunday.

7.3 Deadlines and expired products

In normal mode require t_star > t_run and t_star <= T, and require a currently
unexpired, supported position with a future supported observation/payoff horizon.
Recheck immediately after snapshot acquisition in case the deadline passed while
inputs were entered. Preserve the requested deadline; never silently move it to
a market opening, adjacent day or expiration.

Saved-record replay may reproduce an old calculation with historical timestamps,
but is an explicitly separate noninteractive development/test mode. The ordinary
three-stage flow does not analyze an already expired trade as a new opportunity.

A deadline during a closed period is valid. It creates no new eligible profit
observations during that closure. A theoretical at-deadline mark can still be
reported separately.

===============================================================================
08. PREVIOUS-SESSION SNAPSHOTS AND LOCAL CACHE
===============================================================================

8.1 As-of semantics - approved U15 choice

For a Saturday run using Friday's last valid snapshot:
    t_run = Saturday launch time
    t0    = Friday snapshot time

Entry quotes, IV, rate context and remaining option lifetime at initialization
are all interpreted at Friday t0. The engine evolves forward from t0, including
the elapsed weekend under its model. It is not a probability conditioned on all
information that became available by Saturday.

Never take Friday option premiums, set their valuation time to Saturday and claim
that nothing changed. That would reduce time-to-expiry without corresponding
repricing/calibration and may manufacture initial profit.

If the best valid snapshot is slightly before the session end, permitted times
between that t0 and the actual end belong to the as-of-t0 simulation. They are not
reconstructed observations of what actually happened. Disclose that distinction.
Do not condition on 'the target has not been hit since Friday' without data.

8.2 Retained broker data

Retained quotes are usable only when their event times and the companion spot or
forward, volatility and parameter state establish a valid prior-session context.

Critical example: live after-hours SPY shares cannot be combined with closed SPY
option quotes. Similarly, an underlying summary close at 16:00 and unrelated last
option quotes at 16:15 do not prove a coherent 16:00 snapshot. To use 16:00, obtain
option data actually known at that time. To use 16:15, obtain a compatible
underlying reference known then. If neither exists, do not reconstruct them.

The close/prev-close/last fields are different data types, not interchangeable
bid/ask snapshots. Historical candles alone do not supply a complete option quote
and IV state. The first-release adapter does not call a backtesting API to paper
over missing contemporaneous inputs.

8.3 Cache capture and coverage

While the reference CLI is actually running and the relevant session is active:
- Subscribe to/collect selected legs and required reference contracts.
- After an expiration is selected, attempt to capture both puts and calls across
  that expiration, subject to provider limits and actual available data.
- Maintain per-instrument event-time buffers and common spot/forward/parameter
  state, rather than declaring a serially downloaded chain one instantaneous quote.
- Assemble snapshots by as-of joins at a common t0; measure dispersion explicitly.
- Persist a validated snapshot after each completed live acquisition.
- Continue lightweight collection during the application's input wait using its
  own event loop, if that collector is implemented and remains active.
- Before a session closes, preserve the latest valid state available to the app.

This is application behavior only while the process is running. Closing the app,
Android suspending it, or never having started it means it did not collect data.
There is no promise of an always-on service or unrequested scheduled collection.

Store a coverage manifest of actual expirations, contracts, timestamps, quote
fields, IV sources, parameter sources and validation outcomes. Full expiration
coverage is attempted, not falsely guaranteed. A cached last strategy does not
provide uncaptured strikes for a newly requested trade.

8.4 Cache storage

Use immutable, atomic snapshot records and a small manifest. Standard-library
JSON plus optional gzip is sufficient; no database dependency is required.

Suggested layout:
    <private-app-dir>/p50/cache/<underlying>/<session-id>/<snapshot-hash>.json.gz
    <private-app-dir>/p50/cache/manifest.json
    <private-app-dir>/p50/results/<run-id>.json

Use temporary file -> flush/close -> atomic replace. Do not overwrite a valid
snapshot with partial acquisition. Verify schema version and content hash when
reading. Keep the active session and two completed sessions by default, plus
explicitly saved result snapshots. Automatic prior-session selection does not
fall back across several earlier sessions just because an older file exists.

If all historical inputs of a validated cached snapshot exist, a closed-session
run can be computed without a fresh quote connection. Calendar/contract validity
must still be established from an applicable cached or current rules package.
Authentication failure during an expected live session is not such a fallback.

8.5 Cold-start limitation

A first-ever after-hours run succeeds only if the broker exposes enough retained
state to build the snapshot. Without that state or a local record, a valid prior
snapshot cannot be guaranteed. The program must fail informatively rather than
claim that caching can retroactively recover data it never observed.

===============================================================================
09. TIMESTAMP, FRESHNESS AND CONSISTENCY VALIDATION
===============================================================================

9.1 Immutable snapshot fields

Each snapshot records:
- t0, t_run, session identity, live/prior mode and original event timestamps.
- Exact contract definitions and actual series selection.
- Bid, ask, sizes, computed mid, broker mid, broker mark and last where available.
- Underlying spot and/or terminal-maturity forward, with provenance.
- Raw vendor IV, selected frozen IV and any quote-inverted IV separately.
- Path IV and its maturity, normalization and timestamp.
- Raw/normalized rate, yield or carry inputs and effective-date knowledge.
- Pricing/fixing model, calendar version and eligible monitoring intervals.
- Input quality warnings and the exact profile/override decisions used.

9.2 Price and timestamp rules

All required prices finite; S/F and strikes positive. Bid >= 0, ask > 0 and
ask >= bid. Zero bid is possible but flagged. Crossed quotes are not repaired by
swapping their sides. Missing values are not converted to zero.

Use source/event time for freshness. Reception time is an audit field only. Every
quote used in a historical t0 must be known at or before that t0. As-of joins only
move backward. A schema field with unknown timing semantics is marked unverified;
it cannot satisfy strict previous-session reconstruction by itself.

For live snapshots, apply Section 04's age/dispersion limits at t0. For cached or
retained snapshots, apply prior limits at historical t0, NOT at t_run. Days since
Friday are displayed as snapshot age, not interpreted as seconds of in-session
quote staleness. Rate/dividend data use effective-date semantics, not a two-second
quote rule.

Do not use a Saturday-fetched rate as a Friday-known rate merely because rates
usually change slowly. A documented effective period covering t0, a saved value
known at t0, a valid as-of quote-implied fit, or an explicitly dated override is
required. The same principle applies to volatility and dividend metadata.

9.3 Whole-position consistency

Validate selected contracts together with the reference options used for IV or
forward fits. Keep quote-basis and fitted-model residuals per leg and per strategy.
Reject incompatible fixing, units, roots, rate scaling and incompatible IV clocks.

Display entry and hypothetical close basis explicitly. Model midpoint profit is
not proof that a complex order could execute there. Bid/ask depth and future
slippage are not forecast from the snapshot.

9.4 Current model basis

    B0 = entry_cash + sum_i(n_i * multiplier_i * model_price_i(t0))

For a hypothetical midpoint entry, B0 should be near zero. First check stale data,
anchor choice, units, pricing model and IV inversion if it is not.

Define R_model=sum_i(n_i*multiplier_i*(model_price_i(t0)-quote_mid_i(t0))).
The explicit material-basis tolerance is eps_basis=max(0.01 USD,0.10*H0).
Apply it to abs(R_model), not to actual spread costs or an existing position's
genuine accumulated P&L. A model-generated initial target hit contradicting a
failed quote/model basis check is an error, not 100% success. Do not subtract
B0 permanently from future values or erase the entry/campaign cash history.

An existing position can genuinely have nonzero P&L at t0. Its original entry
cash must not be refitted to make that P&L zero.

===============================================================================
10. RATES, DIVIDENDS AND CARRY
===============================================================================

10.1 Rate resolution

Priority:
1. An explicit applicable MARKET_INPUT_OVERRIDES rate, if deliberately supplied.
2. A properly normalized, as-of-valid broker rate from public margin configuration.
3. A well-conditioned contemporaneous European put-call fit, if available.
4. Otherwise MISSING_RATE or AMBIGUOUS_RATE_UNITS, with no extra prompt.

The broker's risk-free-rate field is now an identified data source. It is not
proof of the rate used by P50. [SRC-D, Section 30] Treat that choice as an approved broker-sourced
proxy. Preserve raw value, normalization and source/effective timestamps.

The serializer's 'decimal string' description does not alone prove whether the
number is an annual fraction, percent or basis points, nor its compounding.
The normalized parameter record must explicitly state raw units, compounding,
tenor and effective/known-at times under P50-R06. Implement those conversions
exactly; no outside documentation is needed to define them. An optional raw
adapter without this unit contract rejects the field. Never guess by silently
dividing by 100. If the unit contract cannot be established, proceed to the
quote-implied branch or a normalized global override; do not publish a misleading
rate. This is a resolved error policy, not an additional interactive question.

Normalize to an annual continuously compounded rate for the chosen maturity.
Convert a known effective annual rate as r_cc = log(1 + r_effective). For known
simple rates use the actual quoted tenor in the discount factor before deriving
r_cc. Use a flat rate across this position's horizon once selected, recording the
flat-rate proxy when a full curve is not supplied. No secret live Treasury lookup
or second financial-data vendor is added.

10.2 Continuous dividend-yield approximation

For spot-based American ETF pricing, use constant q for this snapshot. Priority:
1. A deliberately supplied, applicable yield override.
2. A clearly defined broker annual dividend-yield/current dividend-rate field.
3. If the API only supplies a current dividend amount and frequency, and their
   semantics/units are established, q_proxy = amount * payments_per_year / S0.
4. For eligible European products with live spot, quote-implied carry.
5. Otherwise MISSING_DIVIDEND_OR_CARRY_INPUT.

The normalized quote schema supports dividend amount/frequency fields for some
equities. It does not promise complete forward dividend forecasts for every
symbol. [SRC-D, Section 30] The frequency-derived q is an approved continuous-yield approximation,
not a forecast cash-dividend schedule. A documented zero-dividend rate may be
used as zero; a missing field may not.

The initial profile does not query historical dividend time series or simulate
cash-dividend jumps. It also does not require users to type an ex-dividend schedule
for every trade. Surface known ex-dividend/earnings flags when available and state
that scheduled cash jumps and earnings jump risk are not explicitly modeled.

10.3 Pricing and path consistency within each branch

Spot branch: use the same r and q in path drift and option pricing; only the
approved path-volatility versus frozen-leg-IV distinction remains.
Forward branch: use the terminal-maturity forward and its discount factor;
separate q is unnecessary for that branch.

Do not combine continuous q with a second subtraction of the same discrete cash
dividend. A future discrete-dividend adapter must change both pricing and path
evolution and supply different endpoint benchmarks; it is not quietly enabled
by the presence of a dividend date.

===============================================================================
11. SPX AND OTHER EUROPEAN-INDEX FORWARD ANCHORS
===============================================================================

11.1 Approved solution to stale cash SPX during GTH

Use a quote-implied terminal-maturity forward rather than pretending an unchanged
cash-index print is live. The same forward adapter can be used in RTH, avoiding
an artificial model switch when trading sessions change. Retain the cash index
as a diagnostic and use it only where its timestamp is compatible.

This is the approved solution to U17, not a verified statement that tastytrade's
P50 engine uses this anchor.

11.2 Forward construction when r is known

For European calls and puts with the same K, T and settlement:

    tau0 = year_fraction(t0, T)
    D0   = exp(-r * tau0)
    C0(K) - P0(K) = D0 * (F0 - K)
    F0(K) = K + (C0(K) - P0(K)) / D0

Use synchronized bid/ask quotes of multiple liquid call-put pairs near the
forward/spot region, not just the user's legs. Starting with at least five valid
pairs is the engineering default; expand the reference strike window as needed.
Only liquid, valid data are used; do not substitute a guessed forward if fewer
usable pairs or unacceptable dispersion prevent validation.

Each pair implies an executable-quote uncertainty interval:
    F_low(K)  = K + (call_bid - put_ask) / D0
    F_high(K) = K + (call_ask - put_bid) / D0

Fit one robust common F0 using quote uncertainty weights, inspect the overlap and
residuals, and run leave-one-pair-out stability checks. Preserve rejected pairs
and their reasons. Do not remove arbitrary quotes to make the platform P50 fit.

11.3 Joint discount/forward fit when the rate source is unavailable

Fit multiple strikes to:
    C(K) - P(K) = A - B*K
    B = D0 > 0
    F0 = A/B > 0
    r = -log(B)/tau0

Use actual bid/ask bounds in addition to midpoint residuals and quantify the
feasible uncertainty in A, B and F0. A constrained least-squares/linear-feasibility
implementation is appropriate. Check maturity, exercise style and common fixing.

Short-dated options can identify F0 much more reliably than r. Do not report a
precise quote-implied rate from an ill-conditioned slope. If rate uncertainty is
material to repricing/target probabilities, require the validated broker rate or
a normalized global override. Do not borrow SPY American put-call equality to
construct a European discount fit.

11.4 Forward state and valuation

Use Y_t = F(t,T), the forward for the ACTUAL contract maturity, as the simulated
state. With deterministic rates/carry under the approved model:
    dY_t = sigma_path * Y_t * dW_t

This zero forward drift is the forward-coordinate form of the spot risk-neutral
model, not the unrelated assumption that stock-price drift is zero.

At T, Y_T equals the modeled terminal underlying fixing. At earlier times, price
European options with the Black forward formula in Section 16. Never put F0 into
a spot BSM formula and also apply the carry adjustment a second time.

11.5 Live-data requirement

Live SPX option quotes with stale cash-index data are acceptable only if this
forward/IV/discount context is valid and current. If the forward cannot be
constructed, return LIVE_FORWARD_UNAVAILABLE. Do not combine live overnight
quotes with prior-session IV solely to keep the program running.

===============================================================================
12. VOLATILITY SELECTION AND FROZEN-LEG REPRICING
===============================================================================

12.1 Path volatility

Select one annual decimal sigma_path for the full forward simulation:
1. Explicit matching override, if supplied.
2. Current/as-of-valid broker expiration IV matching expiration, root/settlement
   and chain type.
3. If that aggregate is missing or stale, fit a local ATM reference volatility
   from current quotes with the chosen spot/forward anchor and pricer.
4. Otherwise MISSING_PATH_IV.

Do not use IV rank, IV percentile, a generic multi-maturity IV index or a different
expiration's IV as an undocumented substitute. A broker expiration IV is available
as a type of market-metrics output, not guaranteed fresh in every session. [SRC-D, Section 30]

For the ATM fallback, apply the complete contract selection, American IV
inversion, vega/spread weighting, weighted-median fit and identification gates
in P50-R12. Preserve the contracts, method, uncertainty and residuals. Do not
estimate it from historical returns or obtain a fitting procedure elsewhere.

12.2 Per-leg IVs

Each leg gets a frozen sigma_i at t0. Prefer an as-of-compatible vendor Greeks IV
that reprices the corresponding quote within the permitted residual. Preserve
the raw vendor IV even if it is not used.

If the vendor IV is absent, stale, or incompatible with the approved pricer, infer
sigma_i from that leg's valid midpoint under the SAME chosen pricing/anchor/rate
context. Label it QUOTE_INVERTED, not VENDOR_IV. This is a calibration fallback,
not a permanent dollar offset applied to future prices.

Use the explicitly bracketed IV algorithm and final residual/bracket-width
checks in P50-R12. Verify option bounds and vega/identification. At
intrinsic-value plateaus, especially American deep-ITM options, a unique IV may
not be recoverable. In that case a valid compatible vendor IV or explicit scoped
override is needed; do not arbitrarily choose an IV because the root is flat.

12.3 Future IV behavior

Freeze sigma_path and each sigma_i for the simulation. No mean reversion,
volatility-of-volatility, spot/vol correlation, smile rolling, historical skew
calibration or jumps are added. A leg keeps its same scalar IV when spot changes;
this is not a complete dynamically evolving volatility surface.

When all sigma_i equal sigma_path, the coherent common-volatility benchmark is
available. Otherwise prominently report the frozen-leg-IV hybrid qualification
from Section 01.

12.4 Model-quality checks

Check current quote fit and grid-wide contract/vertical bounds, exercise payoff
bounds and numerical monotonicity where justified. Frozen strike IVs can create
inconsistent future cross-strike marks. If material static-arbitrage/portfolio-
bound violations occur within the evaluated region, flag MODEL_MARKS_INVALID;
do not clamp spread values, force a different volatility model, or count an
impossible model profit as a success.

Passing these checks is not proof of full dynamic no-arbitrage. It is a necessary
quality guard for this explicitly approved approximation.

===============================================================================
13. CASH ACCOUNTING, TARGET AND COSTS
===============================================================================

13.1 Signed cash and portfolio value

For leg i:
    n_i = signed contract quantity, positive long / negative short
    m_i = validated contract multiplier
    K_i = strike
    e_i = assumed entry premium per quoted unit
    v_i(t,x) = selected theoretical option mark per unit

Entry cash:
    C_entry = -sum_i(n_i * m_i * e_i)

Or use a matching explicit net entry_cash_dollars override. Do not redistribute
an overridden net credit arbitrarily across legs: the portfolio target requires
net cash, not invented per-leg historical fills.

Signed remaining position value:
    V(t,x) = sum_i(n_i * m_i * v_i(t,x))

Gross trade P&L:
    Pi(t,x) = C_entry + V(t,x)

Positive C_entry is a credit; negative is a debit. For a credit spread, V is
normally the negative remaining closing liability. C_entry does not accrue
interest in the reported ordinary trade P&L convention.

13.2 Terminal payoff and extrema

    g_call(s,K) = max(s-K, 0)
    g_put(s,K)  = max(K-s, 0)
    G(s) = sum_i(n_i * m_i * g_i(s,K_i))

    M = C_entry + sup_{s>=0} G(s)
    L = max(0, -C_entry - inf_{s>=0} G(s))

Compute extrema from the piecewise-linear payoff: evaluate zero, all distinct
strikes and the final tail slope. The high-price slope is the sum of n_i*m_i over
calls. Positive slope implies unbounded maximum profit; negative implies an
unbounded loss tail. Preserve infinity; do not replace it with a sampled-grid
maximum. Validate M > 0 and finite for this target definition.

13.3 Entry selection

Default e_i is the midpoint computed from the valid bid/ask of each leg. Preserve
the broker mid and mark separately; disagreement beyond rounding is a diagnostic.
Do not use last trade as an entry premium merely because a bid is missing.

A supplied exact-position net entry override takes precedence, allowing the
platform's intended order-ticket price or original position cost to be matched.
No entry-price question is added to the interactive stages.

13.4 Frozen target

    alpha = 0.50
    h = alpha * M

Freeze C_entry, M and h before simulating. Never recompute maximum profit from
future option values or move the target as a path becomes favorable/unfavorable.

The target is model P&L, not a short-strike touch, delta threshold, theta amount,
breakeven crossing or terminal ITM probability.

13.5 Gross and optional fixed-cost net results

Primary results are gross. This is the approved U12 fallback, not confirmation
that tastytrade's internal P50 excludes every cost.

Only when an exact-position total fixed round-trip cost F_cost is supplied:
    Pi_net(t,x) = C_entry + V(t,x) - F_cost
    M_net = M - F_cost
    h_net = alpha * M_net

Require M_net > 0. This net output means half of maximum NET profit under the
specified fixed closing-cost assumption. It is not the same as earning half of
maximum GROSS profit after costs.

Net closing probabilities use the fixed closing-cost schedule. Do not silently
apply a closing commission at terminal settlement and call it an actual expiry
fee. Net terminal results require an explicitly stated terminal-cost convention;
otherwise omit them while preserving gross terminal diagnostics. A single optional
TRADE_OVERRIDES entry holds costs; no fee wizard or additional required globals.

No future bid/ask, slippage, queue position or guaranteed execution model is
invented. A mark-based target touch does not ensure a fill.

13.6 Accounting test fixture - not live data

    Short 767 put, long 765 put, one spread, multiplier 100
    Assumed entry credit: 0.80 points = 80 dollars
    Maximum profit: 80 dollars
    Maximum loss: 120 dollars
    50% target: 40 dollars
    Required theoretical closing debit: <= 0.40 points

For debit width w and debit d in price points:
    max profit = w-d
    half-profit target requires close value >= (w+d)/2

Both relationships are mandatory unit tests.

===============================================================================
14. PROBABILITY EVENT AND THE TWO HORIZONS
===============================================================================

14.1 Closing-profit hitting event

Let A be the eligible option-trading observation times for the entire strategy,
not the times at which the underlying alone can trade.

    H_close = inf { t >= t0 : t in A and Pi(t,X_t) >= h }

Use +infinity if there is no hit. X_t is spot S_t or terminal-maturity forward Y_t,
according to the chosen product adapter.

    P50_close_by(t_star) = Q_path(H_close <= t_star | snapshot at t0)

A path that first hits the target and then loses still counts once. A path that
loses first and subsequently hits also counts. There is no stop-loss, adjustment,
roll, assignment event or forced liquidation policy inside this event.

14.2 Terminal settlement reference

Compute separately:
    J_T = 1{C_entry + G(X_T) >= h}

    P50_reference_by_T =
        Q_path(H_close <= T OR J_T = 1 | snapshot at t0)

This includes target achievement through terminal payoff. It must not be called
an executable close after t_last. Preserve the closing-only expiry probability
as a separate result and the incremental settlement-only success probability.

The primary P50_by_deadline equals P50_close_by(t_star) when t_star < T. If the
user sets the deadline exactly to T, it follows the declared reference convention
including terminal payoff, with a visible 'includes terminal settlement' label;
the closing-only companion is still shown. Thus the same defined event is
nondecreasing through T, and the terminal contribution is not hidden.

14.3 Endpoint diagnostics

    P50_at_deadline = Q_path(Pi(t_star,X_t_star) >= h)
    POP_expiry_cent = Q_path(C_entry + G(X_T) >= 0.01 dollars)
    POP_expiry_strict = Q_path(C_entry + G(X_T) > 0)

The cent threshold is a position-dollar threshold, not an option-price-point or
per-share threshold. Tastytrade documents at least $0.01 for equity/ETF POP; that
does not establish every distribution convention of its position/portfolio POP.
[SRC-D, Section 30] Label these outputs 'model POP', not vendor-identical POP/ePOP.

An at-deadline mark during a closed session is explicitly theoretical. Its event
need not be included in closing-only P50 because the deadline is not tradable.

14.4 True expiration must remain unchanged

For every simulated time t:
    tau = year_fraction(t, actual T)

The user deadline stops profit-hit counting; it does not replace actual T in the
option price. The same option chain, rate/IV context, entry and pricing machinery
is used for both the custom deadline and full-expiration reference result.

For t_star < T, time value remains in the deadline option prices. There is no
valid general conversion of a platform P50(T) into P50(t_star) by multiplying a
DTE fraction, using sqrt(time), delta, or one terminal normal CDF.

===============================================================================
15. UNDERLYING DYNAMICS AND EXACT TRANSITIONS
===============================================================================

15.1 Spot branch

Approved constant-parameter process:
    dS_t = (r-q) * S_t * dt + sigma_path * S_t * dW_t

For elapsed model years dt:
    S_next = S_now * exp((r-q-0.5*sigma_path^2)*dt
                        + sigma_path*sqrt(dt)*Z)
    Z ~ independent standard Normal(0,1)

Use exact GBM transitions, not Euler steps that can make prices negative. The
same underlying path drives every leg. Do not simulate puts and calls or spread
legs independently.

15.2 European-index forward branch

For Y_t = F(t,T):
    Y_next = Y_now * exp(-0.5*sigma_path^2*dt
                        + sigma_path*sqrt(dt)*Z)

With deterministic rates/carry, this is the same constant-parameter family in
forward coordinates. At T it supplies the terminal fixing used for payoff.

15.3 Model clock

    year_fraction(a,b) = (epoch_seconds(b)-epoch_seconds(a)) / 31536000

That is ACT/365F with actual elapsed time. Diffusion and time decay continue
through closed periods; closure only restricts profit observations. A full
weekend is not compressed to zero variance or zero theta.

The source IV annualization must be normalized to this clock when known. If the
vendor's variance clock is not documented, use the approved annual ACT/365F model
interpretation and record it as an assumption rather than claiming a measured
vendor conversion. Never silently multiply by sqrt(365/252).

15.4 No physical-probability claim

No historical drift is estimated. Neither using broker IV nor adding more Monte
Carlo paths establishes the real-world frequency of profit targets. This profile
estimates the event under its declared path model and frozen mark rules.

===============================================================================
16. EUROPEAN AND AMERICAN OPTION VALUATION
===============================================================================

16.1 European spot formula

For each leg's frozen sigma_i and tau > 0:
    d1 = [log(S/K) + (r-q+0.5*sigma_i^2)*tau] / (sigma_i*sqrt(tau))
    d2 = d1 - sigma_i*sqrt(tau)

    Call = S*exp(-q*tau)*Phi(d1) - K*exp(-r*tau)*Phi(d2)
    Put  = K*exp(-r*tau)*Phi(-d2) - S*exp(-q*tau)*Phi(-d1)

Phi is the standard normal CDF. At tau = 0 use intrinsic payoff. At sigma_i = 0
use the deterministic limit, not division by zero. Use stable evaluations in
extreme tails and float64 numerical work.

16.2 European forward formula

With Y = F(t,T) and D = exp(-r*tau):
    d1 = [log(Y/K) + 0.5*sigma_i^2*tau] / (sigma_i*sqrt(tau))
    d2 = d1 - sigma_i*sqrt(tau)

    Call = D * [Y*Phi(d1) - K*Phi(d2)]
    Put  = D * [K*Phi(-d2) - Y*Phi(-d1)]

Do not discount twice. Verify equality to the spot formulation when
Y = S*exp((r-q)*tau). This is an independent mandatory test.

16.3 American ETF pricer

Implement the complete monotone finite-difference, boundary-bracketing and
complementarity algorithm in P50-R09 through P50-R11 under the same r, q, expiry
and per-leg sigma_i context. Each leg's auxiliary pricing model can differ from Q_path in sigma_i;
this is the previously disclosed frozen-leg-IV approximation.

The value is the optimal exercise value, not merely the European formula with an
American label. Implement a one-dimensional finite-difference obstacle problem
in log spot. Start with fully implicit, positivity-preserving stepping and a
tridiagonal policy-iteration solve using SciPy's compiled linear algebra.

At every pricing step:
- Impose the terminal intrinsic payoff at T.
- Respect the American exercise obstacle v >= intrinsic.
- Solve continuation and exercise regions to a measured residual tolerance.
- Use drift discretization/grid choices that preserve a valid generator and
  nonnegative transition behavior.
- Use verified low/high-spot asymptotic boundary behavior.

A tree implementation is an independent comparison, not a separate mandatory
compiled dependency. Avoid requiring QuantLib compilation on Termux for the first
release.

16.4 Pricing-grid convergence

Start with an adaptive log-spot domain including current reference price, all
strikes and relevant path mass; an initial 1601-node grid is an engineering
starting point, not a final accuracy claim. Refine space/time and expand the
domain until portfolio prices, target boundaries and resulting probabilities are
stable to their allocated numerical tolerances.

Include observation times and endpoint deadlines in the pricing schedule. During
long closed intervals, the American PDE still needs adequate internal stepping;
do not take one huge exercise/pricing step solely because the path simulator can
use one exact GBM transition over the closure.

Retain current/next time slices for each leg, build the portfolio target regions
while moving backward, and discard unnecessary full option-price surfaces. Keep
only the slices needed for explicit endpoint diagnostics or a validated fallback.
This avoids a large time x spot x leg tensor in Android memory.

Do not clamp negative or impossible values after solving and call the solver
converged. Expose residual, grid sensitivity and domain-expansion results.

16.5 Exercise, assignment and settlement limitations

American pricing incorporates the right to exercise. It does NOT simulate actual
short-option assignments, unwanted share positions, broker risk liquidation or
position changes. The probability event assumes the specified portfolio remains
intact. Ordinary terminal payoff ignores post-fixing assignment/pin surprises;
state this limitation, especially for short-dated physically settled ETF options.

The chosen continuous-yield dividend profile is not an exact discrete-dividend
American model. A more elaborate pricer is not evidence of matching tastytrade's
internal P50 engine.

===============================================================================
17. TARGET-REGION CONSTRUCTION AND ACCELERATION
===============================================================================

17.1 Deterministic regions

With frozen model parameters, at each eligible time t define:
    R_alpha(t) = { x > 0 : C_entry + V(t,x) >= alpha*M }

Precompute these regions before Monte Carlo whenever their topology can be
validated. Paths then need region-membership tests rather than repeated option
pricing for every leg/path/time combination.

17.2 Standard spreads

For a put credit vertical with a common-volatility European pricer:
    D(t,x) = Put(short strike) - Put(long strike)
    target iff D(t,x) <= (1-alpha)*entry_credit_per_unit

It normally yields an upper spot boundary. A call credit vertical normally yields
a lower boundary. Under differing frozen leg IVs, do not presume those monotone
properties without checking; solve the actual total portfolio condition.

17.3 General combinations

For condors, butterflies and arbitrary supported combinations:
- Locate all target intervals, not just one guessed barrier.
- Evaluate all strike-adjacent and high-curvature regions.
- Check stationary points and narrow/tangent target sets, not only sign changes.
- Use bracketed root solvers after establishing candidate intervals.
- Distinguish empty, all-state, bounded and disjoint regions.
- Check asymptotic tails and expand the domain when necessary.
- Compare membership against direct portfolio pricing on independent test points.

When topology is unreliable, fall back to direct European vectorized pricing or
a validated American valuation-grid lookup. Do not drop a target interval merely
to accelerate the calculation. Extra temporary disk-backed grids are preferable
to silently exceeding memory or losing target regions.

17.4 No unjustified path clipping

An endpoint tail-mass estimate is not a bound on every path excursion. If simulated
states leave an American solver domain, expand and rerun the affected numerical
setup reproducibly, or return an unresolved-domain diagnostic. Do not pin paths
to the nearest grid boundary. European direct pricing can be evaluated outside
the original grid and used to validate expanded regions.

===============================================================================
18. OBSERVATION GRID, CLOSED PERIODS AND TERMINAL PAYOFF
===============================================================================

18.1 Production monitoring convention

Count first hits only in actual supported option sessions, plus the separately
declared terminal-payoff event. Start with 60-second observations and refine in
a separate pilot. Endpoint-only sampling can miss a touch between observations;
exact GBM endpoints do not make the continuous hitting calculation exact.

This is the approved U8/U9 solution. No Brownian-bridge hit correction is applied
to the production result. A bridge or probability PDE may be used independently
to diagnose continuous-monitoring sensitivity, not to alter the vendor-comparison
number secretly.

18.2 Grid construction

Construct a master observation grid through T that depends on snapshot, product
sessions and chosen resolution, not merely on t_star:
- Regular observations aligned to an absolute clock grid inside eligible sessions.
- t0 if it is an eligible observation.
- Session openings and the last eligible pre-close observation/left-limit event.
- Contract-specific last-trading boundary and true terminal fixing.
- Exact requested deadlines where they are eligible.
- Exact optional additional reporting times.

Primary Stage-3 deadlines are at whole New York minutes and are naturally included
in all standard 60-second-halving grids. Use integer timestamp arithmetic to
avoid floating-point time drift. Boundaries must obey the actual half-open
session/cutoff semantics; do not invent a tradable quote after a cutoff.

For comparable runs, persist the master grid and use it for both the requested
horizon and the expiry reference. Adding an off-grid optional reporting time can
change a sampled estimate slightly; keep the union fixed in a comparison and
include it in the run identity instead of claiming unchanged random sampling.

18.3 Closures

Propagate the underlying across a closed gap using its complete elapsed duration.
Do not check for success inside that gap and do not interpolate/bridge-count
untradable touches. At the next opening, revalue and check the target immediately.

For a deadline inside a gap, first-hit probability equals that accumulated up to
the last eligible preceding observation. A separate endpoint probability is
computed under the distribution at the precise deadline; it is not a closing hit.

18.4 Terminal handling

For the main expiration reference, evaluate intrinsic payoff once at T and count
previously unhit paths that achieve the target then. Save a separate flag/counter
for settlement-only successes. Do not subtract a modeled closing fee at T without
an explicitly applicable terminal-cost convention.

If t_last < T, evolve through that interval without closing observations. An AM
settlement product is not enabled unless its dedicated economic fixing adapter
exists; a generic simulated 09:30 index is not that adapter.

===============================================================================
19. MONTE CARLO ESTIMATOR, PRECISION AND CONVERGENCE
===============================================================================

19.1 First-hit estimator

For N independent production paths and first-hit times H_j:
    p_hat(t_star) = sum_j(1{H_j <= t_star}) / N

Use N = 200000 for the approved default. Fix N before production begins. Use
P50-R16's exact PCG64/SeedSequence semantic stream keys and canonical4096-path
shards, independent of worker count, scheduling and survivor pruning. Record the library version, seed, shard scheme and stream identifiers; do not
rely on an unspecified future default_rng choice.

Every leg shares one path. Every target/deadline uses the same production paths
within a run. A successful path keeps its success flag even after adverse moves.

19.2 Batching and efficient continuation

Process at most 4096 paths per batch initially, adjusting workspace to the memory
budget without changing the recorded RNG scheme. Store current state, first-hit
time/index per target and necessary counters, not complete price histories.

Once a path has hit all reported target thresholds, it need not continue merely
to compute endpoint POP: the chosen one-factor endpoint distribution can be
integrated separately. Paths not yet successful continue through T for the
reference calculation. Do not stop survivors at t_star when P50(T) is requested.

For multiple targets, do not discard a path that hit 50% but has not yet hit a
higher requested target. Settlement accounting uses the surviving states plus
already-recorded success flags. Sampling stays IID across complete path outcomes.

19.3 Sampling uncertainty

Approximate standard error:
    SE = sqrt(p_hat*(1-p_hat)/N)

Report a 95% Wilson interval:
    z = 1.959963984540054
    d = 1 + z*z/N
    center = (p_hat + z*z/(2*N)) / d
    half = z/d * sqrt(p_hat*(1-p_hat)/N + z*z/(4*N*N))
    interval = [center-half, center+half]

At N=200000, the approximate worst-case sampling half-width is 0.219 percentage
points. This describes independent-path sampling error only, not model error,
quote uncertainty, vendor mismatch, missed intrastep hits or pricing-grid error.
Zero observed hits is not proof of zero underlying event probability; 100% observed
hits is not proof of certainty unless a deterministic event analysis establishes it.

Do not repeatedly stop at a favorable sample estimate while reporting an ordinary
fixed-N interval. A cancelled run is marked incomplete with its actual path count.

19.4 Independent convergence pilot

Use independent pilot streams, initially 20000 paths, to compare coupled grids:
    60, 30, 15, 7.5, 3.75, 1.875 seconds

The fine/coarse comparisons must share underlying continuous-path increments
properly, e.g. by summing fine Brownian increments to coarse intervals. Holding a
random seed constant while drawing unrelated arrays is not a coupled comparison.

Compare all important reported deadlines/targets, including expiry. Where the
only difference is added monitoring points, fine-grid hits cannot erase coarse
grid hits. Track the fraction of paths gaining a hit. Use its paired uncertainty
or Wilson upper bound; seek an upper estimated adjacent-grid change no greater
than 0.0025 probability on two successive refinements.

Choose the production resolution before starting its independent fixed-N run.
If the finest permitted grid or resource cap fails the criterion, show
NUMERICAL_CONVERGENCE_UNRESOLVED. A sampled estimate may be saved/displayed as
preliminary with its actual grid; do not label it a resolved continuous-hit value.

An adjacent-grid test, even when passed, is not a rigorous bound to continuous
time. Keep observation-grid, spatial-grid, quote-basis and Monte Carlo uncertainty
as distinct diagnostics. More paths do not repair model error.

19.5 Independent benchmark solvers

Use the analytical terminal probabilities, exact truncated-normal transition
recursion and separate continuous-probability PDE fully defined in P50-R19, plus
the independent fixtures in P50-R21. No outside benchmark script is required. The probability PDE outside the target is:
    u_t + (r-q)*s*u_s + 0.5*sigma_path^2*s^2*u_ss = 0

No -r*u term appears because u is an undiscounted probability. Impose u=1 in the
eligible target region and propagate without absorption through closed periods.
For the forward state replace the drift by zero.

A diagnostic solver must not become the production algorithm automatically because
it produces a closer tastytrade percentage.

===============================================================================
20. SECONDARY PROBABILITIES AND ANALYTICAL BENCHMARKS
===============================================================================

20.1 Endpoint integration under this profile

For H = year_fraction(t0,t):

Spot branch:
    log(S_t) ~ Normal(log(S0)+(r-q-0.5*sigma_path^2)*H,
                     sigma_path^2*H)

Forward branch:
    log(Y_t) ~ Normal(log(Y0)-0.5*sigma_path^2*H,
                     sigma_path^2*H)

Find every interval [a,b] where the relevant target or terminal payoff condition
holds. Integrate each interval with the lognormal CDF and sum disjoint pieces.
Use 0/infinity limits explicitly and handle deterministic zero-variance cases.

For a > 0 and b < infinity with normal log-mean mu and log-sd sd:
    probability([a,b]) = Phi((log(b)-mu)/sd) - Phi((log(a)-mu)/sd)

These endpoint calculations need not carry production paths past their first hit.
For an American deadline mark, only the pricing-grid/region approximation remains;
at expiration the piecewise-linear payoff intervals are analytical.

Do not apply this lognormal benchmark to a future cash-dividend-jump or stochastic-
volatility extension without changing its distribution.

20.2 Credit-vertical endpoint fixture

For a put credit spread with short strike Ks, long strike Kl < Ks, credit c and
fraction alpha, in price points:
    terminal profit >= alpha*c iff S_T >= Ks - (1-alpha)*c

For alpha=0.50:
    B50 = Ks - c/2

For positive gross expiration profit:
    B0 = Ks-c

The $0.01 whole-position criterion shifts the boundary by
    0.01 / (spread_quantity * multiplier)
relative to the zero-profit boundary. Do not confuse this dollar threshold with
0.01 quoted option points.

Spot-branch half-profit endpoint probability:
    Phi([log(S0/B50)+(r-q-0.5*sigma_path^2)*H]
         / [sigma_path*sqrt(H)])

For a call credit spread the terminal half-profit region is
    S_T <= Ks+(1-alpha)*c

These are ENDPOINT probabilities, not P50 first-hit probabilities.

20.3 Probability invariants

On the same snapshot, paths, target and event convention:
- Every probability is in [0,1].
- A later deadline cannot lower the first-hit probability.
- A higher profit threshold cannot increase it.
- Adding observations cannot erase earlier hits.
- Closing P50 is at least at-deadline target probability when that exact deadline
  is an included eligible observation.
- Expiry reference including terminal payoff is at least target-at-expiry.
- There is NO universal requirement that P50 exceed expiration POP.
- Do not apply the closing-versus-endpoint inequality to a closed-market deadline.
- Probability of a hit before t_star uses no observations after t_star.

===============================================================================
21. FIRST-HIT REPORTING AND ADDITIONAL TARGETS/DEADLINES
===============================================================================

21.1 Mandatory outputs

Always calculate:
1. P50 by the requested deadline, with its observation/settlement convention.
2. Closing-only P50 by expiry/last eligible observation.
3. Expiration-reference P50 including terminal settlement.
4. Probability of being at or above the 50% target exactly at the deadline.
5. Model POP at expiration using the $0.01 whole-position threshold.
6. Strictly-positive expiration POP as a mandatory saved diagnostic; display may be optional.

Report the main requested P50 first. Additional results must not obscure it.

21.2 First-hit distribution

Use saved first-hit indices to calculate cumulative probabilities by eligible
session/day and unconditional interval probabilities. Record a separate atom for
terminal-settlement-only achievement. Never call the density of first hits the
same thing as cumulative P50.

A conditional median/mean hit time is computed only over successful paths, with
the conditioning horizon stated. It is not the expected waiting time for every
trade, because some paths never hit. Show actual New York timestamps and optionally
elapsed hours from t0; do not imply elapsed time begins at t_run in prior mode.

21.3 Extra targets without more questions

EXTRA_TARGET_PCTS accepts a numeric scalar or a tuple/list of percentages.
Normalize to sorted unique fractions in (0,1], adding the mandatory 0.50. Numbers
in this global are percent values: 25 means 25%, not 0.25%. Reject invalid types,
nonfinite values and booleans. Do not use eval on strings from a data source.

The same paths and portfolio marks evaluate all target thresholds. Store each
threshold's first hit, or an equivalent running-maximum structure with verified
crossing times. Do not reuse the 50% first hit as the first hit of every target.

21.4 Legacy day-offset reporting

EXTRA_DAY_OFFSETS accepts one positive integer or a tuple/list of positive
integers, supporting both the original single-number and series requirements.
They mean New York calendar dates advanced from t0, preserving its local clock
time. Resolve DST deliberately; a calendar day is not always 24 elapsed hours.
If a preserved local time is ambiguous/nonexistent, reject that optional global
value instead of shifting it silently.

Build t_j from t0 plus j local calendar days. Include valid t_j <= T in the same
run's reporting grid. An optional t_j > T is listed as outside the horizon and
not silently clamped to T. The required Stage-3 deadline remains unchanged.

A deadline inside a closure is retained exactly and follows Section 18.3. Days are
not automatically trading days, and weekends are not skipped.

===============================================================================
22. TERMINAL OUTPUT AND REPRODUCIBILITY SCHEMA
===============================================================================

22.1 Primary display

Use ordinary readable text with no dependence on terminal graphics. Example
structure only; angle-bracket values are runtime fields, not invented results:

    Probability of reaching 50% of maximum profit by
    <YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM EDT/EST>: <computed percentage>
    95% Monte Carlo sampling interval: <lower> to <upper>

    Target: <dollars>, gross
    Entry: <credit/debit dollars>, <midpoint or explicit override>
    Symbol/root: <actual contract root>
    Expiration/fixing: <actual New York timestamp>
    Mode: <LIVE_RTH / LIVE_EXTENDED / PREVIOUS_SESSION>
    Snapshot as of: <t0 with offset>
    Program run at: <t_run with offset>
    Reference: <current spot or quote-implied forward>
    Paths: 200000; observation interval: <chosen seconds>
    Numerical status: <resolved-grid-tests / unresolved diagnostic>
    Model: GBM paths with frozen-leg-IV repricing
    Vendor equivalence: NOT VERIFIED

    Closing-only P50 by expiry: <computed>
    P50 including terminal settlement: <computed>
    Settlement-only additional probability: <computed>
    50% target exactly at deadline: <computed>
    Model POP at expiry ($0.01 threshold): <computed>

    Data quality / model basis: <concise diagnostics>
    Saved result: <actual path>

For an expiry deadline, the main line explicitly states whether terminal payoff
is included. For a closed-market endpoint, add 'theoretical mark; not an available
closing observation'. Do not print numerical placeholders as real percentages.

22.2 Prior-session warning

Place next to the main result, not buried in logs:
    'Calculated as of <t0> from the previous session. This is not conditioned on
     market information arriving after that snapshot, and the assumed entry is
     not an available current fill.'

Print snapshot age separately from quote age at t0. A Friday snapshot's age on
Saturday is expected, not a reason to reset all source timestamps to Saturday.

22.3 Saved JSON record

Persist at least these groups:

    identity:
        run_id, schema_version, script/profile version, position fingerprint
    times:
        t_run, t0, t_star, t_last, T, UTC instants and New York display offsets
    contracts:
        exact normalized legs, raw identifiers, metadata, multipliers, fixing
    snapshot:
        mode, source, raw quotes, source/reception timestamps, session id, hash
    anchor:
        spot/forward choice, actual cash-index diagnostic, forward fit inputs
    parameters:
        rate/yield/path_IV/leg_IV values, raw units, provenance, effective dates
    accounting:
        entry convention, net cash, maximum profit/loss, targets, cost treatment
    model:
        path dynamics, frozen repricing, exercise/dividend assumptions
    monitoring:
        complete eligible intervals, terminal convention, actual observation grid
    numerical:
        N, seed, bit generator, batch stream scheme, pilot settings, grid results
    results:
        probabilities, hit counts, intervals, first-hit summaries, endpoint values
    validation:
        missing/stale checks, quote/model residuals, domain/pricing convergence
    vendor_alignment:
        per-component verified/approved/unsupported status, reference comparisons
    environment:
        Python/NumPy/SciPy versions, platform, checkpoint identity

Use null plus a reason for a value that was not calculated. Do not insert 0.0 as
a substitute for unavailable probabilities, rates or fees. Serialize unbounded
payoff extrema explicitly, e.g. an infinity flag/string under a documented schema,
rather than nonstandard unquoted JSON Infinity.

No secrets, bearer tokens or full authorization headers may be serialized.

===============================================================================
23. PYTHON COMPONENTS AND END-TO-END PSEUDOCODE
===============================================================================

23.1 Suggested types

Use typed dataclasses for:
    LegInput, ResolvedLeg, ContractSpec, ProductProfile
    SessionInterval, TimeSpec, RawQuote, IVObservation, ParameterObservation
    SnapshotCoverage, MarketSnapshot, EntryAssumption, ModelSpec
    NumericalSettings, TargetRegionSet, ProbabilityResult, RunRecord

Use Decimal for exact strike/cash parsing and contract identity; float64 for
pricing and simulation arrays. Avoid pandas, a database, web server, GUI and
large analytic frameworks in the runtime's critical path.

23.2 Components and responsibilities

CLIController:
    owns the existing input state machine; never collects model inputs mid-trade
TastytradeClient:
    read-only OAuth/HTTP/stream transport, schema normalization, retries
ContractResolver:
    resolves exact returned contracts and root preferences
SessionResolver:
    product/broker calendars, effective dates, closures and expiry cutoffs
SnapshotManager:
    chooses live versus previous-session state and freezes it
SnapshotCache:
    immutable capture, coverage manifests, atomic writes and as-of retrieval
SnapshotValidator:
    timestamps, data quality, parameter timing and whole-position compatibility
ParameterResolver:
    broker/override/quote-fit rate, yield, forward and volatility selection
TradeAccounting:
    entry cash, payoff extrema and fixed target construction
PricingEngine:
    BSM, Black forward and American finite-difference values
TargetRegionBuilder:
    validated threshold regions or direct-pricing fallback
MonteCarloEngine:
    coupled pilot, fixed-N production paths and first-hit counts
EndpointEngine:
    lognormal integration over all payoff/mark intervals
ResultWriter:
    readable terminal results, JSON and checkpoint records
VendorComparator:
    offline comparison fixtures and discrepancy diagnostics; no orders

23.3 Data flow

    def calculate_position(symbol, expiration, leg_inputs, deadline_ny):
        contracts = resolve_exact_contracts(symbol, expiration, leg_inputs)
        product = resolve_supported_product_profile(contracts)
        times = resolve_deadline_fixing_and_cutoffs(product, deadline_ny)
        market_status = resolve_relevant_option_session(product, times.run_time)

        snapshot = choose_and_validate_snapshot(
            contracts, product, market_status, times
        )
        # SnapshotManager may collect raw retained/reference data in several
        # steps, but all values must be reconciled to the final single t0.
        model = resolve_parameters_and_pricing(snapshot, product, globals_block)
        entry = resolve_exact_position_entry(snapshot, TRADE_OVERRIDES)
        accounting = calculate_payoff_extrema_and_targets(contracts, entry)
        validate_initial_quotes_model_basis(snapshot, model, accounting)

        reports = normalize_deadlines_and_targets(times, accounting, globals_block)
        sessions = resolve_all_observation_intervals(snapshot.t0, times.T, product)
        price_workspace = prepare_and_validate_pricer(snapshot, model, reports)
        pilot = run_independent_coupled_grid_pilot(
            snapshot, model, accounting, sessions, reports, price_workspace
        )
        production_grid = freeze_grid_from_pilot(pilot, sessions, reports)
        regions = build_validated_target_regions_or_fallback(
            price_workspace, accounting, production_grid
        )

        hit_results = run_fixed_N_first_hit_simulation(
            snapshot, model, accounting, production_grid, regions
        )
        endpoint_results = integrate_endpoint_probabilities(
            snapshot, model, accounting, reports, price_workspace
        )
        results = combine_and_validate_outputs(hit_results, endpoint_results)
        record = save_nonsecret_reproducibility_record(results, snapshot, model)
        print_primary_probability_and_diagnostics(results, record)
        return results

The actual implementation must handle exceptions around this pipeline at the
correct CLI stage, keep existing user inputs where possible, and never submit an
order to test whether the snapshot is usable.

23.4 Network-independent mathematical core

Everything after snapshot/parameter freeze is a pure numerical computation on
explicit inputs. Unit tests use clearly labeled synthetic fixtures. Integration
tests validate real adapters separately. Production must never auto-fallback to
those synthetic fixtures when live/retained data is missing.

===============================================================================
24. FAILURE HANDLING AND OPERATIONAL BEHAVIOR
===============================================================================

24.1 Structured failures

Use typed exceptions/status codes including:
    AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED
    MARKET_DATA_ENTITLEMENT_REQUIRED
    MALFORMED_BROKER_RESPONSE
    REQUESTED_CONTRACT_NOT_FOUND
    AMBIGUOUS_CONTRACT_ROOT
    UNSUPPORTED_PRODUCT_OR_SETTLEMENT
    UNBOUNDED_MAXIMUM_PROFIT
    UNBOUNDED_RISK_NOT_ALLOWED
    NONPOSITIVE_TARGET
    DEADLINE_NOT_FUTURE / DEADLINE_AFTER_FIXING
    OPTION_SESSION_UNKNOWN
    LIVE_DATA_UNAVAILABLE
    PREVIOUS_SNAPSHOT_UNAVAILABLE
    UNVERIFIED_QUOTE_TIMESTAMP
    INCOHERENT_SNAPSHOT
    AMBIGUOUS_RATE_UNITS / MISSING_RATE
    MISSING_DIVIDEND_OR_CARRY_INPUT
    LIVE_FORWARD_UNAVAILABLE / PARITY_FIT_UNSTABLE
    MISSING_PATH_IV / LEG_IV_NOT_IDENTIFIABLE
    MODEL_BASIS_TOO_LARGE / MODEL_MARKS_INVALID
    PRICER_NOT_CONVERGED / NUMERICAL_CONVERGENCE_UNRESOLVED
    CACHE_CORRUPT / CHECKPOINT_MISMATCH

Differentiate a missing field from a real zero and a closed market from failed
connectivity. Return the relevant stage with the position preserved as appropriate;
do not create a new 'answer these model questions' stage.

24.2 Error content

State what failed, the instrument/time it affects, and the specific allowed
resolution. For a missing normalized rate, point to MARKET_INPUT_OVERRIDES and
its required units/effective dates. For absent cache coverage, list missing
contracts and explain that an earlier capture or valid retained source is needed.
For unsupported roots, show the actual series and the PRODUCT_OVERRIDES mechanism.

Do not recommend entering an arbitrary value just to bypass a diagnostic. Do not
hide a schema failure by catching every exception and returning an empty list.

24.3 Partial numerical work versus invalid inputs

Invalid essential data/model inputs: no probability is emitted.
Resource/cancellation-limited valid simulation: a preliminary sampled result may
be saved with actual completed N/grid and an incomplete status; never relabel it
as the completed 200000-path/converged result.

A write failure must not lose the displayed result; print the real failure and
keep the calculation in memory until leaving the result state. Never print a path
as saved unless the atomic write completed.

===============================================================================
