25. ANDROID/TERMUX, PERFORMANCE AND CHECKPOINTING
===============================================================================

25.1 Runtime environment

Target Python with zoneinfo plus NumPy, SciPy, a lightweight HTTP client and a
compatible WebSocket client. Use tzdata when the system lacks the IANA timezone
records. Validate these imports at startup before the user spends time entering
a trade. Keep the preflight noninteractive except for the existing symbol prompt.

Given the project's earlier Android installation failures, the implementation
must document a tested Termux environment using available platform-appropriate
binary numerical packages rather than repeatedly attempting unsupported source
builds. Do not prescribe an untested pip build as a guaranteed installation path.
Dependency versions are pinned after actual integration testing, not invented in
this planning document.

No full Linux desktop, browser, database, historical-data service or compiled
quant-finance library is required for the proposed baseline.

25.2 Memory and execution cost

Use vectorized NumPy/SciPy operations, small path batches and precomputed target
regions. Never loop in Python over individual Monte Carlo paths. Avoid evaluating
an American tree from scratch for every path and observation.

The fixed 200000 paths with fine monitoring over many GTH sessions can be CPU-
intensive on a phone. Memory budgeting does not guarantee a short runtime. Keep
progress visible by completed batches/steps, and do not silently reduce N, omit
GTH, coarsen a failed grid, or change to an unrelated fast probability formula.

Use surviving-path pruning only under the valid rules in Section 19.2. Bound
reference-chain subscriptions to provider constraints and disclose partial cache
coverage. Pricing-domain expansion and convergence tests have separate resource
budgets recorded in results.

25.3 Reproducible checkpoints

Checkpoint completed independent batches with:
    snapshot/model/accounting/calendar/grid hashes
    exact target/deadline set
    completed path counts and first-hit counters
    bit-generator scheme, batch stream IDs and required RNG state
    numerical settings and code/library versions

Resume only when every identity field matches. A refreshed live quote invalidates
an old checkpoint for that new run. Never combine paths from different snapshots,
IV assumptions, targets or random-stream interpretations.

A cancelled half batch is discarded or resumed with its exact saved state; it is
not counted as a complete batch. Write checkpoints atomically and avoid secrets.

25.4 Phone suspension

The program may collect/cache data only while it is running and receiving events.
If Android suspends collection or the network disconnects, do not backdate a
reconnection snapshot to the missed close. A persisted earlier valid snapshot is
usable only under the prior-session rules and its actual timestamp/coverage.
No scheduled task or always-on recorder is part of the default terminal workflow.

===============================================================================
26. MATHEMATICAL AND INTEGRATION ACCEPTANCE TESTS
===============================================================================

26.1 CLI and globals

- Exact existing symbol/expiration/legs/deadline sequence.
- e at every stage, undo, netting, done, quit and EOF.
- Automatic return to Stage 2 after a result; no fourth prompt.
- Blank/filled DEFAULT_SYMBOL behavior.
- A global entry override affects only its exact position fingerprint.
- New strikes never inherit an old manual credit.
- Optional scalar/list target and day-offset globals normalize correctly.
- Invalid infrastructure inputs do not trigger an interactive model questionnaire.

26.2 Cash accounting

- 767/765, 0.80 credit fixture gives M=80, L=120 and target=40 dollars.
- Credit/debit signs and multipliers are consistent.
- Debit vertical half-profit closing value equals (w+d)/2.
- Duplicate/opposite legs net correctly while undo preserves entry order.
- Payoff extrema include zero, all strikes and tails; no sampled fake maxima.
- Unlimited max profit versus unlimited loss have different diagnostics.
- Net versus gross targets and a one-cent whole-position POP remain distinct.

26.3 Prices and distributions

- BSM put-call parity and intrinsic/zero-volatility limits.
- Spot and forward European formulas agree after the correct carry transform.
- Forward model has zero forward drift; spot model has r-q spot drift.
- Exact GBM moments and analytic terminal probabilities match independent samples.
- American value is at least intrinsic and the corresponding European value
  under matching auxiliary parameters; negative-rate cases are not excluded by
  an unjustified test assumption.
- American pricing converges in space, time, domain and obstacle residual.
- Vendor Greeks IV and quote-inverted IV are not mislabeled as the same source.
- Current model/quote mismatch cannot create a fictitious immediate target hit.
- Common-vol benchmark has the appropriate European martingale checks. Do not
  require arbitrary frozen-leg-IV hybrid marks to be Q_path martingales, nor an
  American option value to obey an unstopped European martingale identity.

26.4 First-hit logic

- A hit followed by a loss remains a success.
- A loss followed by a later hit counts at the correct first-hit time.
- No observation after the chosen deadline enters P50_by_deadline.
- No closed-gap crossing enters closing-only probability.
- Opening observations and expiry settlement are handled exactly once.
- One underlying path drives all legs.
- Multiple intervals/targets are not reduced to one unvalidated barrier.
- Coarse/fine pilots use coupled paths and independent production randomness.
- All probability invariants in Section 20.3 hold in their stated domains.
- POP and target-at-deadline are not accidentally substituted for P50.

26.5 Calendar and prior-snapshot cases

- Ordinary RTH live collection.
- Closed weekday, weekend and exchange holiday.
- Friday cached snapshot used on Saturday with Friday t0 preserved.
- No prior snapshot on a first-ever after-hours launch.
- Cache exists but the newly requested strike is not covered.
- Underlying has advanced after its options close: reject mixed-time construction.
- Valid live GTH SPX with a current forward anchor and stale cash diagnostic.
- Expected GTH is active but one leg/forward/IV is missing: no silent stale fallback.
- Expiring SPXW stop time is enforced independently of product-wide Curb hours.
- Break between session segments, early close and overnight trading-date labels.
- T and t_last are separate; expired cached contracts cannot become new trades.
- Future deadline falling inside a closure remains at its requested time.
- DST duration, nonexistent/ambiguous wall time, and calendar-day offset handling.
- A rate or IV fetched after historical t0 does not enter an as-of snapshot without
  valid effective-date/as-of evidence.

26.6 Transport/storage regression tests

- pagination absent, null, {}, integer, numeric string and malformed values.
- Nonpaginated quote endpoint never triggers the old total-pages int conversion.
- <=100-symbol request batching and exact returned-symbol reconciliation.
- Token refresh, 401/403/429, retry bounds and invalid JSON/content type.
- Correct URL encoding of OCC symbols/spaces and returned stream symbols.
- Stream reception time is not treated as event time.
- Corrupt, incomplete and wrong-session cache records are rejected.
- Atomic writes do not replace a complete snapshot with partial data.
- Fixed snapshot/settings reproduce the result in the pinned environment.
- Checkpoints cannot combine different snapshots or grids.
- Credentials do not appear in any saved result/error log.

26.7 Test artifacts

Unit fixtures are explicitly synthetic. Live-adapter tests use authorized broker
access and saved nonsecret responses. The production script cannot auto-select
a fixture when a live resource is missing. No actual trade is required for any
test in this plan.

===============================================================================
27. TASTYTRADE COMPARISON PROTOCOL
===============================================================================

27.1 Purpose

Measure compatibility with the platform; do not turn observed numerical similarity
into a claim of internal source-code identity. The same-data requirement covers
observable inputs. Hidden vendor input selection remains part of the unverified
model status until established independently.

27.2 Comparison records

For each reference capture:
- Actual platform/engine version if visible and capture time.
- Underlying, precise option root, expiration, quantities and strike/right list.
- Displayed entry/order-ticket price and max profit.
- Observable underlying/option prices, marks, IV data and timestamps.
- Displayed P50 and applicable POP, rounding precision and platform screen context.
- Trading session, eligible-session preference and costs if visible.

Store reference values in a development comparison record or exact-position
TRADE_OVERRIDES metadata, not as a new normal runtime input stage. Do not invent a
public P50 endpoint or scrape undocumented internal services to automate this.

27.3 Comparison sequence

First use t_star = T and compare the two expiry conventions separately. Test
credit/debit verticals and multi-leg bounded-risk positions across moneyness and
expirations, with coherent snapshots. Then test live/prior-session and SPX GTH
behavior. Compare intermediate cumulative P50 points only if a current platform
feature actually exposes them and its definition is confirmed.

Investigate discrepancies in this order:
    contract/fixing identity
    entry and dollar target
    snapshot timing and mark basis
    spot versus forward anchor
    rate/carry/IV selection and units
    pricing and future IV treatment
    eligible observations and terminal inclusion
    numerical grid, sampling and rounding

Do not optimize arbitrary parameters just to fit a few displayed percentages.
Do not add historical returns/backtesting merely to tune to tastytrade.

27.4 Output agreement criterion

Exact input/accounting matches are deterministic tests. Numerical comparison is
an uncertainty-aware compatibility check. Use known display rounding, our actual
sampling error, pricing/grid sensitivity and, where measurable, the platform's
own repeat variation under sufficiently unchanged inputs.

A possible diagnostic comparison band combines those measured errors, but no
universal percentage-point tolerance is called proof of equivalence. Repeated
identical platform displays can reflect caching or a deterministic seed rather
than zero simulation error. Repeated changing markets are not independent samples
of the same frozen P50 problem.

Do not round our internal estimate to a whole percent merely to manufacture a
match. Save full precision and present a readable percentage next to uncertainty.

27.5 Future vendor-confirmed changes

If a current official specification or directly confirmed behavior establishes a
previously unknown convention, implement it as a versioned vendor profile with
regression tests and a clear change record. Retain old approved-fallback records
for reproducibility. Do not alter completed runs in place.

===============================================================================
28. IMPLEMENTATION ORDER AND DEFINITION OF DONE
===============================================================================

Phase A - Core contracts and configuration
Implement typed inputs, the existing CLI state machine, globals, exact-position
overrides, cash/payoff accounting and nonsecret record schemas.

Phase B - Data adapters and sessions
Implement/test OAuth, quote parsing, null-pagination handling, exact contracts,
product/session rules, event timestamps and live/prior snapshot selection.

Phase C - Snapshot cache and parameter resolution
Implement atomic coverage-aware cache, rate/units/effective-time resolution,
yield approximation, European forward fit and current/as-of IV selection.

Phase D - Pricing and target regions
Implement BSM/Black forward, the American solver, IV inversion, model-basis guards,
all target regions and numerical domain/price validation.

Phase E - Probability engine
Implement nested-grid pilots, independent fixed-N simulation, first-hit tracking,
separate terminal settlement and analytical endpoint diagnostics.

Phase F - Operations
Add terminal progress, actual numerical status, saved records, checkpoint/resume,
error routing and Android memory controls. Preserve the same input stages.

Phase G - Validation
Run unit/integration/calendar tests and then actual platform compatibility tests
where matching reference data are available. Record failures and evidence gaps
rather than filling in unobserved vendor values.

Definition of done for the implemented approved-fallback calculator:
- The three trade-entry stages operate without additional required prompts.
- Extra manual inputs are optional top-of-script globals.
- Eligible live extended-hours markets use live coherent data.
- Closed markets use actual previous-session data with preserved t0, when available.
- Missing historical snapshot data cannot be fabricated.
- The main result is the requested first-hit probability with actual T in repricing.
- All U1-U19 choices are implemented and labeled consistently.
- Numerical, source-quality and hybrid-model qualifications remain visible.
- Cash accounting, probability invariants, calendar logic and parser regressions pass.
- Saved records reproduce the run and contain no credentials.
- Exact tastytrade equivalence is not claimed without actual supporting evidence.

This final plan does not require another round of model questions. Routine
implementation tests may reveal missing broker data or unsupported products;
their error/fallback policies are already defined above.

===============================================================================
29. U1-U19 FINAL DECISION REGISTER
===============================================================================

All nineteen design decisions are closed by the user's approval. 'Closed' means
there is an implementation rule, not that the vendor's internal detail became
public. The choices below remain visible in the versioned profile.

U1 - Probability measure and dynamics
Decision: constant-parameter risk-neutral-style GBM for spot with drift r-q;
zero drift for the equivalent terminal-forward state. No physical drift fit.
Qualification: Q_path plus frozen-leg marks is not generally a coherent full-market
risk-neutral valuation model. Sections 01, 11 and 15.

U2 - Path IV selection
Decision: matching as-of-valid expiration IV, otherwise a validated current ATM
quote fit for the correct expiration and settlement. Never IV rank/percentile or
an unrelated maturity. Section 12.

U3 - Future IV behavior
Decision: freeze each leg's current compatible IV and the path IV. Reprice the
actual options with those frozen values; no invented stochastic/skew dynamics.
Quote-invert a leg IV when needed under the selected pricer, preserving provenance.
Reject material invalid model marks instead of silently changing models.
Sections 09 and 12.

U4 - Option-pricing algorithm
Decision: BSM/Black forward for European contracts; converged exercise-aware
finite differences for supported American ETF options. No automatic European
approximation for American contracts. This is not verified tastytrade code.
Section 16.

U5 - Rates, dividends and carry
Decision: automatically try the identified broker risk-free-rate source with a
validated unit/as-of contract; otherwise validated European quote inference or
scoped normalized globals. Use sourced continuous dividend-yield approximation
for spot ETF models; forward index models need no separate q. Missing required
inputs produce an error, not an invented rate or zero dividend. Section 10.

U6 - Time convention
Decision: ACT/365F using actual elapsed UTC seconds and New York display/input.
Record any assumed vendor-IV variance clock. Include nights/weekends in model
time while monitoring is separately session restricted. Sections 06 and 15.

U7 - Monte Carlo count and randomness
Decision: 200000 independent production paths, named PCG64/SeedSequence scheme,
fixed recorded seed, independent pilot and uncertainty reporting. Do not claim to
reproduce a hidden vendor random-number realization. Section 19.

U8 - Profit-hit observation frequency
Decision: 60-second initial grid, independently tested nested refinements down to
1.875 seconds within limits; use the chosen refined grid in production. No bridge
correction in the main result. Report unresolved convergence honestly. Sections
18 and 19.

U9 - Closed-market profit observations
Decision: main closing-hit event only uses verified tradable option sessions,
including eligible extended sessions. Continuous theoretical marks may appear as
endpoint/validation diagnostics, never disguised as executable hits. Section 18.

U10 - Terminal settlement
Decision: preserve a closing-only result and a separately labeled expiry reference
including terminal payoff. An exact-expiry primary deadline uses that disclosed
terminal reference convention. Never call settlement an available closing trade.
Sections 14 and 18.

U11 - Entry premium and target basis
Decision: exact-position net order/entry override when supplied; otherwise the
coherent snapshot's net leg midpoint as hypothetical entry. Fixed original cash
for existing positions. No automatic zeroing of genuine or model-basis P&L.
Sections 04, 09 and 13.

U12 - Fees and slippage
Decision: gross primary P50; optional exact-position fixed-cost net closing
probability when costs are supplied. No assumed future fills/slippage, no hidden
expiry fee convention and no required fee prompt. Section 13.

U13 - POP reproduction
Decision: independently compute model terminal POP with the documented applicable
$0.01 position threshold and retain a strict-positive diagnostic. Do not copy a
portfolio beta-weighted ePOP distribution into this single-position engine or
claim full vendor POP identity. Sections 14, 20 and 27.

U14 - First-ever after-hours launch
Decision: timestamp-proven retained broker state first, valid same-session cache
as another candidate; choose the newest complete valid candidate. If neither
exists, explain that no valid snapshot is available. Caching is not retroactive.
Sections 07 and 08.

U15 - Previous-session time origin
Decision: start from the actual previous snapshot t0, not the later launch clock.
The result is explicitly as-of-t0, including its modeled elapsed closed periods,
not newly conditioned on subsequent real-world information. Section 08.

U16 - Previous-session snapshot quality
Decision: latest complete compatible as-of state within the preceding eligible
session, using declared engineering age/dispersion limits at t0 and recording its
coverage. Do not manufacture an exact closing-second snapshot. Sections 08 and 09.

U17 - SPX GTH underlying reference
Decision: validated European put-call forward from current matching option pairs,
with an appropriate discount factor; simulate/reprice in forward coordinates.
Keep stale cash-index data diagnostic only. If the fit fails, no false live P50.
Section 11.

U18 - Active market with missing live inputs
Decision: after bounded collection/retry, report live-data unavailability. Never
silently replace expected live SPX/GTH inputs with a prior-session number. Unsupported
products require their own adapter. Sections 02 and 07.

U19 - Meaning of a tastytrade match
Decision: test exact observable inputs, model specification and numerical agreement
separately. Compare within measured numerical/quote/rounding uncertainty and preserve
NOT VERIFIED until sufficient methodology evidence exists. Similar rounded numbers
are compatibility evidence, not proof of an identical engine. Section 27.

===============================================================================
30. INTERNAL SEMANTICS AND DATA-ADAPTER CONTRACT
===============================================================================

D30 is part of this file. The numerical implementation is SRC-D Sections 13-21
plus P50-R. P50 measures a first eligible profit-target hit; POP measures a
terminal profit event. A matching rounded number is not evidence of an identical
proprietary implementation. P50-R gives the actual functions and convergence
rules to implement. Keep $0.01 total-position terminal POP distinct from the
$0.01-per-share exercise moneyness threshold in APP Section 13.

For replay, consume the canonical MarketSnapshot produced by SRC-B/C and APP
Sections 05-08. It supplies exact contract identities, as-of bid/ask, compatible
IVs, authoritative underlying spot, rates/carry, contract clocks, quote times
and quality status. All units, timestamp validation, normalization, fallbacks
and failure states are specified in this file. The mathematical core accepts
these normalized records and never requests a website, live account, or script.

For a conditional read-only market adapter, distinguish bid, ask, computed mid,
vendor mark, last trade and previous close; do not substitute one for another.
Batch at most 100 instrument symbols per quote request, follow returned pagination
until complete, and match every response by instrument type and exact symbol.
Obtain Quote and Greeks through a market-data channel if that adapter is enabled;
an account-event channel is not quote data. Preserve original event time,
receive time, sequence/revision, delayed flag and permissions separately.
Expiration-specific IV is a distinct field from IV rank, percentile and index.
A quoted risk-free-rate field is a rate input proxy, with explicit units and
compounding, not evidence of a platform's internal P50 rate. Missing normalization
metadata requires a dated normalized override or MISSING_RATE; never guess scale.
Dividend amount and frequency require validated units and timing; use the
continuous-yield equations in Section 10 or an explicit dated q override.

Contract metadata must distinguish exercise style, physical/cash delivery,
multiplier, deliverable, administrative expiry, trading stop and economic fixing.
AM/PM is a fixing convention, not the physical/cash settlement flag. SPX-type
European cash-settled contracts use the forward branch and their supplied
contract-specific windows; QQQ uses the American branch and APP-C's QQQ profile.
An active underlying session does not make its options executable. Product,
date and broker eligibility are separate fields; use the intersection with the
declared monitoring calendar. Unsupported optional product/session adapters
return a typed status. They do not change the QQQ core's specified behavior.

The optional adapter boundary is a normalized SnapshotProvider interface,
specified in P50-R and APP Section 19. The desktop application implements its
historical provider locally. Conditional live resources mentioned in Section 05
are transport identifiers, not reading assignments and not core dependencies.
Authentication/permission failures affect that optional provider only; never
silently substitute stale data for an active-session request.

===============================================================================
31. IN-FILE REQUIREMENT INTEGRATION
===============================================================================

SRC-A supplies the repeated three-stage terminal navigation/reporting intent.
The newer expiration/legs/New-York-deadline flow in Section 03 is the binding
compatibility CLI. Additional parameters are noninteractive configuration fields.
SRC-B and SRC-C supply the historical QQQ runtime-data contracts. APP's historical
source and replay-time decisions supersede the older live-only source restriction.
SRC-E supplies persistent campaign targets, risk limits and amortized query rules.
P50-R and P50-M supply all numerical and managed-policy implementation details.
The U1-U19 decisions are written in full in Section 29; no conversation or earlier
revision is needed to recover them. Retain every compatible numerical/quality/
reporting invariant and implement the explicit APP integration decisions.

END OF P50 DEADLINE SPECIFICATION

END SRC-D

===============================================================================
BEGIN P50-R — NUMERICAL REFERENCE IMPLEMENTATION
===============================================================================

===============================================================================
P50 REFERENCE ENGINE: COMPLETE IN-FILE TECHNICAL SPECIFICATION
Internal section identifiers: P50-R01 through P50-R23
===============================================================================

P50-R01. AUTHORITATIVE BEHAVIOR AND SCOPE

This section supplies the executable-level behavior behind every instruction to
price an option, calculate P50, construct a target region, invert an IV, compare
with a reference engine, or preserve the P50 workflow. It is an implementation
specification to build from this document. No pre-existing script, source-code
archive, old output file, online article, or proprietary implementation is a
prerequisite. Expose the resulting command-line controller as
  python -m qqq_sim p50-reference
This command denotes a module to implement here, not a file to obtain elsewhere.

Implement one mathematical reference engine and expose it through both the
standalone command-line controller and the application's offline build/test
services. The normal desktop query path uses the validated model pack described
in P50-M; it must not invoke this expensive reference calculation as an automatic
fallback. An explicit offline build, numerical test, or user-selected reference
analysis may invoke it.

The baseline model is constant-rate, continuous-dividend-yield GBM with frozen
individual option IVs. American options use an optimal-exercise numerical pricer.
Continuous q does not mean discrete cash-dividend jumps. Do not add a scheduled
cash-dividend subtraction to this baseline. Actual portfolio assignment,
financing, orders, margin and campaign management are separate account/policy
mechanisms; they must not be silently inserted into the unchanged-position HOLD
probability event.

The mathematical profile is a specified implementation choice. It is not a claim
to reproduce the internal source code or unobserved conventions of any broker.
The P50 reference produced from this section replaces any requirement to inspect,
import, preserve, optimize, or regression-test an unavailable earlier script.
Use the analytical and numerical fixtures in P50-R21 as the initial reference
corpus. Test subsequent changes against frozen results produced by the implemented
and validated engine, with complete recorded inputs and numerical settings.

P50-R02. INPUT CONTRACT AND FUNCTION BOUNDARIES

A numerical job consumes the following immutable values; none is fetched during
pricing or simulation:

  ReferenceJob:
    job_schema = "P50_REFERENCE_V1"
    snapshot_id, snapshot_hash, calculation_mode
    t0_utc_us, deadline_utc_us, fixing_utc_us
    eligible_intervals = sorted disjoint [open_utc_us, close_utc_us) pairs
    last_trading_utc_us, calendar_profile_id
    state_kind = "SPOT" or "TERMINAL_FORWARD"
    x0 > 0
    rate_cc, dividend_yield_cc, sigma_path
    signed_legs[]
    entry_cash_usd, target_usd, target_basis
    optional_targets_usd[], reporting_times_utc_us[]
    parameter/quote/contract provenance and known-at times
    model_profile, numerical_settings, software/array-library versions

Each signed leg has exact contract_id, right in {CALL,PUT}, Decimal strike,
signed integer quantity, positive integer multiplier, fixing time, exercise
style, deliverable identity, frozen annual-decimal sigma, and optional normalized
bid/ask/IV observations. Reject fractional contracts, bool-as-number, nonfinite
values, incompatible deliverables, or mixed fixing times in this HOLD profile.
An absent normalized input is a data error, not an invitation to choose a number.

Core pure interfaces:

  normalize_job(raw) -> ReferenceJob | typed input error
  payoff_extrema(legs, entry_cash) -> {max_profit, max_loss, tail_slopes}
  resolve_parameters(snapshot, parameter_records) -> FrozenModel
  european_price(kind, S_or_F, K, tau, r, q, sigma) -> price_per_unit
  american_price_slices(model, contract, requested_times, domain) -> GridSlices
  invert_iv(contract, price, model_without_iv) -> IVResult
  build_regions(job, price_slices, times, targets) -> RegionCollection
  run_monitoring_pilot(job, regions) -> PilotDecision
  run_production(job, regions, fixed_grid, settings) -> FirstHitCounts
  endpoint_probability(job, time, threshold, basis) -> EndpointResult
  calculate_reference(job) -> ReferenceResult

All monetary accounting is in USD; option prices are per quoted unit. Convert
between them only through signed quantity times multiplier. Strike parsing and
contract equality use Decimal; numerical arrays use float64. Cash ledgers use
integer cents or Decimal. Numerical model P&L is not rounded to cents before the
threshold comparison; only displayed amounts are rounded.

P50-R03. CLOCKS, ELIGIBLE OBSERVATIONS AND FIXING

One microsecond is the canonical timestamp resolution. Parse New York inputs
with America/New_York, validate that each local time maps to exactly one UTC
instant, and reject nonexistent or ambiguous local times. Compute all elapsed
time from UTC integer microseconds:

  year_fraction(a,b) = (b_utc_us - a_utc_us) / 31,536,000,000,000.

The divisor is 365*24*60*60*1,000,000. Calendar-day reporting offsets preserve the
New York wall-clock time and must be resolved again across DST. They are not
252-trading-day fractions or an unconditional 86,400-second addition.

Keep these clocks separate:
  run time: actual computer clock, for audit;
  t0: coherent valuation snapshot, origin of the probability calculation;
  deadline: requested reporting cutoff;
  last trading: contract's exclusive cutoff for closing observations;
  T: economic option payoff/fixing time;
  payment/settlement date: account-ledger timing, not option maturity.

Historical replay validates deadlines against replay t0. It must not reject a
historical trade because the computer's date is later. The command-line live
controller can additionally require a deadline later than its actual run time.
In both cases require t0 < deadline <= T for a new prospective HOLD request.
An exact t0 diagnostic is permitted for tests and already-open-position analysis.

For resolution delta in {60,30,15,7.5,3.75,1.875} seconds, form integer-microsecond
grid times aligned to the Unix epoch. For each eligible interval [a,b), include:
  a when a >= t0;
  every aligned grid time in [max(a,t0),b);
  t0 when t0 is eligible;
  each explicitly requested reporting time that lies in the interval;
  b-1 microsecond as the declared last representable pre-close observation,
    unless it is earlier than a or t0.
Deduplicate times, sort, and retain their event-role flags. The one-microsecond
pre-close observation is a numerical monitoring convention; it does not assert
that historical quote data have that precision or that an order can execute at
that time. A close at b itself is ineligible under the half-open interval rule.
If pricing or a market model later includes jumps at a boundary, their event
order must be explicit; this baseline's GBM and continuous-q price process do
not add a cash jump at market close.

Remove all ordinary closing observations at/after the exclusive last-trading
cutoff or at/after T.
Include T as a distinct terminal-payoff event, never as an invented post-cutoff
closing trade. Session closes and actual fixing are separate even when their
clock labels coincide. The product/calendar inputs contain all supported
holidays, early closes, session intervals and contract cutoffs; this engine does
not infer them from weekday arithmetic or require another document to define
how the supplied intervals are interpreted.

Build one master grid through T, including all requested deadlines, before
sampling. A deadline inside a closed interval adds no closing observation.
It can be included as a propagation/endpoint time without becoming a hit check.
Model variance and time decay continue across every elapsed second of closures.

P50-R04. CASH, PAYOFF EXTREMA AND ORIGINAL TARGET

For signed option quantities n_i, multipliers m_i, entry premiums e_i:

  C = -sum_i n_i*m_i*e_i
  V(t,S) = sum_i n_i*m_i*v_i(t,S)
  Pi(t,S) = C + V(t,S)
  G(S) = sum_i n_i*m_i*max(sign_i*(S-K_i),0),
    sign_i=+1 for calls and -1 for puts.

A supplied exact net entry amount replaces C; do not manufacture per-leg fills
from a net override. For an ongoing campaign use its cumulative cashflow basis
and retained exposures, as defined by the ledger instructions, while keeping its
original target H0. Do not reset H0 after a roll or replace it with half of the
new position's maximum profit.

For a new same-fixing option position, evaluate C+G(S) at S=0 and at every distinct
strike. Between consecutive strikes the payoff is affine, so there is no other
finite extremum. Above the largest strike its slope is
  b_inf = sum_{call legs} n_i*m_i.
If b_inf>0, maximum profit is +infinity. If b_inf<0, loss is unbounded. If it is
zero, the final tail value is the value at the largest strike. The low boundary
S=0 is included; negative spot is outside the domain. Then
  M = sup_{S>=0}(C+G(S)),
  L = max(0,-inf_{S>=0}(C+G(S))).
For a percentage-of-max-profit target require finite M>0 and set H0=0.50*M.
The default new-trade gate additionally requires finite L. Serialize infinity
with an explicit flag/string, never as a fabricated large finite maximum.

If a separately supported stock exposure is included in an existing campaign,
add shares*S to V and G and shares to b_inf. Do not use this extension to certify
mixed-expiry, adjusted-deliverable or assignment-lifecycle positions that the
selected model pack does not support.

A hypothetical midpoint trade uses e_i=(bid_i+ask_i)/2. Separate the two quantities:
  Pi_quote_now = C + sum_i n_i*m_i*mid_i,
  residual_model = sum_i n_i*m_i*(model_i-mid_i).
An actual fill has spread cost; an existing position has real prior P&L. Neither
should be mistaken for model calibration error. Reject a material model residual;
never subtract it as a permanent future P&L offset or refit C to erase a loss.

Gross HOLD uses Pi and H0. If a fixed total round-trip cost F is explicitly
supplied for a separate net target, use Pi_net=Pi-F, M_net=M-F and
H_net=0.50*M_net, requiring M_net>0. A net campaign with frozen original target
uses its original declared basis instead. Closing costs and terminal settlement
costs are separate conventions; a generic closing charge must not be applied at
T without an explicitly supplied terminal-cost rule.

P50-R05. PROBABILITY EVENTS AND REQUIRED OUTPUTS

For the finite eligible monitoring set A and frozen target H:

  H_close = first t in A at or after t0 with Pi(t,X_t)>=H,
            or +infinity when no such observation occurs.
  p_close(d) = Q_path(H_close<=d | frozen snapshot).
  J_T = 1{C+G(X_T)>=H}.
  p_reference(T) = Q_path(H_close<=T or J_T=1).
  p_settlement_only = Q_path(H_close>T and J_T=1).
  p_endpoint(d) = Q_path(Pi(d,X_d)>=H).
  pop_cent(T) = Q_path(C+G(X_T)>=0.01 USD).
  pop_strict(T) = Q_path(C+G(X_T)>0).

At deadline d<T the headline result is p_close(d). At d=T the declared headline
reference includes terminal payoff, and the closing-only companion is displayed
beside it. A market-closed endpoint diagnostic is a theoretical mark, not an
eligible close. A threshold touch followed by a loss remains one success.
A position that lost first and subsequently touched the target also counts.
The HOLD event does not incorporate an assumed stop, roll, assignment or forced
liquidation. Managed realized success is a different P50-M event.

At an eligible t0 a genuine already-achieved model threshold gives an immediate
first hit. Do not apply this shortcut when the current model/quote basis fails
validation. The estimate is conditional from t0 and does not reconstruct whether
a trade touched its target earlier in its actual history.

Always return requested-deadline P50; closing-only through last trading;
terminal-inclusive reference; settlement-only increment; exact-deadline target
probability; terminal cent-threshold POP; strict-positive POP; target basis and
amount; numerical/grid status; model assumptions; and actual uncertainty fields.
Use null plus a reason for unavailable outputs. Never use zero to mean missing.

P50-R06. PARAMETER RECORDS, UNITS AND AS-OF RESOLUTION

The mathematical engine accepts normalized local parameter records rather than
requiring a developer to discover a raw broker field's meaning. A parameter
record contains name, raw_value, raw_unit, compounding, tenor/day-count if needed,
normalized_value, effective_from/to, known_at, source_identifier, retrieval_time,
and applicable symbol/expiry/profile. All instructions for normalization are:

  decimal annual fraction: x;
  annual percent: x/100;
  annual basis points: x/10000;
  continuously compounded annual rate: r_cc=x after scale normalization;
  effective annual rate: r_cc=log1p(x), requiring x>-1;
  nominal annual rate compounded m times/year:
       r_cc=m*log1p(x/m), integer m>0 and 1+x/m>0;
  simple quoted-tenor rate:
       D=1/(1+x*tau_quote), requiring positive denominator;
       r_cc=-log(D)/tau_model for the same dated interval;
  discount factor D for [a,b]: r_cc=-log(D)/year_fraction(a,b), D>0, b>a.

Raw annualization of IV must be supplied or explicitly assigned the model's
ACT/365F interpretation. IV percent is divided by 100 once; annual decimal IV
is unchanged. Do not infer scale from a value's magnitude or the fact that it
was serialized as a decimal string. No implicit sqrt(365/252) rescaling is used.
If a different known variance clock is supplied, preserve its original integrated
variance for the actual interval before converting to sigma_model by
sigma_model=sqrt(integrated_variance/tau_model). Otherwise the recorded
ACT/365F assumption applies, without claiming it is a verified vendor convention.

For historical QQQ choose an applicable deliberately supplied dated override,
otherwise a compatible known-at-or-before-t0 normalized record; otherwise fail
with MISSING_RATE or MISSING_DIVIDEND_OR_CARRY_INPUT. A current observation cannot
be assigned a past known_at. Do not infer an ETF rate from European put-call
parity, because the supported ETF options are American.

The continuous-q fallback from a known regular cash dividend amount A and known
annual payment frequency f is q_proxy=A*f/S0. It is explicitly a dividend-yield
proxy, not a forward cash schedule. A supplied normalized q is treated as a
continuous yield directly. Zero q is valid only as an explicit documented model
input/assumption; missing q is not zero. No second dividend subtraction occurs.
The same r,q govern the spot-path drift and each auxiliary option pricer.

For an optional live-data adapter, the adapter must produce this same normalized
record contract and immutable quote/contract snapshots. The offline simulator
needs no live API credentials, proprietary rate field, or outside API manual.
If a live payload does not explicitly establish its units or timestamps, that
adapter rejects the field and can use an already normalized dated override;
it must not block implementation of the mathematical or historical engines.

P50-R07. FORWARD FIT AND IDENTIFIABILITY FOR EUROPEAN ADAPTERS

This is a complete optional European-index adapter, not a substitute for QQQ's
American carry treatment. Use at least five same-fixing, same-settlement call/put
pairs with valid synchronized bid/ask. For pair j at strike K_j define
  y_j=(C_bid+C_ask-P_bid-P_ask)/2,
  lo_j=C_bid-P_ask,
  hi_j=C_ask-P_bid,
  h_j=max((hi_j-lo_j)/2, tick/2).
All selected pairs must satisfy the job's freshness/dispersion rules.

When r is known, D=exp(-r*tau). Each pair implies
  F_j=K_j+y_j/D,
  F_low_j=K_j+lo_j/D,
  F_high_j=K_j+hi_j/D.
Compute the interval intersection [max F_low_j,min F_high_j]. If it is nonempty,
choose the weighted least-squares mean of F_j with weights 1/(h_j/D)^2, projected
onto that intersection. Projection here fits an observed uncertainty interval;
it is not a clamp on an invalid portfolio price. If the intersection is empty,
return PARITY_FIT_UNSTABLE rather than deleting pairs until they agree. Remove
only observations that fail objective quote/timing validation before the fit.
Require F>0. Repeat fits omitting one pair at a time and retain their span;
these diagnostic leave-one-out fits permit four pairs when the original has
exactly five. The final accepted production fit still requires all five or more.

When r is missing, solve a constrained two-variable weighted least-squares
problem for A,B with lo_j<=A-B*K_j<=hi_j, B>0, A>0. To make the problem bounded,
the supported fallback domain explicitly has -0.10<=r_cc<=0.30; translate these
to exp(-0.30*tau)<=B<=exp(0.10*tau). This is an engineering model domain, not an
assertion about possible market rates. Outside-domain inputs require an explicit
normalized rate and a separately qualified profile. Set F=A/B and r=-log(B)/tau.

Solve the two-variable quadratic objective by enumerating the unconstrained
normal-equation solution, each feasible boundary projection, and each intersection
of two boundary lines, then select the feasible point with minimum objective;
ties use lexicographic (B,A). For every candidate, check every inequality with a
scaled floating-point tolerance. If the feasible set is empty, fail. Obtain B's
minimum/maximum from the feasible polygon's vertices. Obtain F's bounds from
A/B at those vertices, which suffices for this positive-denominator polygon.

Identifiability gates for the fallback are deliberately explicit: at least five
distinct strikes; strike span at least 1% of fitted F; implied-r feasible width
<=0.0025 annual decimal; relative F feasible width <=0.001; leave-one-out relative
F span <=0.001. These are initial numerical-quality thresholds, not broker
conventions. If a gate fails, require a normalized applicable rate rather than
printing a falsely precise inferred rate. Record bounds, residuals, included
quotes, scaling and gate outcomes. Frozen-model sensitivity to the admitted
parameter interval remains a separate model-input uncertainty diagnostic.

In forward coordinates Y=F(t,T), use zero drift and Black forward prices below.
Never put F into a spot formula and charge carry a second time.

P50-R08. EUROPEAN FORMULAS AND DETERMINISTIC LIMITS

Use Phi(x)=0.5*erfc(-x/sqrt(2)), barPhi(x)=0.5*erfc(x/sqrt(2)), and
phi(x)=exp(-x*x/2)/sqrt(2*pi). Phi_inverse(p) denotes scipy.special.ndtri(p)
for0<p<1; validate that range before calling it. Use barPhi directly instead of
subtracting Phi from1 in extreme positive tails. For S,K>0,
tau>0 and sigma>0:

  d1=(log(S/K)+(r-q+sigma^2/2)*tau)/(sigma*sqrt(tau)),
  d2=d1-sigma*sqrt(tau),
  C=S*exp(-q*tau)*Phi(d1)-K*exp(-r*tau)*Phi(d2),
  P=K*exp(-r*tau)*Phi(-d2)-S*exp(-q*tau)*Phi(-d1).

In the forward branch replace these with
  d1=(log(Y/K)+sigma^2*tau/2)/(sigma*sqrt(tau)), d2=d1-sigma*sqrt(tau),
  C=exp(-r*tau)*(Y*Phi(d1)-K*Phi(d2)),
  P=exp(-r*tau)*(K*Phi(-d2)-Y*Phi(-d1)).
At tau=0 use intrinsic. At sigma=0 use
  C=max(S*exp(-q*tau)-K*exp(-r*tau),0),
  P=max(K*exp(-r*tau)-S*exp(-q*tau),0),
or exp(-r*tau)*intrinsic(Y) for the forward branch. Never divide by zero.
For deep ITM options, evaluate the OTM counterpart and use put-call parity where
it avoids subtractive cancellation. Store parity and sign residuals; a materially
negative price is a numerical error, not a value to clamp silently to zero.

An American option with sigma=0 is an optimal deterministic stopping problem.
Evaluate u=0, u=tau and any interior stationary point
  u=log(r*K/(q*S))/(r-q)
when r,q are nonzero, r!=q, the ratio is positive and 0<u<tau. The call candidate
value is max(S*exp(-q*u)-K*exp(-r*u),0); the put candidate is its negation before
max with zero. The largest candidate is the exact zero-volatility American
price. If r=q or either coefficient vanishes, endpoints suffice. This avoids
incorrectly using the European zero-volatility limit for an exercisable option.

P50-R09. AMERICAN FINITE-DIFFERENCE GRID AND GENERATOR

For each leg's frozen sigma>0 solve the optimal-exercise problem backward from
its actual fixing T, with payoff g(S), in log spot x=log(S/K_ref). K_ref can be
that leg's strike or one fixed positive shared scale; it is recorded. Let
  a=sigma^2/2,
  mu=r-q-sigma^2/2,
  Lf=a*f_xx+mu*f_x.
The pricing continuation equation is v_t+Lv-r*v=0 with v>=g. The probability
solver in P50-R19 has no -r*v term; do not confuse the two equations.

Start with 1601 uniformly spaced x nodes. From the outset include current spot
and every strike as independent interpolation/validation points, even when
their log coordinates do not coincide with a grid node. Refine until their
interpolation and kink errors pass. A uniform grid cannot generally include all
arbitrary strikes exactly; do not claim that it does.

Choose initial log-domain padding from current state, all strikes, the complete
horizon, path sigma and all leg sigmas:
  sigma_max=max(sigma_path, all positive leg sigmas),
  H=year_fraction(t0,T),
  pad=max(0.50, 8*sigma_max*sqrt(H)+abs(r-q-sigma_max^2/2)*H).
Set lower=min(log(X0/K_ref),log(K_i/K_ref) for all i)-pad and
upper=max(log(X0/K_ref),log(K_i/K_ref) for all i)+pad. This is a starting
domain only. Apply the domain and boundary-bracketing tests below.

For log spacing h define central coefficients
  l=a/h^2-mu/(2*h), u=a/h^2+mu/(2*h).
Use them only when l>=0 and u>=0. Otherwise use the monotone one-sided generator
  l=a/h^2+max(-mu,0)/h,
  u=a/h^2+max(mu,0)/h.
Then L_h f_j=l*f_{j-1}-(l+u)*f_j+u*f_{j+1}.
Positive generator coefficients are mandatory; do not repair negative transition
weights by clipping them after construction.

For backward step dt>0 in model years, with v_next the known later-time slice,
  A=I-dt*(L_h-r*I),
  A_lower=-dt*l,
  A_diag=1+dt*(l+u+r),
  A_upper=-dt*u.
Move known boundary contributions to the right-hand side b. If r<0, enforce
1+dt*r>=0.75 by subdividing the time step. This sufficient strict diagonal-
dominance condition keeps the tridiagonal continuation matrix an M-matrix.
Negative interest rates are not automatically rejected.

The time mesh includes every requested valuation/monitoring time. Between those
times start with an internal maximum step of 3600 elapsed seconds, reduce to
60 seconds within the last 24 elapsed hours, and refine as required. If the
observation spacing is finer, its exact times already require finer steps.
Nights and weekends still receive internal pricing steps; a closed market does
not remove optimal exercise or time decay from the numerical value problem.

Do not keep a complete time-by-spot-by-leg tensor in RAM. Walk backward in common
time order, retaining only current/next lower/upper price slices for each leg.
At each requested observation time, combine the active leg slices on the common
validated spot knots, extract all target intervals/uncertainty bands, and append
those compact records to immutable chronological blocks. Reverse/index their
order for forward simulation; do not rerun pricing in every path shard. Store
only endpoint/quote-calibration slices that are explicitly needed. Space/time
refinement runs can be performed sequentially and compared on saved validation
points. If even active slices or interval records exceed the working budget,
use bounded disk-backed blocks and memory-mapped read-only arrays with content
hashes and explicit time/domain/model identifiers. A lazy GridSlices handle can
refer to these blocks; its name does not require all slices resident. Set a
per-block target of8 MiB and measure actual peak RAM/disk use. Reaching a resource
cap causes a checkpoint/incomplete status, never coarser monitoring, fewer paths,
lower precision, or discarded intervals. The256 MiB standalone target and larger
desktop budgets therefore do not alter the mathematical job.

P50-R10. AMERICAN COMPLEMENTARITY SOLVE AND BOUNDARIES

At every backward step solve, componentwise on interior nodes,
  v>=g,
  A*v-b>=0,
  min(v-g,A*v-b)=0.
Use policy iteration, with no post-solve payoff clamp:
  1. Initialize u from the later-time solution, or the last converged warm start.
  2. Calculate exercise residual e=u-g and continuation residual c=A*u-b.
  3. For each row choose exercise when e<=c; ties choose exercise. Replace that
     row with an identity row and RHS g. Other rows retain A and b.
  4. Solve the resulting tridiagonal system in float64. The Thomas algorithm or
     compiled banded solve is allowed; verify pivot/conditioning diagnostics.
  5. Recompute both residuals. Stop only when max(-e,0), max(-c,0), and
     max(abs(min(e,c))) are all within the prescribed absolute/relative residual
     tolerance. An unchanged policy alone is insufficient if residuals fail.
  6. Otherwise repeat, up to 100 policy iterations. On failure halve this time
     step and retry from the previous converged slice, at most eight halvings.
     A further failure returns PRICER_NOT_CONVERGED.

The residual tolerance per quoted unit is min(1e-9,eps_leg/100) plus
1e-12*max(1,max(abs(v))) relative scaling. Do not publish a price merely because a
linear solver returned without an exception. Retain maximum complementarity,
linear-solve and monotonicity residuals for each grid run.

Use boundary bracketing to avoid unjustified asymptotic assumptions, including
negative-rate or negative-yield cases. At every finite domain endpoint S and
remaining tau, valid price bounds are
  lower=max(intrinsic,European_value),
  upper_call=S*exp(max(-q,0)*tau),
  upper_put=K*exp(max(-r,0)*tau).
Run one solution using lower boundary values and one using upper boundary values,
with identical payoff and interior discretization. Their ordering follows the
monotone discrete comparison principle. This brackets boundary-condition
uncertainty for that discretization; it is not by itself a proof of zero spatial
or temporal error. The full-domain option value must not be replaced by the
upper bound or by an arbitrary asymptotic line.

Expand each log endpoint by max(0.5,0.25*current_width) while retaining or
improving h until the lower/upper boundary solution difference at all queried
states and target-sensitive neighborhoods fits the allocated boundary budget.
Use the midpoint of the accepted bracket as the reported numerical value and
carry half its width as the measured boundary uncertainty. At S=0 the exact
limits are call=0 and put=K*max(1,exp(-r*tau)); these are additional diagnostics,
not permission to treat a positive S_min as exactly zero.

P50-R11. SPATIAL/TEMPORAL CONVERGENCE AND PRICE ERROR BUDGET

For one portfolio define
  W=sum_i abs(n_i)*m_i,
  eps_port=min(0.005 USD,1e-4*max(1 USD,abs(H0))),
  eps_leg=eps_port/max(W,1).
This is a shipped offline numerical target, not an established broker tolerance.
It allocates at most half a cent to measured portfolio pricing sensitivity.
A tighter build may reduce it; do not silently relax it when a run is expensive.

Keep separate budgets: spatial eps_leg/4, temporal eps_leg/4, boundary
 eps_leg/4, and solver/interpolation eps_leg/4 per leg. Start at 1601 log nodes;
compare with 3201 then 6401 as needed. Halve time steps independently while
keeping the spatial grid fixed, then refine space while holding the tighter time
mesh fixed. Compare all required observation slices on independent spot points:
current state; every strike and its immediate neighbors; candidate target roots;
exercise-boundary neighborhoods; and a fixed 257-point log-uniform validation
mesh over the utilized domain. Insert extra points where derivative/curvature or
threshold uncertainty is large. If errors concentrate between sampled points,
refine there and rerun the test; do not declare all spots validated from only
current-spot agreement.

Require two successive refinements within each allocated sensitivity budget and
require stable target membership/probability under P50-R14/R18. Save all measured
differences. These are numerical convergence diagnostics; without a rigorous
error proof they are not mathematical worst-case error certificates. Domain-tail
bounds below are separate analytic bounds under the specified GBM law.

One independent American comparator is a CRR tree. For N steps, dt=tau/N,
 u=exp(sigma*sqrt(dt)), d=1/u,
 p=(exp((r-q)*dt)-d)/(u-d), discount=exp(-r*dt).
Require 0<=p<=1; otherwise increase N, with a finite resource cap and an explicit
unresolved result on failure. At each backward node choose max(intrinsic,
discount*((1-p)*down_value+p*up_value)). Compare N,2N,4N values and their parity
oscillation. The tree supplies an independent numerical check, not a second
production engine whose output can be selected because it is more favorable.

P50-R12. QUOTE-INVERTED IV AND PATH-IV FALLBACK

For a valid midpoint p, solve price(sigma)=p with the same American/European
pricer, r,q, anchor and actual remaining maturity used in future repricing.
The initial supported IV domain is 0<=sigma<=8 annual decimal. This wide domain
is an implementation choice; an IV above it is an explicit unsupported-domain
case, not an impossible-market assertion.

Compute price(0) using the correct deterministic limit. Reject p below price(0)
minus measured pricing/quote tolerance. Start the upper bracket at 0.25, doubling
until price(high)>=p or high=8. If even price(8)<p beyond tolerance, return
LEG_IV_OUTSIDE_MODEL_DOMAIN. Bisection is sufficient: maintain low/high with
opposite residual signs, evaluate midpoint, keep the bracketing half, and stop
only when sigma width<=1e-8 AND price residual<=eps_leg. A small price residual
with a wide IV bracket is insufficient. If floating-point bisection can no
longer reduce the bracket and either gate fails, return IV_SOLVER_UNRESOLVED.
Verify the final price on the
independent refined grid; maximum 80 bisections. A bracket whose endpoints do
not straddle the target must not be passed to a root solver.

Price precision alone does not establish unique IV. Also invert bid and ask
within their valid price range to obtain an uncertainty interval. Estimate local
vega by two-sided sigma bump max(1e-5,1e-3*max(sigma,0.01)), keeping sigma>=0.
If price is flat to numerical tolerance over an IV interval wider than 1e-4,
or quote-induced IV uncertainty is wider than max(0.02,0.25*sigma), mark the
inversion weakly identified. A valid compatible vendor IV or deliberate scoped
IV override can supply the parameter with that status recorded. Otherwise return
LEG_IV_NOT_IDENTIFIABLE; never choose an arbitrary IV on an intrinsic plateau.
At exact zero-volatility quotes, use zero only if the input explicitly declares a
zero-volatility scenario; the ordinary market quote alone does not identify it.

Vendor IV acceptance requires normalized units, a valid known-at timestamp,
selected-model price within max(half_spread,eps_leg) of midpoint, and no other
model/quote-quality failure. Keep raw vendor IV even if a quote-inverted IV is
selected. An override has explicit scope and takes precedence without pretending
to be vendor-observed.

Path sigma priority is deliberate applicable override; compatible matching-expiry
aggregate IV already present in the snapshot; otherwise the following local ATM
fallback. Let F_proxy=S0*exp((r-q)*tau) for spot mode, or use Y0 in forward mode.
Select up to ten nearest valid contracts by abs(log(K/F_proxy)), requiring
abs(log(K/F_proxy))<=0.05, at least four contracts, at least two distinct strikes,
and both call and put rights. Sort ties by strike then right then contract_id.
Infer their well-identified individual IVs. With h_j=max(half_spread_j,tick_j/2,
eps_leg) and vega_j>0, set raw weight (vega_j/h_j)^2. Cap each weight at 25 times
the median positive raw weight; normalize weights to sum to one. The weighted
median IV is the path sigma. A weighted median is the smallest sorted IV whose
cumulative weight is at least one half. No solver or initial guess is hidden in
this robust local estimator.

Require the weighted 90th-minus-10th percentile IV span to be no more than
max(0.03,0.30*median_IV); otherwise return MISSING_PATH_IV with the local-dispersion
diagnostic. These gates describe this local-ATM fallback's supported domain,
not universal market liquidity or skew rules. Record every contributing contract,
quote, inverse-IV uncertainty, vega, weight and rejected-observation reason.
No history, realized volatility, IV rank or unrelated expiration enters the fit.
A fast GUI query consumes a precomputed admissible IV record or a qualified
packed inverse-pricing operator; this iterative inversion is offline work.

P50-R13. TARGET REGIONS WITHOUT MONOTONICITY ASSUMPTIONS

For every eligible time and target H, define
  f(S)=C+sum_i n_i*m_i*v_i(t,S)-H,
  R={S>0:f(S)>=0}.
A region is a sorted list of disjoint closed intervals plus optional isolated
points; endpoints may be zero or +infinity. Store threshold equality explicitly.
Do not reduce a condor, butterfly, uneven ratio or differing-IV spread to a
single barrier by strategy name.

For numerical American slices, construct each leg on a shared validated spot
knot set. Use piecewise-linear interpolation in S for membership construction;
log-coordinate grid generation does not imply interpolation must be linear in
log S. Sum leg values at each knot before constructing portfolio segments.
Within a segment [a,b], f is affine: if both endpoints are nonnegative the entire
segment qualifies; if both are strictly negative no part qualifies; if signs
differ, the root is a+(b-a)*(-f(a))/(f(b)-f(a)); if an endpoint equals zero retain
it; if both equal zero retain the whole segment. Merge touching intervals.
This finds all components of the declared piecewise-linear numerical function,
including isolated knot contacts and flat threshold intervals.

It does not prove that an unresolved true pricing curve has no narrow interval
between knots. Therefore repeat on the refined grid, include every neighborhood
with small |f| or large slope/curvature change, and compare target topology and
first-hit probabilities. Form numerical uncertainty bands with the recorded
portfolio pricing sensitivity E(t,S):
  certainly-in relative to that sensitivity: f_hat-E>=0;
  possibly-in relative to that sensitivity: f_hat+E>=0.
Keep both sets and evaluate their probabilities on the same paths. Call the
result a numerical sensitivity bracket, unless E has an actual mathematical
error bound. Do not advertise estimated error bands as rigorous certification.

For direct European prices, use adaptive subdivision on S. A valid conservative
Lipschitz bound for each option under this constant-q model is
exp(max(-q,0)*tau); therefore L=sum abs(n_i)*m_i*exp(max(-q,0)*tau) bounds the
portfolio's variation per dollar of S. For interval [a,b], evaluate f at midpoint
m. If f(m)-L*(b-a)/2-E>0 the entire interval is in; if
f(m)+L*(b-a)/2+E<0 it is out. Otherwise subdivide. Use every strike as an initial
breakpoint. Bracket each observed sign change and bisect to spot tolerance
max(1e-8 USD,1e-10*S). Intervals remaining unresolved at that tolerance are kept
in the possible set and excluded from the certain set until the probability
sensitivity test passes. This procedure does not lose tangencies merely because
there was no sign change.

Terminal regions use the exact piecewise-affine payoff, not a numerical option
price grid. Solve each affine inequality on [0,K1], [K1,K2], ..., [Kmax,+infinity)
and merge qualifying pieces. Strict POP uses >0, cent-POP uses >=0.01 dollars.
For continuous nondegenerate lognormal variables, isolated points have zero
endpoint probability; a deterministic sigma_path=0 state must still test equality.

P50-R14. DOMAIN CONTROL AND REGION ACCURACY

Paths are never clipped to a pricing-grid edge. A state outside the validated
American domain triggers an expanded grid and deterministic rerun of the affected
reference job, or an explicit unresolved-domain result. A direct European
formula can evaluate outside a provisional region grid; it must still establish
any newly encountered target components. A sampled endpoint tail estimate is
not a bound on the entire path's excursion.

For an analytic conservative continuous-path bound, write
  log(X_t/X0)=mu*t+sigma_path*W_t,
where mu=r-q-sigma_path^2/2 for spot and -sigma_path^2/2 for forward. Let H be the
full model horizon, l=log(X0/S_min)-abs(mu)*H and
u=log(S_max/X0)-abs(mu)*H. If l,u>0 and sigma_path>0, a valid union/reflection bound
on any domain exit is
  2*barPhi(l/(sigma_path*sqrt(H)))
  +2*barPhi(u/(sigma_path*sqrt(H))).
Require this bound <=1e-8 as an initial domain target, alongside boundary-price
sensitivity and the actual no-clipping policy. Expand the domain if it fails.
For sigma_path=0 check the entire deterministic drift range directly. The bound
is under this specified GBM, not a statement that the real underlying cannot
jump outside the domain.

Evaluate certain/possible membership on the same fixed 20000 independent region-
validation paths, using phase_id=30. Let C_region be the preregistered number of
mandatory target/deadline comparisons and use z=Phi_inverse(1-0.05/C_region)
in the one-sided Wilson upper endpoint. Count a gained possible-only hit per
path. Require that upper probability bound<=0.0005 for every comparison. Repeat after one further space/time
refinement. If the gate fails, refine the price/region representation. Do not
change the target, remove a narrow interval, or return the midpoint probability
as if topology had been resolved. The total error report distinguishes this
region sensitivity, domain bounds, observation-grid sensitivity and Monte Carlo
sampling uncertainty.

P50-R15. EXACT PATH EVOLUTION AND SHARED STATE

For elapsed model years dt>=0 and standard Normal Z:
  spot:
    S_next=S*exp((r-q-sigma_path^2/2)*dt+sigma_path*sqrt(dt)*Z);
  terminal-forward:
    Y_next=Y*exp(-sigma_path^2*dt/2+sigma_path*sqrt(dt)*Z).
All legs and all targets use the same underlying state on each path. Never
simulate separate underlying prices for calls, puts or individual spread legs.
For dt=0 do not consume a normal draw. For sigma_path=0 propagate deterministically
and do not add artificial sampling uncertainty.

Use complete elapsed duration across a closed interval in one exact transition,
with no closing-target check inside it. At the next eligible opening, price and
test immediately. Eligible times up to the requested deadline contribute to its
first-hit count; later times may contribute only to later reports/expiry.
Continue unhit paths through T even when the primary deadline is earlier.

Actual option maturity remains T at every mark. At time t use
  tau_i=year_fraction(t,T_i),
not year_fraction(t,deadline). A deadline restricts the probability event; it does
not turn still-unexpired options into intrinsic payoffs.

P50-R16. RANDOM STREAMS, PARALLELISM AND CHECKPOINTS

The canonical production design is N=200000 independent paths, logical shards
of 4096 paths, seed entropy 20261008, named PCG64 and NumPy SeedSequence. The final
shard contains the remaining N mod 4096 paths. Logical shards do not depend on
CPU count, process scheduling, RAM availability or completion order.

For logical shard b create the seed sequence directly as
  SeedSequence(entropy=20261008, spawn_key=(phase_id,b)),
then Generator(PCG64(seed_sequence)). phase_id=10 for production; 20 for pilot;
30 for independent region validation; 40 for independent benchmark samples.
Do not call one shared mutable spawn method concurrently. These explicit keys
and the exact software versions are part of the result identity.

Within each shard, visit the frozen master grid in chronological order and draw
one float64 normal vector of the shard's original path count for every positive-
duration transition. Even if some paths have already hit all targets, consume
the same full vector. Calculations for completed paths may be skipped, but their
normal slots must not be reassigned to survivors. This preserves the result when
survivor pruning or worker count changes. Do not replace standard_normal with a
different transform and claim bit-for-bit compatibility merely because PCG64's
raw stream is unchanged.

Each worker owns its generator and fixed shard. The coordinator merges integer
hit counts and histograms in ascending shard order. Integer counters avoid
floating-point reduction-order differences. Divide by final N once. If any
floating aggregates are required, use the same documented reduction order and
compensated summation. Windows multiprocessing starts from an import-safe
if __name__ == "__main__" controller; numerical worker functions are module-level
and receive immutable serializable job identifiers/arrays, not Qt widgets.

A checkpoint stores snapshot/model/accounting/calendar/complete-grid hashes,
software and RNG scheme versions, fixed N and target set, completed shard IDs,
first-hit and settlement counts, and diagnostics. Atomic writes use a temporary
file, flush, fsync where supported, then same-filesystem replace. A partial shard
is discarded and rerun with its same semantic seed, or resumed only from a full
saved state including bit-generator state and path/target arrays. Never count a
partially completed shard as complete. Refreshed market data or changed targets
make a different job, not a resumable continuation of the old probability.

P50-R17. PILOT MONITORING REFINEMENT

Use 20000 pilot paths independent of production. Pilot fine/coarse paths must be
coupled to the same Brownian realization. Construct the union of all six grids
60,30,15,7.5,3.75,1.875 seconds, the same pre-close points, openings, and required
reporting times. Generate increments on the finest union once and accumulate
them for every coarser endpoint. Equivalently use a recorded Brownian-bridge
construction to fill missing times, but a shared seed with unrelated calls is
not coupling. The production output applies no bridge hit correction.

At any common time use the identical price/region representation for all grid
levels. Adding observations must not remove a coarse-grid hit. For each adjacent
pair count gained successes D_j=I_fine_j-I_coarse_j, with D_j in {0,1}. Negative
D_j is a test failure, not sampling noise. Calculate the Wilson upper bound on
the gained-success fraction. Test every requested target/deadline and the
expiry reference, including the terminal payoff event on identical paths.

The acceptance threshold is 0.0025 absolute probability on two successive
adjacent refinements for every mandatory report. Choose the finer step in the second of the first two successive passing
comparisons. Thus passing 60->30 and 30->15 selects15 seconds, never60 or30.
If a later pair is the first to satisfy both gates, select its corresponding
finer step. Every mandatory report must pass the same two comparisons. Use a preregistered simultaneous
one-sided confidence allocation across C comparisons:
  z=Phi_inverse(1-0.05/C),
where C is the fixed number of adjacent-grid/report/target comparisons. If this
z would be below the ordinary 95% one-sided z, use the latter. This strengthens
rather than weakens the single-comparison uncertainty gate.

If no admissible grid passes through 1.875 seconds, return
NUMERICAL_CONVERGENCE_UNRESOLVED. A preliminary result may retain its actual grid
and status. Freeze the chosen grid before starting the independent fixed-N
production run. Passing adjacent refinements is not a rigorous error bound to
continuous monitoring; display the actual discrete monitoring convention and
separate the convergence diagnostic from Monte Carlo error.

P50-R18. FIRST-HIT ESTIMATOR, WILSON INTERVAL AND STOPPING RULE

Initialize first_hit_index[path,target] to an unmatched sentinel and
settlement_success[path,target]=false. At each eligible time evaluate membership
only for targets not yet hit. Save the first matching time, never overwrite it
with a later time. At T evaluate the exact terminal payoff for unmatched paths
and mark settlement-only achievements. A complete production shard returns the
histogram of first hits and its terminal-only counts; full spot histories are
not required.

For k successes in N fixed independent paths,
  p=k/N,
  SE=sqrt(p*(1-p)/N),
  z=1.959963984540054,
  d=1+z^2/N,
  center=(p+z^2/(2*N))/d,
  half=z/d*sqrt(p*(1-p)/N+z^2/(4*N^2)),
  Wilson95=[center-half,center+half].
Use these formulas even at k=0 or N; a zero-width Wald interval is not valid
there. For one-sided gates use the same Wilson expression with the explicitly
chosen one-sided z and retain the upper endpoint. Report ordinary production
intervals as per-output sampling intervals, not simultaneous coverage for an
unlimited collection of queried strategies.

Fix N before sampling. Do not stop when the displayed probability looks favorable
or when an ordinary fixed-N interval first becomes narrow. A cancelled job is
incomplete and records actual completed independent shards/path count; it cannot
be relabeled as the intended 200000-path run. At p=0.5,N=200000 the approximate
normal half-width is 0.0021913 probability, or 0.21913 percentage points.

For deterministic sigma_path=0 jobs evaluate the sole deterministic path and
return an exact model-event indicator with no Monte Carlo interval. Do not
manufacture a Wilson interval from 200000 duplicated deterministic paths.
An all-state region can similarly establish an exact hit analytically.

P50-R19. ENDPOINT INTEGRATION AND AN INDEPENDENT PROBABILITY SOLVER

For H=year_fraction(t0,t),
  log(X_t)~Normal(mu_log,sd_log^2),
  mu_log=log(X0)+(r-q-sigma_path^2/2)*H for spot,
  mu_log=log(X0)-sigma_path^2*H/2 for forward,
  sd_log=sigma_path*sqrt(H).
For each disjoint target interval [a,b], integrate
  Phi((log(b)-mu_log)/sd_log)-Phi((log(a)-mu_log)/sd_log),
with CDF(0)=0 and CDF(+infinity)=1. Use normal survival functions for remote upper
tails and stable CDF differences for narrow intervals. Sum disjoint probabilities
with compensated summation. At H=0 or sd_log=0 evaluate the deterministic state
against the exact interval endpoint/equality rules. This computes endpoint
probability, not the probability of having hit earlier.

An independent backward solver validates the discrete-observation first-hit
model without reusing the production Monte Carlo logic. Let A_k be the target
region at grid time t_k. For the terminal-inclusive version initialize
  u_T(x)=1{C+G(x)>=H}.
For the closing-only version initialize u_T(x)=0 if T is not an eligible close.
Then recurse backward:
  continuation_k(x)=E[u_{k+1}(X_next)|X_k=x],
  u_k(x)=1 if t_k is eligible and x in A_k,
         continuation_k(x) otherwise.
Closed propagation times apply expectation only. This is an undiscounted
probability recursion; no exp(-r*dt) factor is present.

One implementable transition evaluator uses a fixed log-state grid, piecewise-
linear interpolation of u in log x, and exact truncated-normal cell integrals.
If u(y)=a_j+b_j*y within log interval [l_j,r_j], Y~N(m,s^2), then its contribution is
  a_j*(Phi(z_r)-Phi(z_l))
  +b_j*(m*(Phi(z_r)-Phi(z_l))+s*(phi(z_l)-phi(z_r))),
where z_l=(l_j-m)/s and z_r=(r_j-m)/s. Sum all cells. Run a lower
probability recursion assigning0 outside the numerical log domain and an upper
recursion assigning1 outside it; include both normal tail masses explicitly at
every transition. Refine/expand until the resulting probability bracket width
is<=0.0005 at all requested inputs, separately from time/space sensitivity.
For s=0 interpolate/evaluate at deterministic m. For stable nonnegative cell
weights, write P_j=Phi(z_r)-Phi(z_l), M_j=m*P_j+s*(phi(z_l)-phi(z_r)); then
left_weight=(r_j*P_j-M_j)/(r_j-l_j) and
right_weight=(M_j-l_j*P_j)/(r_j-l_j). Their sum is P_j and both are mathematically
nonnegative. Material negativity or a mass deficit requires stable recomputation/
refinement, not post-hoc clipping. This defines the transition operation without
an unspecified quadrature method. Refine its grid and expand its domain independently. Store
normalization and [0,1] preservation diagnostics rather than clipping invalid
probabilities after a bad transition.

For a separate continuous-monitoring diagnostic, solve
  u_t+(r-q)*S*u_S+0.5*sigma_path^2*S^2*u_SS=0
outside an eligible absorbing target region, with u=1 on the region, propagation
without absorption in closures, and the declared terminal condition. Use the
same nonnegative-generator discretization as P50-R09 with r-discount removed.
The continuous diagnostic and the finite-grid production event differ. A closer
match to a broker display is not authority to change which event is reported.

P50-R20. NUMERICAL DEFAULTS AND FAILURE CONTRACT

Shipped reference settings:
  schema/model version: P50_REFERENCE_V1 / approved frozen-IV continuous-q profile
  path measure: constant-parameter GBM Q_path
  primary fraction: 0.50 of original finite positive maximum profit
  primary mark basis: gross; optional explicit fixed-cost net companion
  time basis: ACT/365F elapsed UTC; New York display/input
  N production: 200000; N pilot: 20000; N independent region validation:20000
  RNG: PCG64; seed: 20261008; canonical shard size:4096
  pilot grid seconds:60,30,15,7.5,3.75,1.875
  paired monitoring upper-bound tolerance:0.0025 twice consecutively
  initial American space nodes:1601
  numerical portfolio sensitivity target:min(0.005 USD,1e-4*max(1 USD,abs(H0)))
  measured region uncertainty probability target:0.0005
  initial analytic GBM path-domain exit bound target:1e-8
  initial IV supported domain:[0,8] annual decimal
  main bridge hit correction:disabled
  numeric arrays:float64
  standalone numerical memory target:256 MiB

Resource budgets are operational constraints, not permission to weaken these
settings. A GUI build can use more PC memory and worker processes while preserving
logical streams, arithmetic precision, model inputs and acceptance thresholds.
If the requested scope exceeds resources, checkpoint and return a precise
incomplete/offline-build status. An ordinary GUI query must abstain when its
validated pack lacks coverage; it must not start this expensive engine secretly.

Missing/invalid essential inputs yield no probability. Distinguish:
  INVALID_CONTRACT_OR_UNITS, INCOMPATIBLE_FIXING, INVALID_DEADLINE,
  MISSING_RATE, MISSING_DIVIDEND_OR_CARRY_INPUT, MISSING_PATH_IV,
  LEG_IV_NOT_IDENTIFIABLE, LEG_IV_OUTSIDE_MODEL_DOMAIN, IV_SOLVER_UNRESOLVED,
  PARITY_FIT_UNSTABLE, INCOHERENT_SNAPSHOT,
  MODEL_BASIS_TOO_LARGE, MODEL_MARKS_INVALID,
  PRICER_NOT_CONVERGED, TARGET_REGION_UNRESOLVED,
  NUMERICAL_CONVERGENCE_UNRESOLVED, NUMERICAL_DOMAIN_UNRESOLVED,
  CHECKPOINT_MISMATCH, RESOURCE_LIMIT, CANCELLED.
Each error contains affected fields/contracts/times, the failed numerical gate,
and permitted local-data/scenario correction. A need for actual dated market
values is a data requirement; the algorithm for handling them is fully given
here and never delegates its definition to another source.

P50-R21. SELF-CONTAINED FIXTURES AND EXPECTED RESULTS

These are synthetic engineering fixtures, not market prices or trading advice.
The following exact or independently calculated values seed the test suite:

  F01 Credit put spread:
    short 767 put, long 765 put, one each, multiplier100, C=80 USD.
    M=80, L=120, H50=40 USD.
    Target closing debit<=0.40 quoted points.
    Terminal H50 region S>=766.60.
    Strict positive terminal region S>766.20.
    Whole-position cent-POP region S>=766.2001.

  F02 Debit call vertical:
    long100 call, short105 call, one each, multiplier100, C=-200 USD.
    M=300, L=200, H50=150 USD.
    Required closing spread value>=3.50 quoted points.
    Terminal H50 region S>=103.50.

  F03 Equal-wing iron condor:
    long90 put, short95 put, short105 call, long110 call; multiplier100;
    C=200 USD. M=200,L=300,H50=100 USD.
    Terminal H50 region [94,106]. The two outer losing tails must remain separate.

  F04 Unlimited-upside rejection:
    one long100 call with C=-500 USD,multiplier100 gives unbounded max profit.
    A fresh half-of-maximum-profit target is undefined. Do not set M from a grid.
    One short100 call produces an unbounded loss tail, a distinct diagnostic.

  F05 European BSM:
    S=100,K=100,r=0.05,q=0,sigma=0.20,tau=1 ACT/365F year.
    Call=10.450583572186; Put=5.573526022257 quoted units.
    Discount=0.951229424500714; forward=105.12710963760242.
    Black-forward prices agree with the spot formula to 1e-10 quoted units.
    Call-Put=S-K*exp(-r) to the same tolerance.

  F06 American no-dividend call:
    Same as F05 with q=0,r>=0. American and European call agree within the
    accepted numerical error budget. A false positive early-exercise premium
    larger than that budget fails the solver test.

  F07 American put independent tree:
    Same as F05 for the put. CRR N=2000 yields approximately6.08998995255238;
    N=4000 yields approximately6.090180715022948.
    These are finite-tree numerical outputs, not an exact closed-form price.
    The converged PDE should agree with the independently refined tree within
    their measured joint error, and the value must exceed the European put.
    Generate N=8000 and refinements as needed for a tighter regression target.

  F08 Zero-volatility American put with negative r:
    S=80,K=100,r=-0.05,q=0.02,sigma=0,tau=1.
    Value=max_u[100*exp(0.05*u)-80*exp(-0.02*u)]
         =26.711215773061994 at u=1.
    Intrinsic is20; a solver that always returns intrinsic at sigma=0 is wrong.

  F09 Exact GBM terminal moments:
    E[S_H]=S0*exp((r-q)*H),
    Var(S_H)=S0^2*exp(2*(r-q)*H)*(exp(sigma_path^2*H)-1).
    Validate independent samples with preregistered sampling tolerances; do not
    expect random sample moments to equal theoretical values bit for bit.

  F10 Constant upper barrier diagnostic:
    S0=100,r=0.02,q=0,sigma_path=0.20,H=0.25 years,
    synthetic eligible observation domain includes all model times,
    target region is S>=110. Log drift mu=r-q-sigma^2/2=0.
    Continuous hit probability=2*Phi(-log(1.1)/(0.2*sqrt(0.25)))
                              =0.3405384952145612.
    Endpoint probability=0.1702692476072806.
    Finite-grid first-hit approaches the continuous value from below as
    monitoring refines, within sampling/numerical uncertainty. This fixture
    explicitly tests that endpoint probability is not mislabeled as P50.

  F11 General constant barrier formula for an independent test:
    For X_t=mu*t+sigma*W_t, b>0,H>0,
    P(max_{0<=t<=H}X_t>=b)
      =Phi((mu*H-b)/(sigma*sqrt(H)))
       +exp(2*mu*b/sigma^2)*Phi((-mu*H-b)/(sigma*sqrt(H))).
    Evaluate extreme arguments stably. This is a continuous constant-barrier
    diagnostic only; do not apply it to moving, multiple, American-profit
    regions or across ineligible market-closed intervals as a shortcut.

  F12 Account-independent first-hit state machine:
    A supplied deterministic mark sequence [-10,45,-30] with H=40 counts once
    at the middle eligible observation. Sequence [45] during a closed gap
    does not count. A terminal-only payoff45 with no earlier eligible hit
    increments settlement-only, not closing-only. These are arithmetic tests.

  F13 Reproducibility:
    Same immutable job, library versions, grid and shard scheme produces the
    same integer counts with1,2,4 or8 worker processes, and after checkpoint
    resume. Survivor pruning on/off must preserve those counts. If a legitimate
