Generate position templates from actual admitted strikes in this sequence:
  vertical: one long and one short of the same right, both credit and debit
    orientations, widths selected from 1,2,5,10,20 listed strike steps;
  butterfly: quantities (+1,-2,+1) or their negatives on three ordered same-
    right strikes, with equal and unequal wings included as separate families;
  iron condor: a bounded put vertical plus bounded call vertical, with short
    put below short call, independent widths from the same width set;
  asymmetric combination: two compatible defined-risk components with integer
    size ratio 1:1,1:2,2:1, subject to total quantity and eight-leg ceilings.
Choose short/central strikes by rotating through offsets -10,-5,-2,-1,0,1,2,5,10
from the nearest spot strike, and include far-tail cases from the extra ladder.
Reject unbounded-tail structures, nonpositive/undefined original M0, bad marks,
and invalid contracts; count every rejected template and reason. Risk-infeasible
cases remain in a separate feasibility-test set, not as supported policy labels.

Create an original campaign by actually executing the synthetic opening package
at the stated modeled sides, posting exact fees, and calculating M0_gross and
H0 from that completed original position. For ongoing-campaign examples, evolve
this synthetic original state using the specified GBM and action generator for
zero, one, two, or three declared adjustments. Post each real modeled fill or
nonfill; retain the complete original ledger and H0. This yields current cash
and remaining risk/expense budgets from events rather than assigning a new
target to a convenient final position. Include flat-below-target/reentry and
failed/realized-target states. For boundary fixtures whose arbitrary c is not
generated by these paths, label them ACCOUNTING_COUNTERFACTUAL and include an
explicit synthetic prior closed-trade cashflow explaining c; no deposits may
be labeled trading profit.

Generate each case's requested-deadline set before labeling. Include original
target and hard cutoff where applicable, eligible times nearest 25%,50%,75%
of the forecast span, and one seeded eligible subminute time. Deduplicate and
sort. In designated closed-period cases include an ineligible reporting instant
to test endpoint-vs-hit semantics. The whole set enters the P50-R master grid;
the identical set, masks, timestamps, and grid hash enter the surrogate input.

Near-boundary augmentation is deterministic: for each retained base state,
form spot perturbations +/-0.01% and +/-0.1%, IV perturbations +/-0.001 and
cost/target-gap perturbations +/-$0.01 and +/-$1 where the resulting exact
ledger/objective fixture remains defined. In normal campaign cases H0 is never
reset; target perturbations are separate original-objective fixtures. Include
budgets exactly at, one cent below, and one cent above the BP/loss/expense
threshold. Save labels of rejected states for classifier/abstention testing,
not as ordinary numerical zeros.

If a deterministic case generator cannot obtain 32,768 accepted design states
within 1,000,000 raw attempts, terminate DESIGN_COVERAGE_INSUFFICIENT with counts
by family and rejection reason. Do not silently underfill an important family,
broaden the domain, or substitute a different strategy. The exact generator,
draws, manifests, rejected-case accounting, fitting algorithms, and validation
gates in this document are sufficient to build the initial pack from scratch.

END P50-M

===============================================================================
BEGIN SRC-A — TERMINAL NAVIGATION AND REPORTING REQUIREMENTS
===============================================================================

we are going to calculate Q with data fetched from tastytrade's API. we are not gonna have historical data. with this assumption, plan in great detail how to write a script in python that will get a DTE case and strikes with P or C at the start to symbolize puts and calls and also it will get a number which will represent the amount of % out of the max profit and a then a series of numbers which will represent the amount of days passing from the open of the trade, and the script will calculate the chance of the trade reaching the given % of max profit up until each one of the given times and it will order them accordingly and emit the probabilities for them in the results (the series will come like this: 1, 2, 3, 4... and sometimes, there will be only one number. you gotta handle both cases), and after combining them and calculating the strategy and the Q for it, it will return the ALL of the probabilities for it, from probability of profit to probability of 50% of profit at some point before expiration, and also the probability of x% profit until t* time given at the inputting stage. 
all of the operation, including inputs and outputs, will be done via the terminal. the moment the script is activated, it won't just run once. it will first ask for a symbol, then a DTE case, then for the strikes. these inputs will have 3 levels, and once emitting the results for the chosen trade, it will go back to the point where it asks for the strikes and will be ready to do the calculation again while considering the symbol and DTE case are staying the same. at every point in the inputs' stages, there will be an option to write the command 'e' which will fall back to the previous stage until the very first one (in which writing 'e' will do nothing).

END SRC-A

===============================================================================
BEGIN SRC-C — QQQ OPTIONS DATA CONTRACT
===============================================================================

QQQ OPTIONS / raw_theta — CANONICAL DATA CONTRACT

Use these rules whenever reading, researching, backtesting, replaying, repairing, or extending the local QQQ options dataset. Treat them as data-contract requirements, not strategy settings. Do not silently substitute other data, invent missing values, or use future information.

SOURCE / LAYOUT
- Authoritative root: D:\QQQ\raw_theta
- Canonical current folders:
  greeks\       option quotes + IV + Greeks
  ohlcv\        per-contract option OHLCV
  trades\       option trades
  oi_before\    OI safely available before/during the session (prior/opening snapshot)
  oi\           same-session/EOD OI; NOT intraday-known
- Raw source files are immutable .csv.gz partitions. Index metadata first; load only partitions/columns actually needed.
- Logical partition key = (data type, historical session date, expiration). Never merge different session/expiry pairs.
- Filenames contain session and expiry, normally:
  greeks_YYYYMMDD_exp=YYYY-MM-DD.csv.gz
  ohlcv_YYYYMMDD_exp=YYYY-MM-DD.csv.gz
  trades_YYYYMMDD_exp=YYYY-MM-DD.csv.gz
  oi_prev_YYYYMMDD_exp=YYYY-MM-DD.csv.gz / related prior-OI files in oi_before
  oi_eod_YYYYMMDD_exp=YYYY-MM-DD.csv.gz in oi
  Prefix variations/copy suffixes may exist; parse the embedded YYYYMMDD and exp=YYYY-MM-DD rather than relying only on the leading text.
- Scan the actual directory each run or maintain an invalidatable index. Do not assume fixed date/DTE coverage from old counts or filenames.
- Duplicate files mapping to the same logical partition are an error unless an explicit duplicate-resolution policy is requested. Never silently choose one.

CANONICAL FIELDS / NORMALIZATION
- Greeks partitions can contain: symbol, expiration, strike, right, timestamp, bid, ask, delta, theta, vega, rho, epsilon, lambda, gamma, vanna, charm, vomma, veta, vera, speed, zomma, color, ultima, d1, d2, dual_delta, dual_gamma, implied_vol, iv_error, underlying_timestamp, underlying_price, plus possible other vendor fields.
- OHLCV commonly contains: symbol, expiration, strike, right, timestamp, open, high, low, close, volume.
- Trades commonly contain contract identity + timestamp/sequence/price/size/exchange/conditions.
- OI contains contract identity + open_interest and may or may not have a meaningful intraday timestamp.
- Historical Theta schemas can vary. Canonicalize recognized aliases instead of hard-coding one export header. Important aliases include:
  timestamp: timestamp, datetime, regular_time, time, quote_time, date_time
  ms_of_day: ms_of_day, msofday, milliseconds_of_day
  strike: strike, strike_price, strikeprice, strike_milli, strike_millis
  right: right, option_type, type, put_call, cp
  bid/ask/last: corresponding *_price aliases; last may appear as price
  implied_vol: implied_vol, implied_volatility, impliedvolatility, iv
  underlying_price: underlying_price, underlying_px, underlying
  open_interest: open_interest, openinterest, oi
- Required fields must be validated explicitly. If a required field is absent, fail/report it; never guess which unrelated column is the price/IV/etc.
- Normalize rights to C/P (or CALL/PUT internally, consistently).
- Strike units have varied. Preserve raw strike until scale is resolved. Auto-detect a plausible positive divisor using QQQ spot/strike plausibility (commonly 1 or 1000; inspect broader candidates if needed), then normalized_strike = raw_strike/divisor. If scale is ambiguous, stop/flag it instead of guessing.
- Normalize IV consistently. Determine whether source IV is decimal or percent from schema/data checks; convert once to decimal for calculations and record the convention. Never mix 25 with 0.25.

TIME / SESSION SEMANTICS
- Market timezone: America/New_York. Interpret naive Theta timestamps as New York wall-clock time. Preserve Eastern wall-clock semantics; do not silently reinterpret them as UTC.
- Regular session for minute research is 09:30 <= t < 16:00 ET. A vendor request may use end_time=16:00; treat 16:00 observations as the close/EOD boundary unless the task explicitly needs them.
- Floor/normalize minute data consistently only after parsing the original timestamp; preserve the original timestamp when quote age or event ordering matters.
- Legacy reader convention that must be preserved: for Greeks, IV, and option OHLCV requests, an intraday selector of 00:00:00 means “return/use all timestamps for that session,” not “midnight only.”
- OI is a daily snapshot, not a true intraday time series. Intraday clock filters do not give it additional temporal precision.
- Legacy semantic aliases:
  oi_yesterday / prior OI -> oi_before
  oi_today / same-session OI -> oi
  iv view -> implied-volatility data, normally implied_vol from greeks, with the same timestamp rules.

NO-LOOKAHEAD / AS-OF RULES
- For any target time T, use only information known by T. Never choose a later observation because it is closer.
- As-of option quote = latest valid quote/event with timestamp <= T.
- In a replay where cursor T denotes a fully revealed one-minute bar, sub-minute events may be included only if timestamp < T + 1 minute; events from the next minute are excluded. The normalized quote minute itself must never be later than the cursor minute.
- Preserve the selected source timestamp and calculate quote age. Distinguish current, stale, missing, and invalid quotes rather than backfilling from the future.
- Contract OHLCV and trades must also be filtered to revealed time before aggregation/volume/last-trade calculations.
- Deduplicate only within the same logical contract/time identity using deterministic event order (timestamp, then sequence when present); for minute snapshots, keep the last valid event in that minute.
- If a requested time has no acceptable prior quote, return missing/skip according to the task. Never use a future quote.
- When the task specifies maximum quote age, minimum OI, or maximum relative spread, enforce those thresholds exactly. Relative spread = (ask-bid)/mid when mid > 0. If no threshold is supplied, do not invent a strategy-specific threshold; still expose age/spread/quality so downstream work can filter.
- Basic quote validity: finite bid/ask, bid >= 0, ask > 0, ask >= bid. Treat crossed/invalid markets as invalid rather than repairing them.
- For intraday work, use only oi_before as known OI. Same-session/EOD oi may be used only for explicitly post-session/EOD analyses and must never leak into an earlier timestamp.

UNDERLYING PRICE AUTHORITY
- The authoritative QQQ underlying price source is the separate dataset D:\QQQ\OHLCV_daily, not raw_theta’s embedded underlying_price.
- Whenever the QQQ OHLCV dataset is available/required, join each options observation to the QQQ Parquet price known at the same as-of time and use that for spot, ATM/moneyness, boundaries, scenarios, and underlying returns.
- raw_theta underlying_price / underlying_timestamp may be retained for diagnostics/vendor comparison, but must not silently replace the authoritative QQQ Parquet close.
- Never join an underlying observation from the future. If an exact minute is unavailable, use only a permitted prior observation and expose its age, or mark the join missing.
- Keep the options and underlying datasets physically/logically separate; join them explicitly by session/time.

DATA-TYPE SEMANTICS
- greeks: primary source for historical bid/ask, IV and Greeks. For a chain snapshot, resolve exact session+expiration, keep revealed rows only, then take the last revealed row per symbol/strike/right.
- ohlcv: option-contract OHLCV. Filter by contract/session/expiration and as-of time before using. Volume is contract volume, not QQQ underlying volume.
- trades: event-level trade source for last trade and trade-derived volume when needed. Respect sequence/order and as-of filtering.
- oi_before: safely-known prior/opening OI; valid for intraday liquidity filters.
- oi: same-session/EOD OI; catalog it but do not expose it as morning/intraday-known information.
- If both trade-derived and stored option OHLCV exist, do not mix them silently. State which source is used and validate consistency if combining.

READ / PERFORMANCE RULES
- Prefer metadata-only catalog scans, then read only required partitions and columns.
- .csv.gz is sequential: row filters do not avoid decompression. For repeated work, cache normalized/decompressed partitions in memory and/or an optional Parquet mirror; the raw .csv.gz files remain the source of truth.
- Cache identity should include file path plus size/mtime (or stronger content fingerprint) so changed files invalidate cached frames.
- Treat cached frames as read-only; copy before mutation.
- On SSD/NVMe, independent partition/data-type reads may be parallelized; on HDD, sequential reads may be faster due to seek contention. Do not parallelize blindly.
- Use vectorized filtering/normalization. Avoid repeatedly reopening the same gzip merely to inspect an unchanged schema; cache schema mappings, but re-inspect a file if the cached mapping fails validation.
- Missing folder/file/schema/data must produce an explicit diagnostic. No automatic synthetic-data fallback for production research/backtests.

THETADATA FETCH / REPAIR CONTRACT
Apply this section only when the task is to acquire, recover, or repair raw_theta data; ordinary analysis should read the existing local dataset.

- ThetaData v3 is accessed through the local Theta Terminal, historically at the loopback host on TCP port 25503.
- Iterate the supplied/discovered trading-day -> expiration mapping. Do not assume the current dataset is limited to a particular DTE merely because an old mapping filename included “5day”; scan actual partitions.
- Historical production defaults were 1-minute data, 09:30:00-16:00:00 ET, global HTTP concurrency 8 and day concurrency 8. Respect the active ThetaData plan limits.
- Greeks acquisition is FULL-DAY, SINGLE-SHOT:
  primary: /v3/option/history/greeks/all with interval=1m
  fallback: /v3/option/history/trade_greeks/all
  Do not stitch/chunk a supposedly full-day Greeks partition.
- Strict integrity is the default: require start/end-session presence and at least ~98% expected minute coverage. If a downloaded Greeks file is partial/invalid, discard it and retry; never keep it merely because it is non-empty.
- Trades are fetched as a full-session file and validated. Option 1-minute OHLCV may be rebuilt locally from validated trades:
  per (minute,strike,right): open=first trade, high=max, low=min, close=last, volume=sum(size).
- Use atomic writes: write to a temporary .part file, validate, then os.replace/rename into the final path. Never leave a partial file looking complete.
- OI handling:
  prior/opening OI: request the appropriate same-date/prior snapshot, filter to the target expiration, and store semantically under oi_before.
  EOD/same-session OI: when the vendor publication requires it, probe forward through later mapped trading days until the target session’s EOD value is obtainable; store semantically under oi. Do not cap this to an arbitrary short calendar window unless explicitly requested.
- Retry transient/network/server failures with jittered exponential backoff. Historical retryable statuses included 408, 425, 429, 500, 502, 503, 504, 570, 571.
- Maintain resumable state per (dataset, day, expiration): append event log + current manifest/state + unresolved report. Historically these lived under raw_theta\_logs as stream_results.log, manifest_events.jsonl, manifest_state.csv, unresolved.csv.
- On restart, validate existing outputs before marking them complete. Reuse valid completed files; rebuild invalid/partial ones; retry unresolved/retryable records in recovery passes.
- Never overwrite a known-good raw partition merely to simplify a rerun.
- At completion, explicitly report unresolved/invalid/no-data records; do not silently claim full coverage.

FINAL SAFETY RULE
When application strategy/replay convenience conflicts with dataset facts, preserve this data contract: authoritative QQQ Parquet spot, Eastern-time semantics, immutable raw partitions, explicit schema/quality validation, OI publication timing, and strict no-lookahead take precedence.

END SRC-C

===============================================================================
BEGIN SRC-B — QQQ UNDERLYING DATA CONTRACT
===============================================================================

QQQ UNDERLYING OHLCV — CANONICAL DATA CONTRACT

Use these rules whenever reading, researching, backtesting, replaying, validating, or extending the local QQQ underlying-price dataset. This dataset is the authoritative QQQ price source when options work is joined to it.

SOURCE / LAYOUT
- Authoritative root: D:\QQQ\OHLCV_daily
- One Parquet file per trading session.
- Exact canonical filename: QQQ_1m_YYYY-MM-DD.parquet
- Canonical columns: regular_time, open, high, low, close, volume
- regular_time is the preferred timestamp field. For compatible historical readers, accepted fallbacks are datetime, timestamp, time, date.
- Index files by the date embedded in the filename. Scan the real directory rather than assuming a fixed date range.
- More than one file for the same session date is an error. Missing/nonmatching sessions must be reported explicitly.
- Treat source Parquet files as immutable. Do not overwrite or “fix” source data in-place during analysis.

TIME / SESSION SEMANTICS
- Market timezone: America/New_York.
- Preserve Eastern wall-clock time; do not silently convert the trading clock to UTC.
- If timestamps are timezone-aware, normalize them to New York time before removing timezone metadata for wall-clock joins. If naive, interpret them as New York time.
- Canonical regular-session minute range: 09:30 through 15:59 ET, i.e. 09:30 <= t < 16:00.
- A normal full session has 390 one-minute slots. Do not fabricate missing minutes simply to force 390 rows.
- Sort strictly by timestamp after parsing.

ROW VALIDATION
For each source row:
- timestamp must be valid and belong to the file/session date after New York normalization;
- open/high/low/close must be finite;
- volume must be finite and >= 0;
- reject invalid candle geometry: high < low, high < max(open,close), or low > min(open,close);
- reject duplicate timestamps within a session rather than silently keeping an arbitrary one;
- exclude rows outside regular trading hours for RTH studies/replay;
- canonical replay/production handling ignores volume == 0 rows. If a research question explicitly needs zero-volume placeholders, preserve them only as a separately declared alternative treatment.

NO-LOOKAHEAD / AS-OF RULES
- For a target/replay time T, filter source one-minute rows FIRST to timestamp <= T; only then aggregate, calculate indicators, features, realized returns, boundaries, or other derived values.
- Future one-minute rows must never influence current OHLC, volume, VWAP, SMA/EMA, volatility, market state, or an options join.
- Never backfill a missing minute from a future minute.
- If an exact timestamp is unavailable and the task explicitly permits an as-of lookup, use only the latest prior valid row and expose its age. Otherwise mark it missing.
- Weekend, holiday, absent, or invalid session dates are not automatically substituted with a neighboring day.

AUTHORITATIVE-SPOT RULE FOR OPTIONS
- D:\QQQ\OHLCV_daily is authoritative for QQQ underlying spot.
- When joining to D:\QQQ\raw_theta, use the latest QQQ close that is legitimately revealed at the target time. Use this value for spot, ATM selection, moneyness, IV-boundary calculations, scenario starting spot, and underlying returns.
- raw_theta fields such as underlying_price / underlying_timestamp may be retained for diagnostics but must not silently override this dataset.
- Keep both datasets separate at rest and join them explicitly by New York session/time with no future join.

AGGREGATION
- Base data is one minute. Build higher timeframes from revealed 1-minute rows, with buckets anchored to the 09:30 session open.
- For each aggregate bucket:
  open   = first revealed 1m open
  high   = max revealed 1m high
  low    = min revealed 1m low
  close  = latest revealed 1m close
  volume = sum revealed 1m volume
- The current higher-timeframe bar is partial until all of its source minutes are revealed; never insert future minutes to complete it.
- Daily RTH bars, if derived, use only that session’s valid RTH rows.
- Calculate indicators only after the required as-of filtering/aggregation. Warm-up history may be loaded, but it must also obey the target-time cutoff.

ACCESS / PERFORMANCE
- Scan/index filenames and metadata first; load only the sessions and columns required for the task.
- Cache validated session frames when repeated access is expected, and invalidate caches when file size/mtime/fingerprint changes.
- For large ranges, materialize only needed sessions/windows where possible rather than concatenating the entire archive unnecessarily.
- Columnar readers such as DuckDB, PyArrow, or pandas are acceptable; implementation choice must not change the semantics above.
- Automatically noticing newly added daily files is desirable; refresh the index when the directory changes.
- Never replace missing production data with synthetic/demo prices unless the user explicitly requests a separate synthetic mode, and label that mode unmistakably.

EXTENDING / REPAIRING THE DATASET
- This file defines the complete local Parquet storage/access contract. No automatic underlying-data downloader is part of replay; a separately requested import must supply compatible underlying observations.
- If an explicit import/repair task supplies underlying observations for added/rebuilt sessions, preserve the exact one-file-per-session naming, required OHLCV schema, 1-minute granularity, New York-time semantics, and validation rules above.
- Validate any new session before it becomes part of the canonical directory; use atomic temp-file -> final-file replacement.
- Never substitute ThetaData’s embedded option underlying reference as the mechanism for repairing this authoritative QQQ dataset unless the user explicitly changes the data-source policy.

FINAL SAFETY RULE
Any backtest, replay, or options study using this dataset must preserve: New York wall-clock timing, RTH filtering, data validation, immutable source files, explicit missing-data handling, and strict no-lookahead. These rules override convenience behavior in older scripts.

END SRC-B

QQQ OPTIONS TRADING SIMULATOR — INTEGRATED APPLICATION PLAN

One application and one repository evolve through these five work parts:
  PART 01: Sections 00-06 | M0-M1 | Foundation and historical QQQ data.

Read the P50 and data blocks at the beginning of THIS file, then APP Sections
00-05 and 28. Build the interfaces required by Sections 19-23 from day one.
All phases use the same immutable inputs, exact ledger and state-version gates.
All trades are local simulation events. Source archives remain read-only.
No-lookahead covers prices, contracts, OI, parameters, policies and training.
Use typed PENDING/UNSUPPORTED states during implementation; they are not a
substitute for completing and validating the scope explicitly supported here.

===============================================================================
START APPLICATION IMPLEMENTATION WORK PART 01/05 — FOUNDATION AND HISTORICAL DATA
===============================================================================
Assigned application chapters: 00-06. Implementation milestones: M0 and M1.
Deliverables: runnable desktop/headless scaffold, schemas/configuration, immutable
data catalog and audits, bounded as-of APIs, normalized cache and exact fixtures.
Gate: APP Section 28.1/28.2 and DT01-DT18; no guessed units/timestamps, no future
information in earlier snapshots, no source mutation. Construct the in-file
reference fixtures; there is no prerequisite to obtain an older implementation.

00. READ FIRST

00.1 Objective

Build one local Python desktop application for historical QQQ options trading.
The user advances a candlestick chart through time, selects actual historical
contracts from a synchronized chain, submits simulated buy/sell/close/roll orders,
and sees positions, cash, profit and loss, capital requirements, and the complete
universal P50 risk-envelope analysis for a draft trade or an existing campaign.
Use the user's QQQ Parquet and raw_theta archives as the market-data sources.
Use opening source block SRC-E in this same document as the controlling P50
specification, with the compatible mathematical requirements inherited from
opening source block SRC-D. The complete QQQ options and underlying OHLCV
instructions are opening source blocks SRC-C and SRC-B, respectively.

One replay clock, one authoritative event ledger, one consistent market snapshot,
one account state, and one selected validated model pack must serve every panel.
Simulation transactions affect local simulated money only. There is no live order
submission, cancellation, modification, broker login requirement, or account PII
requirement in this application.

00.2 How another LLM should use this document

1. Read the complete P50 instructions (SRC-E, SRC-D, P50-R, P50-M, SRC-A),
   then SRC-C and SRC-B, before the application plan.
2. Read integrated Sections 00-05 and the source-precedence decisions before
   coding. Every SRC-A through SRC-E reference means the corresponding
   opening block in this same document; no external instruction file is needed.
3. Implement the execution contracts in Sections 06-23 before polishing the UI.
4. Follow the gated milestones in Section 28. Every milestone belongs to this
   single application; an early chart prototype is not completion of the task.
5. Use Sections 25-27 as the validation and operational contract.
6. Resolve evidence gaps using Section 29. Do useful independent work first;
   do not invent unavailable inputs or ask the user to decide routine engineering.
7. Preserve requirement IDs, source references, version fields, and uncertainty
   distinctions in code, tests, manifests, and implementation progress reports.

MUST is a release requirement. SHOULD is a preference with a documented reason
required for deviation. MAY is optional. A stated engineering default is a design
choice, not a measured property of the dataset or a verified broker convention.
All monetary quantities are USD unless explicitly stated otherwise.

01. SOURCE INVENTORY, PRIORITY, AND CONFLICT RESOLUTIONS

01.1 Complete blocks and algorithms in this same document

SRC-E is the universal P50/campaign/risk-envelope block in the P50 instructions.
SRC-D is the full deadline reference block in the same P50 instructions.
P50-R supplies numerical reference algorithms, fixtures and deterministic defaults.
P50-M supplies pack construction, managed control, surrogate and query algorithms.
SRC-A supplies retained terminal/reporting requirements in the P50 instructions.
SRC-C and SRC-B are the complete options and underlying data blocks that follow.
APP-C in Section 30 supplies executable app/account/data/lifecycle/UI procedures.

E00-E21 mean SRC-E chapters; D01-D31 mean SRC-D chapters. SC, B, Q, T, M and U
identifiers keep their block-local scope. Unqualified Section numbers in APP
mean APP chapters. No source filename or missing script carries normative content.

All mathematical algorithms and implementation decisions needed to build the
supported app are specified here. Actual market rows, recorded contract metadata,
as-of parameter values and observed official closes are runtime inputs. The
Windows roots are configurable installation paths, not data validated during
this revision. Scanning real runtime files determines their coverage. Explicit
scenario profiles and typed missing-input states are defined here as well.

01.2 Binding priority for this application

PR-01. The user's current desktop, QQQ historical simulation request controls the
       product, market source, presentation, and requested deliverable.
PR-02. SRC-B and SRC-C govern all reads/joins/repairs of their respective archives.
PR-03. SRC-E governs P50, campaign targets, risk envelopes, model amortization,
       uncertainty, exact proposal verification, and model-release gates.
PR-04. SRC-D supplies compatible inherited mathematical/data-quality contracts.
PR-05. SRC-A supplies retained legacy reporting/navigation intent where compatible.
PR-06. This plan supplies application architecture and explicit integration choices.
PR-07. P50-R/P50-M are the canonical numerical implementation contract; APP-C
       fixes simulation-rule grouping/defaults. Validate generated code against
       the in-file fixtures. Historical rule data remains effective-dated.

Do not use a line in an older opening source block to undo an explicit resolution below.
Do not reinterpret an unavailable input as zero, supported, verified, or passed.

01.3 Explicit integration decisions

RES-01. QQQ replaces the SPY primary product for this app. The current request
        explicitly authorizes the QQQ historical feature contemplated by SRC-E.
        The P50 engine must be qualified for QQQ; renaming a SPY model is not enough.
RES-02. Historical replay replaces broker-only/live-only source restrictions and
        the old prohibition on historical replay. No present-day broker quote may
        repair a past quote. A future read-only broker adapter is optional only.
RES-03. A desktop interface replaces terminal-only presentation. Implement the
        three-stage reference CLI from SRC-D Section 03 and P50-R. Headless
        test/analysis commands remain available without GUI terminal prompts.
        All relevant engine behavior is defined here and built into this project.
RES-04. Current simulation time replaces real wall time when validating historical
        deadlines and unexpired contracts. All pricing uses actual contract expiry.
RES-05. Historical minute timestamps require an explicit replay timing/quality
        profile. Do not impose live 2-second spot freshness on 1-minute candles,
        or silently pretend minute data satisfies it. See Sections 07 and 15.
RES-06. Heavy Monte Carlo, high-resolution American pricing, global region solving,
        convergence pilots, and control training belong exclusively to explicit
        offline model construction/reference validation. No ordinary GUI query
        may invoke them. SRC-D's numerical procedures remain oracle requirements.
RES-07. The source's gross hold-P50 remains a distinct output. Actual simulated
        account P&L is net of booked costs. A campaign's chosen target basis is
        frozen explicitly; rolling never resets its original dollar target.
RES-08. Read-only broker restrictions forbid real trades, not local simulated
        ledger entries. Manual simulation buy/sell is required. Risk guidance
        does not place even simulated adjustments automatically by default.
RES-09. Preserve separate exact terminal payoff bounds and operational assignment/
        liquidation risk. A width-minus-credit calculation is not automatically
        a hard bound on total ETF trading loss or broker financing needs.
RES-10. The chart's base replay support is the supplied RTH archive. Official option
        sessions and available historical observations are different concepts.
        Missing 16:00-16:15 data may not be fabricated or silently called closed.
RES-11. The reference HOLD model retains Q_path GBM and frozen-leg IV. Historical
        data availability does not authorize fitting physical drift or silently
        changing this metric. Any empirically fitted managed-IV model is a
        separately versioned, leakage-controlled model specification.
RES-12. Keep sources immutable. Repair/downloading is a separate explicit operation
        subject to all SRC-B/SRC-C repair rules, never a replay side effect.
RES-13. The old 256 MiB/Termux budget is retained for legacy reference compatibility;
        it is not a desktop-wide RAM cap. The Windows hardware budget is Section 21.
RES-14. Legacy SPX forward/GTH, other products, OAuth/streaming, and Android details
        are specified in the P50 blocks as conditional adapter/compatibility requirements.
        They are not mandatory QQQ runtime dependencies or claimed implemented.
RES-15. The 390-slot 09:30 <= time < 16:00 canonical contract overrides older
        391-row/16:00-inclusive recollections. Early-close calendars narrow it.
RES-16. Tastytrade rules must be versioned. Distinguish rules actually effective
        on replay date from current rules deliberately applied to past prices.
        The latter is a labeled counterfactual, not exact historical emulation.
RES-17. A finite candidate search and finite pack domain cannot cover every
        conceivable option strategy. Every selection receives a computed supported
        result or a precise unsupported/insufficient-data result, never a fake P50.

02. USER REQUIREMENTS AND COMPLETE PRODUCT BEHAVIOR

APP-01. Display QQQ OHLC candles and a synchronized volume panel from SRC-B.
APP-02. Provide forward-step arrows, editable step amount/unit, session jump,
        jump-to-date/time, optional play/pause, and a visible New York clock.
APP-03. Keep chart aggregation interval separate from elapsed replay step size.
        A 15-minute chart may advance one minute; its current candle stays partial.
APP-04. Display the as-of QQQ options chain by expiration with calls/puts, bid/ask,
        computed mid, last known trade, volume, safely known OI, IV, Greeks, quality,
        and quote age. Never present unavailable fields as zeros.
APP-05. Buy/sell one or multiple contracts in a draft ticket; support signed integer
        quantities, limit/market simulation, close/reduce, cancellation, and rolls.
APP-06. Analyze a draft without changing account cash or positions. Analyze open
        positions with their actual simulated fill/fee/campaign history.
APP-07. Provide all SRC-E outputs: hold-P50, executed-target managed probability,
        original benchmarks, attainable joint risk envelope, feasibility,
        exact candidate verification, error/domain diagnostics, and probability
        shortfall when the original benchmark cannot be restored.
APP-08. Show realized/unrealized/total gross and net P&L, account equity, cash,
        settled funds, collateral, margin, buying power used/available, reserved
        order capital, and remaining campaign/account limits as distinct metrics.
APP-09. All panels describe the same committed state version. Never show new chart
        candles alongside an old probability without an obvious stale/as-of label.
APP-10. Every time jump processes intervening orders, fees, lifecycle events,
        settlements, constraints, and already-authorized exit instructions.
APP-11. Persist a resumable session, append-only event history, snapshots, exact
        model/rule identities, branches, and reproducible result exports.
APP-12. Explain missing data, unavailable model support, and uncertain broker rules
        at the affected action/panel. Browsing valid candles remains possible.
APP-13. Support repeatable A/B exploration by separate branches. Rewinding cannot
        retain cash or open positions earned from a discarded future.
APP-14. Work offline after dependencies, local data, reference metadata, and a
        validated model pack are installed. Network access is not a replay input.
APP-15. Provide an implementation-grade setup guide, data audit report, tested
        Windows package, complete tests, model validation report, and benchmark
        report when implementation is eventually complete.

02.1 Default scope and safe expansion

Primary product: standard QQQ American, physically delivered equity/ETF options
with verified contract metadata and normally 100 shares per contract. Never infer
100 for adjusted contracts. Currency USD. Whole option contracts only.
Primary analytics domain: supported finite-max-profit, defined-risk, same-expiry
combinations, especially short-dated verticals, iron condors, butterflies, and
iron butterflies. Discovery must not assume the data contains only 4/5 DTE.
The chain can display other available contracts. Trade accounting, pricing, and
P50 support are separately declared capabilities. A long call's unlimited maximum
profit makes a 50%-of-maximum target undefined, even if the position is tradable.
Calendar/diagonal/overlapping-expiration/adjusted-deliverable and stock-exposure
analytics need explicit additional qualification. Do not quietly flatten them
into one expiry. Close-then-open rolls can be supported before overlapping rolls.

Account default: a configurable USD 5,000 initial-cash simulator using the
OPTIONS_COLLATERAL_QQQ_V1 cash-collateral account defined completely in APP-C01
and APP-C06. Its supported tastytrade-style component economics and conservative
permissions are explicit; full Reg T/SMA behavior is not asserted. Starting cash
is distinct from the USD 5,000 buying-power cap inherited from SRC-E. Campaign loss cap is USD 1,000.
Do not reinterpret the BP cap as buying USD 5,000 of option premium or as a loss
budget. Initial cash may be changed in session creation and is frozen thereafter.
Cash accounts and other account types have separate rule profiles. Portfolio
margin is unsupported until an independently validated engine exists.

03. CHOSEN IMPLEMENTATION ARCHITECTURE AND TECHNOLOGY

03.1 Selected stack

Use a native PySide6 Qt Widgets desktop application, pyqtgraph for candlesticks
and volume, Python domain/application services, NumPy/SciPy float64 numerical
kernels, PyArrow for Parquet/csv conversion, DuckDB for bounded analytical scans,
and standard-library SQLite for session/event persistence. Use zoneinfo and tzdata
for timezones. Use dataclasses plus explicit schema validation, Decimal for exact
prices/strikes/cash input, and integer minor-unit ledger postings after documented
rounding. Keep dataframes outside the event/valuation hot path.

PySide6's model/view API is appropriate for a virtualized options chain; Qt models
must be updated on their owning GUI thread. Workers return data, not widget
operations. [APP-C09] pyqtgraph's graphics-item API supports a custom cached
candlestick item. [APP-C10] Parquet projection/filter pushdown supports bounded
reads; gzip CSV remains sequential until normalized into an optional mirror.
[APP-C10]

Development baseline: a dedicated 64-bit Python 3.12 environment, chosen as an
engineering compatibility starting point, without changing the user's system
Python 3.13. At milestone M0, verify available binary wheels and supported Windows
versions; pin exact tested package versions in a lockfile. A compatibility-driven
baseline update is allowed only with a recorded reason and full regression gates.
Do not claim untested dependency versions work. No web server, browser runtime,
cloud service, actual broker connection, or GPU is needed for normal use.

03.2 Layers and authority

Presentation: Qt panels, table models, chart rendering, user commands.
Application: ReplayController, CommandDispatcher, AnalysisCoordinator, SessionService.
Domain: contracts, orders, fills, cash ledger, lots, campaigns, account rules.
Market data: catalog, adapters, normalization, as-of views, immutable snapshots.
Broker simulation: execution, commissions, collateral, margin, lifecycle, settlement.
Risk inference: pack loader, features, hold operator, value operator, envelopes.
Offline research: pricing/transition/oracle/training/validation/build commands.
Persistence: SQLite event log, checkpoints, manifests, snapshots, exports.

Only ReplayController/CommandDispatcher may mutate authoritative simulation state.
Numerical functions accept frozen inputs and return immutable outputs.
GUI objects cannot directly alter a position or advance one panel independently.
The online risk package must not import offline trainers/oracles. A dependency
test enforces this boundary, beyond merely hiding a slow function behind a flag.

03.3 Processes and asynchronous work

GUI main thread handles interaction and applies published state bundles.
A serialized simulation worker owns state transitions and the SQLite writer.
A bounded I/O pool loads/decompresses independent partitions and warms caches.
A long-lived inference worker loads one pack and handles cancellable fast queries.
Offline build runs in a separate process/command with resumable worker batches.
Use explicit spawn-compatible entry points, serializable requests, and Windows
freeze_support where needed. ProcessPoolExecutor workers require an importable
main module; do not create recursive process pools on import. [APP-C11]

04. PROJECT LAYOUT, FILESYSTEM BOUNDARIES, AND CONFIGURATION

Suggested project root on the user's PC: D:\QQQ\optionsimulation
Authoritative underlying source: D:\QQQ\OHLCV_daily
Authoritative options source: D:\QQQ\raw_theta
All paths are configurable. Sources are opened read-only and kept outside the
application's writable cache/output trees. Changing a path triggers a catalog
audit; it is not permission to modify or repair the source.

Suggested code modules:
  pyproject.toml, dependency lockfile, README.md, CHANGELOG.md
  src/qqq_sim/__main__.py, app.py, config.py
  src/qqq_sim/domain/contracts.py, units.py, events.py, state.py, errors.py
  src/qqq_sim/data/catalog.py, underlying.py, theta.py, normalization.py
  src/qqq_sim/data/asof.py, quality.py, mirror.py, parameters.py, metadata.py
  src/qqq_sim/replay/clock.py, calendar.py, controller.py, ordering.py, branches.py
  src/qqq_sim/broker/orders.py, execution.py, fees.py, margin.py, risk_checks.py
  src/qqq_sim/broker/lifecycle.py, settlement.py, rule_registry.py
  src/qqq_sim/accounting/ledger.py, lots.py, marks.py, campaigns.py, equity.py
  src/qqq_sim/risk/contracts.py, payoff.py, features.py, pack_loader.py
  src/qqq_sim/risk/pricing_fast.py, hold.py, policy.py, envelope.py, verify.py
  src/qqq_sim/risk/offline/build.py, design.py, pricing_oracle.py, hit_oracle.py
  src/qqq_sim/risk/offline/control_oracle.py, train.py, validate.py, export.py
  src/qqq_sim/services/commands.py, snapshots.py, analysis.py, exports.py
  src/qqq_sim/storage/schema.py, events.py, checkpoints.py, migrations.py
  src/qqq_sim/ui/main_window.py, chart.py, chain.py, ticket.py, portfolio.py
  src/qqq_sim/ui/risk_panel.py, account_panel.py, history.py, diagnostics.py
  tests/unit, tests/integration, tests/property, tests/numerical, tests/ui
  tests/fixtures/synthetic, tests/fixtures/reference, tools, docs, packaging

Suggested writable runtime directories:
  runtime/catalog, runtime/cache/normalized, runtime/cache/snapshots
  runtime/sessions/<session_id>, runtime/model_packs/<pack_id>
  runtime/builds/<build_id>, runtime/results, runtime/logs, runtime/backups
Default them under the project or a user-chosen application-data directory.
Never write into either authoritative archive merely because it is nearby.

Configuration groups, validated and versioned:
  data: roots, mirror path, audit strictness, timezone, schema mappings.
  replay: bar convention, start/end, step, calendar, data-gap behavior.
  account: initial cash, account type, rule-basis mode, dated fee/margin profiles.
  execution: next-event policy, spread fill policy, slippage, liquidity evidence.
  campaign: target_fraction, target_basis, BP/loss limits, deadlines, roll rules.
  analytics: pack path, replay quote quality, candidate/work limits, uncertainty.
  resources: CPU workers, BLAS limits, memory/cache budgets.
  persistence: checkpoint cadence, export location, backup/retention policy.

Campaign hard cutoff and maximum roll-extension allowance must be explicit before
the first campaign is opened. Do not silently select an unlimited horizon. The UI
may prefill a finite suggested cutoff but must show its exact New York timestamp
in the ticket before submission. This is one objective field, not a repeated
model-configuration questionnaire. A pack-domain horizon does not itself choose
the user's campaign deadline.

05. DOMAIN OBJECTS, UNITS, IDENTIFIERS, AND STATE OWNERSHIP

05.1 Canonical types

ContractKey:
  underlying, root, expiration_date, right, strike_decimal, multiplier,
  deliverable_id, exercise_style, physical_or_cash_settlement, currency,
  last_trade_at, economic_fixing_at, listing_evidence, metadata_version.
  Keep administrative expiry, exercise cutoff, and payment date separate.
  A derived local contract ID is not falsely labeled a verified OCC symbol.

SourceRecord:
  dataset_type, path, source_fingerprint, session_date, expiry, row_number,
  source_event_at, normalized_minute, available_at, timestamp_precision,
  original_units, mapping_version, source_sequence, quality_flags.

ReplayClock:
  session_id, branch_id, visible_bar_label_ny, decision_at_ny, decision_at_utc,
  event_sequence, phase, next_event_at, calendar_version, coverage_status.

MarketSnapshot:
  snapshot_id, state_version, asof, exclusive_event_cutoff, QQQ spot/spot_source,
  quotes_by_contract, vendor/model IV and Greeks separately, known OI,
  known volume/last trades, rates/carry/dividend metadata, product/calendar,
  quality/freshness/dispersion, data fingerprints, model-input profile.

Order/OrderLeg:
  id, campaign_id, branch_id, submitted_at, submitted_sequence, submitted_snapshot,
  intended action, exact legs, signed quantity, opening/closing effect,
  order type, limit/debit-credit convention, TIF, latency policy, status,
  reserved_capital, fill_policy_id, optional contingent/exit instructions.

Fill:
  id, order_id, event_at, sequence, contract, signed quantity, price,
  multiplier, source quote/trade IDs, fill assumption, fee-event references.

LedgerEvent and CashPosting:
  immutable event ID, economic time, known/processed time, accounting category,
  signed cash, cash bucket, instrument quantity changes, gross/net components,
  settlement date, campaign allocation, source event IDs, rule version.

PositionLot:
  contract, signed remaining quantity, opening fill IDs, allocated basis/fees,
  close/exercise links, campaign owner. Net account positions derive from lots.

Campaign:
  id, initial_fill_ids, original gross/net M0, frozen target_basis/H0,
  p0_hold/p0_policy with objective/model/snapshot identity or explicit unknown,
  T0, active_deadline, T_hard, roll rules, all events/fees/stock obligations,
  open lots, realized P&L, target_realized status, constraints and certificate.

AccountState:
  economic_cash, settled_cash, receivables/payables, option/stock inventory,
  long/short market values, equity, initial/maintenance requirement,
  gross_collateral, options_BP, stock_BP, working_order_reserve, fees,
  margin/cash restrictions, campaign-risk aggregation, last valid valuation.

RiskRequest/Result:
  complete input hash, exact legs/campaign ledger hash, snapshot/state IDs,
  requested event/deadlines, pack/rule/feature identity, uncertainty status,
  probabilities or null+reason, feasible candidates, exact constraint evidence.

05.2 Identity, money, and unit rules

ID-01. Positive signed position quantity means long; negative means short.
ID-02. Positive cash flow adds money; premium purchase posts negative cash.
ID-03. Premiums/strikes are per quoted underlying unit; multiply by contract
       multiplier and quantity exactly once. Do not multiply fees twice.
ID-04. IV=0.25 and probability=0.75 mean 25% IV and 75% probability. Rates use
       normalized annual continuously compounded decimals for pricing.
ID-05. Store both raw and normalized vendor units. Vendor theta/vega units must
       be established; an unlabeled vega per 1.0 volatility is not per 1% vol.
ID-06. Use Decimal for order validation and exact cash formulas; fees may need
       sub-cent accrual before the schedule's defined posting/rounding point.
       Store full accrual plus booked cents. Do not round every partial fragment
       independently unless the broker rule/fixture says to do so.
ID-07. Use float64 for pricing/inference; convert into accounting money only at
       explicit boundaries. Price tick validation uses the product rule profile.
ID-08. Canonical sorted exact-leg fingerprints distinguish root, expiry, style,
       deliverable, strike, multiplier, and signed quantity. No fuzzy matching.
ID-09. Snapshot/hash identity includes all rates, IVs, prices, timing semantics,
       rules, fees, target basis, calendar, pack, and ledger inputs that can
       affect a result. A result from another state is never silently reused.
ID-10. Null+reason means unavailable. Zero means an actual computed/observed zero.
       JSON infinities use explicit flags/strings, not nonstandard NaN/Infinity.

06. QQQ DATA INGESTION, CATALOG, VALIDATION, AND CACHING

06.1 Catalog and immutable sources

DATA-01. Scan real filenames/metadata on first launch and refresh/invalidate when
         a directory changes. Never assume historical date ranges, partition
         counts, expiry patterns, or 4/5-DTE-only coverage.
DATA-02. Underlying key is filename session date; canonical filename is
         QQQ_1m_YYYY-MM-DD.parquet. Duplicate files for one date are an error.
DATA-03. Options partition key is (data_type, session_date, expiration). Parse
         embedded YYYYMMDD and exp=YYYY-MM-DD despite prefix/copy suffix variations.
         Duplicate logical partitions fail unless an explicit recorded resolution
         policy was requested. Do not select whichever file is newest/largest.
DATA-04. Catalog columns include source path, type, dates, size, mtime, strong hash
         when computed, schema fingerprint, normalization version, row/contract
         counts, earliest/latest raw times, quality status, and mirror identity.
         Content counts require a read; label unscanned metadata as unknown.
DATA-05. Missing folders/files/schema/sessions produce diagnostics. No network or
         synthetic/demo prices substitute for missing production data.
DATA-06. Keep optional normalized Parquet mirrors separate from raw sources.
         Mirror provenance includes raw hash and canonicalizer version; changed
         bytes/schema/units invalidate it. Publish by atomic temp-to-final replace.
         A mirror is a derived cache, never a second source of truth.

06.2 Underlying canonicalizer

Preferred timestamp regular_time; accepted fallback names are datetime, timestamp,
time, date. Required OHLCV fields: open, high, low, close, volume. Normalize aware
timestamps to America/New_York before deriving local date/minute. Interpret naive
timestamps as New York, never UTC. Preserve exact event metadata and a timezone-
aware canonical instant even when a naive wall-clock key is used for joins.

Validate timestamp belongs to filename date, all OHLC finite, volume finite and
nonnegative, high >= low, high >= max(open,close), low <= min(open,close).
Sort strictly. Duplicate timestamps are errors, not silently last-wins.
Filter 09:30 <= local time < 16:00 and the actual early-close session boundary.
Ignore volume==0 rows in canonical production replay. Alternative placeholder
treatment requires a separately named research profile. Do not synthesize 390 rows.
Quarantine invalid rows/partitions in a report, not by modifying original files.

The authoritative and required price source for spot, ATM, moneyness, IV inversion,
scenario starting
state, and underlying returns is the latest legitimately revealed valid QQQ
Parquet close. Keep raw_theta underlying_price/underlying_timestamp diagnostic
only. If underlying data is missing, do not use that embedded price as repair.

06.3 Options canonicalizer

Canonical folders: greeks, ohlcv, trades, oi_before, oi. Keep each source separate.
Greeks is primary historical bid/ask/IV/Greeks source. Preserve all provided
vendor fields, including higher Greeks, d1/d2, dual Greeks, iv_error, and raw
underlying references; these are not assumed present or required for every view.

Recognize aliases exactly as SRC-C:
  timestamp/datetime/regular_time/time/quote_time/date_time;
  ms_of_day/msofday/milliseconds_of_day;
  strike/strike_price/strikeprice/strike_milli/strike_millis;
  right/option_type/type/put_call/cp;
  corresponding bid_price/ask_price/last_price, with price as possible last;
  implied_vol/implied_volatility/impliedvolatility/iv;
  underlying_price/underlying_px/underlying;
  open_interest/openinterest/oi.

Construct timestamps from validated date plus milliseconds-of-day when necessary.
Conflicting redundant fields are errors/diagnostics, not silently preferred values.
Normalize right to C/P. Preserve raw strikes; resolve a plausible positive divisor,
normally 1 or 1000 but not limited to them. Use past/known authoritative QQQ spot,
schema metadata and consistency checks; ambiguous units stop the affected data.
Do not use the future full-day price range to resolve an intraday uncertainty.
Determine IV decimal versus percent from validated schema/data evidence; record
one conversion, never mix 25 and 0.25. Required fields depend on capability: a
quote can be displayed with unknown IV, but P50 cannot silently assume one.

Validate each option row against the partition session, expiration and QQQ
contract identity. Record filename-derived fields; disagreement between row and
filename is an error. Unexpected symbols, roots, expiries or dates are excluded
with a diagnostic, not admitted merely because the timestamp passes a cutoff.

Keep original sub-minute timestamps for order/age semantics. Floor to minutes only
after parsing. Preserve the legacy 00:00:00 selector meaning ALL session times for
Greeks/IV/option OHLCV; the new API should expose an explicit whole_session flag
so midnight is never accidentally interpreted as a valuation request.
whole_session is an ingestion/offline/legacy-adapter selector. Replay-facing
services must still apply the current visibility cutoff and must never expose
an unrestricted full-session frame to chart, execution, accounting or P50.

Normalize semantic aliases oi_yesterday -> oi_before, oi_today -> oi, iv -> the
implied_vol view of greeks. OI remains a daily snapshot, never a faux intraday
series created by applying a clock filter. oi_before alone is eligible for known
intraday OI. Same-session oi is restricted to explicitly post-session analysis,
after its actual availability is established, and cannot enter earlier features.

06.4 Quote/event validity and as-of lookup

First restrict to exact dataset, historical session, expiration and contract;
then apply RTH/calendar and visibility rules. Deduplication/last-event selection
operates only within that logical contract/time identity, never across strikes,
rights, expiries or sessions. A 16:00 event is a separate close/EOD-boundary record
in the canonical RTH view, not a 391st minute. Prior-session carry requires a
separate explicit policy and keeps its original timestamp/age.

Valid quote: finite bid/ask, bid >= 0, ask > 0, ask >= bid. Zero bid is allowed but
flagged. Crossed markets are invalid, never repaired by swapping prices.
Use the latest valid known event, ordered by source timestamp then sequence when
available. Identical duplicates may collapse deterministically with audit counts;
conflicting events with identical timestamps and no reliable order are ambiguous.
Do not invent event ordering from file concatenation. For a minute snapshot,
retain the last valid event in that minute under an established ordering rule.

No future nearest-neighbor joins. Preserve selected timestamp, age, precision,
current/stale/missing/invalid state, and the source of every selected field.
An invalid latest quote is visible as an invalid update; a prior valid quote may
remain a stale valuation reference but does not become a fresh fill opportunity.
Relative spread=(ask-bid)/mid when mid>0. No implicit minimum OI or strategy-specific
spread/freshness screen is added. Apply an expressly configured threshold exactly.

Option OHLCV and trades are filtered before aggregation. Contract volume is not
QQQ volume. Choose stored OHLCV volume as the default chain source when available;
use a labeled trade-derived alternative only under an explicit source policy.
Never sum both. Derive trade volume as sum(size) only for eligible conditions and
known events; preserve canceled/corrected trade semantics if provided. Unknown
condition meanings remain a capability limitation, not assumed valid executions.

06.5 Historical contract universe and data gaps

Only contracts listed/known as of the replay time may be selected. Where listing
metadata is absent, use first eligible observed contract quote as conservative
availability evidence and label the universe as observation-limited. A contract
appearing later that day must not appear earlier because the whole gzip was read.
Future expirations already quoted are valid; future quote values are not.

Distinguish exchange holiday, known closure, absent archive session, absent expiry
partition, absent contract, missing minute, invalid observation, and truncated
file. A missing data day is not automatically the preceding/next trading day.
Coverage indicators may describe the archive's span as operational metadata, but
future price/volume/liquidity/contract outcomes cannot influence selection.

Strict replay pauses before crossing an unresolved gap needed by open positions
or pending orders. It offers a diagnostic and an explicitly labeled inspection
continuation with affected execution/analytics disabled. Never assume an order
did not fill, a stop did not trigger, or no assignment occurred inside unknown data.
At archive end, keep positions unresolved and mark the session incomplete unless
the user previously selected a declared terminal liquidation convention.

06.6 Read performance

Read only needed sessions, expirations and columns; cache normalized read-only
arrays and binary-searchable time offsets. CSV row filters do not avoid gzip
decompression. Decompress once per fingerprint, optionally materialize a sorted
columnar mirror, and reuse it across repeated replay steps. Cache schema mappings
but revalidate when mapping fails. Parallelize a small number of independent NVMe
partition reads after measuring; HDD behavior may prefer sequential reads.
Prefetch into an internal cache is permitted; exposing it to as-of snapshots,
policy features, indicators, or fills before availability is forbidden.

06.7 Conditional repair tooling

Normal app operation never repairs the archive. If explicitly requested later,
the repair command must implement every SRC-C repair instruction: Theta Terminal
v3 via the configured local endpoint (historically localhost:25503), discovered
day/expiry mapping, 1-minute/full-session retrieval, plan-aware concurrency,
single-shot full-day /v3/option/history/greeks/all with trade_greeks/all fallback,
start/end and approximately 98% expected coverage integrity against the actual
calendar session (including early closes), initial global HTTP concurrency 8 and
day concurrency 8 subject to plan limits, complete trade files,
validated trade-to-OHLCV aggregation, .part then atomic publish, correct oi_before
versus oi timing, forward publication probing without an arbitrary short cap,
resumable manifests/unresolved reports (including the source stream_results.log,
manifest_events.jsonl, manifest_state.csv, unresolved.csv conventions), jittered
backoff for retryable 408/425/429/500/502/503/504/570/571 responses,
validation on resume, preservation of known-good files, and explicit failures.
SRC-B establishes no canonical external underlying vendor; do not invent one or
substitute Theta embedded spot to rebuild the Parquet archive.

==============================================================================
END APPLICATION IMPLEMENTATION WORK PART 01/05
Do not report this part complete until its Section 28 milestones,
corresponding full tests and the above completion gate are evidenced.

==============================================================================
START APPLICATION IMPLEMENTATION WORK PART 02/05 — REPLAY ENGINE, SYNCHRONIZED DESKTOP UI, AND SIMULATED TRADING
==============================================================================
Assigned application chapters: 07-11.
Implementation milestones: M2 and M3 (see Section 28).

Objective: Build a causal replay clock and shared state publication protocol; display candles, options chain and tickets; implement simulated buy/sell/close/roll events, immutable ledger, positions, gross/net P&L and campaign initialization.

Required deliverables: Functional synchronized chart/volume/time controls and chain; deterministic replay stepping and event ordering; drafts/orders and fill/cancel/reverse/close/roll flows; accountable lots/fees placeholders under explicit test profiles; account, positions and history panels; persisted state/event schema and reproducible tests.

Completion gate: Meet Section 28.3 M2 and Section 28.4 M3 gates: DT19-DT20 and AT01-AT14; identical economics under small steps versus a long advance; no future bars/quotes; no duplicated fills/fees; H0 frozen on valid opening fills; crash recovery balances reconcile. Unverified broker charge profiles remain explicitly nonproduction.

BINDING RULE: This work-package checkpoint supplements the full in-file
instructions. Apply Section 01's precedence and the concrete P50-R/P50-M/APP-C
algorithms. Continue in the same codebase, preserve all passed checks, and never
expose future information to an as-of decision.
