START APPLICATION IMPLEMENTATION WORK PART 04/05 — OFFLINE P50 MODEL PACK, MANAGED POLICY, AND RISK ENVELOPE
==============================================================================
Assigned application chapters: 16-18.
Implementation milestones: M6 and M7 (see Section 28).

Objective: Build and validate the reusable offline P50 model pack and fast query path. Add managed success probability, exact adjustment verification, feasible risk envelopes, strict model-domain checking and campaign loss/BP certification.

Required deliverables: Reproducible B0-B9 offline build with checkpointing, validated pricing and first-hit oracles, held-out oracle/policy fixtures, immutable model pack and loader, batched HOLD/value inference, practical candidate engine, envelope/verification APIs and user-visible uncertainty/abstention reasons.

Completion gate: Meet Section 28.7 M6 and Section 28.8 M7 gates: PT15-PT30 and applicable SRC-E/SRC-D numerical gates; tested model-pack reload, validated error/coverage maps and policy rollouts, zero accepted known constraint violations, measured fast query path, no offline Monte Carlo/PDE/region-solving/training invoked by ordinary GUI actions.

BINDING RULE: This work-package checkpoint supplements the full in-file
instructions. Apply Section 01's precedence and the concrete P50-R/P50-M/APP-C
algorithms. Continue in the same codebase, preserve all passed checks, and never
expose future information to an as-of decision.
==============================================================================

16. OFFLINE MODEL-PACK CONSTRUCTION AND VALIDATION

16.1 Pack manifest and compatibility

A pack contains product/contract/clock/monitoring/rate-dividend/dynamics profiles,
pricing and inverse-pricing bases, transition operators, HOLD probability operator,
managed state-action/value operator, uncertainty/calibration surfaces, domain
detector, normalization/feature schema, action grammar, execution/fee/risk model,
training design/seeds, dependencies/hardware precision, validation evidence,
source fingerprints, coverage, artifact hashes, and build status.
The loader rejects unknown versions, changed bytes, unsupported product/calendar/
objective/execution/cost regimes, or validation failure. No untrusted pickle
execution; use declarative metadata and safe tensor/array formats. Memory-map
large immutable arrays. One selected production pack serves normal queries.

Changing an ordinary market input within the supported domain does not rebuild.
Changing mathematical assumptions/action grammar/feature schema/cost behavior
outside supported pack inputs requires an explicitly built and validated new pack.
Archive old packs for reproducibility; do not rewrite results in place.

16.2 Required build sequence B0-B9

B0 Freeze model specification, versions, calendars, actions, costs, risk semantics,
   observation schedules, domain, precision, seeds and reproducibility manifest.
B1 Generate stratified/adaptive normalized state/portfolio designs over moneyness,
   time, spot/IV/skew/term/carry, target gap, risk budget, payoff shapes and market
   closures. Include near expiry, narrow/tangent/disjoint regions and tail states.
   Hold out complete strike sets and payoff morphologies, not only random rows.
B2 Build verified American QQQ pricing bases and analytic validation cases. Check
   bounds, exercise obstacle, grid/time/domain convergence and inverse-IV support.
B3 Build normalized GBM/joint-factor transition operators with positive probability
   and mass conservation. Preserve correlations, closed gaps and terminal fixing.
B4 Generate high-fidelity first-hit oracle labels with all target-region topology,
   boundaries, monitoring and terminal companions. Independently compare transport/
   probability-PDE/reference Monte Carlo and P50-R's independent fixtures.
B5 Fit ONE fast HOLD operator using exact-state payoff-shape features; constrain
   probabilities and temporal monotonicity where the event is fixed. Model hard
   session/expiry discontinuities explicitly. Save error maps and abstention rules.
B6 Generate supported exact feasible actions with full cost/account/lifecycle
   transitions. No free Greek targets, fractional contracts or unattainable fills.
B7 Solve/fit constrained finite-horizon state-action continuation values; evaluate
   the returned policy on independent paths and realistic execution assumptions.
B8 Certify within a stated domain: sampling, spatial/time/grid, surrogate, policy,
   model-form and quote/execution uncertainty separately. Stress limits/assignment.
B9 Save immutable pack, reload its actual bytes in a clean process and run release
   gates. Failed validation means failed pack, not "production with lower accuracy".

16.3 Inherited reference numerical contract

Preserve SRC-D oracle defaults as starting settings: 200,000 independent production
paths; PCG64 plus SeedSequence batch streams; seed 20261008; initial batch 4096;
20,000 independent coupled pilot paths; monitoring grids 60,30,15,7.5,3.75,1.875
seconds; the paired gained-hit probability's upper uncertainty bound must be
<=0.0025 on two successive refinements, not merely its observed mean; no hidden
production Brownian-bridge correction. The American 1601-node initial grid is
a starting grid, not an accuracy certificate. Build tolerance manifests may
strengthen work explicitly; never silently weaken an acceptance gate.

Couple fine/coarse Brownian increments properly; merely setting the same seed is
not coupling. Even a passed adjacent-grid test is not a rigorous continuous-time
error bound; unresolved refinement remains NUMERICAL_CONVERGENCE_UNRESOLVED.
Freeze resolution before fixed-N production, report Wilson sampling
intervals, and preserve conditional hit-time/terminal atoms. Discard/resume partial
batches correctly. No path clipping, dropped narrow target region, fake root,
or silent European replacement for American legs. Common-vol analytical endpoint
and probability-PDE benchmarks are independent checks. All oracle caveats and
the complete formulas remain in opening source block SRC-D.

16.4 Managed dynamics and historical calibration

Start with a declared frozen-IV/sensitivity policy model consistent with the
legacy HOLD definition, or a separately approved/versioned joint spot/IV-factor
law. Current IV/skew/term structure is an input. A single chain does not identify
future IV dynamics. If the dynamic model is not defensible, mark it unsupported;
do not invent mean reversion/correlation to print an attractive probability.

Synthetic theoretical training can be performed now and used on past dates,
because it does not learn realized future historical outcomes. If actual QQQ
history is used to fit IV transitions, liquidity, scalers, hyperparameters, feature
selection or model/domain selection, historical-as-of evaluations require training
and knowledge cutoffs before the evaluated period. Purge overlapping campaign/
label horizons and embargo through the longest allowed roll cutoff. Use walk-
forward, session/campaign grouped validation. Post-hoc fully trained replay must
be labeled research and cannot claim historical point-in-time predictive validity.

16.4.1 Concrete baseline managed forecast profile

Use FROZEN_KNOWN_SURFACE_QQQ_V1 as the initial managed-model specification to
qualify, not an undisclosed dynamic-IV fit. It has one action-independent market
law per snapshot/campaign: GBM r-q with one sigma_managed selected by a fixed,
versioned ATM-term anchor rule relative to the campaign hard horizon, BEFORE
candidate enumeration. A suitable initial explicit rule is the valid known expiry
nearest T_hard (tie: later expiry), within the pack's supported horizon-proxy
domain. Record anchor expiry/proxy mismatch; no valid supported anchor means no
managed result. Do not select sigma_managed from each proposed trade's expiry.
All Q(s,a), future decision nodes and joint-account simulations share this law.
Legacy HOLD may use its own expiry-specific path-IV convention; label that event/
model difference and do not compare their numbers as if identical objectives.

Freeze the entire known compatible strike/expiry IV surface at query t0, with
validated within-support interpolation only. A contract keeps its assigned
strike/expiry scalar IV as remaining time decays. Price newly chosen future
legs from this same surface and actual remaining lifetime. The baseline future
action universe contains only contracts already evidenced as listed/observed at
t0, until their actual stop times; no future-listed strikes or actual future
archive quotes enter a forecast. Later real replay queries may incorporate newly
revealed contracts as new current information. More general listing/smile/IV
transition laws require new explicitly qualified profiles.

For baseline forecast execution, use the known contract's t0 half-spread h_i as
a stated constant-dollar spread proxy: modeled bid=max(0,v_i-h_i), ask=v_i+h_i,
rounded outward to verified ticks. This is a modeling assumption, not historical
future bid/ask. Preserve any resulting cost/portfolio-bound problem; do not clamp
an unfavorable package price to its width to make a candidate feasible.
Default policy redecision grid is 60 eligible seconds; action eligibility begins
at the next decision opportunity under the declared latency. Qualified full-ratio
fills at those modeled sides are a size-unverified baseline scenario. Separate
bounded adverse-spread/no-fill/partial-fill/latency/assignment stresses are required
for risk certification. A no-fill probability cannot be called empirically
calibrated without data. If this full surface/action/fill specification cannot
be validated, managed values/envelopes remain unsupported while HOLD may operate.

16.5 Accuracy gates

Inherited development targets, not measured results:
  HOLD absolute error <=0.01 on at least 99% of representative supported held-out
  states/position shapes; disclose worst cases and tighten near decisions.
  Managed probability error target <=0.02 against independent rollouts of the
  actual returned policy; quantify regret versus a stronger offline benchmark.
  Zero accepted known deterministic BP/loss violations in the validated scope.
  Normal queries in seconds on the target Windows PC, with measured p50/p95/p99.

Do not call a surrogate error range a Monte Carlo confidence interval. Include
sampling/grid/basis/policy/model-form/execution uncertainty separately. Reject
failing domain cells and test that the detector abstains. A small collection of
easy verticals cannot establish arbitrary-portfolio accuracy.

17. RISK-ENVELOPE CONTROL MODEL AND EXACT PROPOSAL CHECKING

17.1 State and feasible action space

Use the exact state: market/quotes/IV surface/rates/calendar, full option+stock
legs, campaign cashflows/original objective/remaining horizon, account/risk/BP,
execution assumptions and model identity. Compressed features include a
multi-node normalized mark/payoff curve, IV-shock response, expiry structure,
cash-target gap, realized history and remaining budgets. {Delta, IV, DTE} alone
is not sufficient. Check coverage for path-dependent/mixed-expiry portfolios.

Required action grammar: HOLD, FULL_CLOSE, PARTIAL_CLOSE, REDUCE_RISK,
ADD_DEFINED_RISK_HEDGE, REPOSITION_STRIKES, ROLL_EXPIRATION, EXIT_STOP.
Reentry is allowed only when declared within the same campaign rules. Exact
candidate quantities are integers; listed/observed-as-of contract constraints
apply. Remove duplicates by full poststate identity, including cash/fees/timing.

17.2 Bellman/value semantics

Let Q(s,a) be probability of net realized target success after feasible action a
and subsequent stated policy, with full execution/cost/account transitions.
Success state is absorbing value 1, genuine failure/deadline value 0. An immediate
close has value 1 only if its actual modeled realization meets H0; otherwise its
flat state may continue if reentry and time/risk remain. Closed sessions permit
market evolution but no nonexistent option trades.

Compute continuation from the shared transition/pricing/account model. The agent
chooses future actions from information available then, not hidden realized path.
Future contract availability and fills in rollouts are modeled assumptions
conditioned on current known universe/listing rules, not copied future archive
strikes/prices. Preserve policy identity and fixed redecision interval in tests.

A managed HOLD action means defer trading until the next policy decision,
then continue that managed policy. Q(s,HOLD) is generally not the legacy
unchanged-position gross first-hit probability. V_search is compared with its
own feasible managed HOLD Q, never assumed to exceed the different HOLD event.
EXIT_STOP records a stop decision and closes/hedges through actual supported
execution mechanics; it cannot set remaining shares/options to zero or erase
cash obligations. If exits do not fill, exposures and risk remain in state.

17.3 Envelope definition

For bounded searched feasible actions A(s):
  V_search(s)=max over a in A(s) of Q(s,a).
  p_required=min(p0_policy,V_search(s)) when p0_policy is known and comparable.
  Envelope(s) consists of post-action profiles with
      Q(s,a)>=p_required-epsilon_decision,
      exact BP/loss/lifecycle checks passing,
      model domain and decision-confidence checks passing.

epsilon_decision comes from validated numerical/approximation/execution error,
not an arbitrary user-chosen percentage to make a trade pass. If the initial
policy benchmark is unknown, show a near-best supported envelope relative to
V_search and say initial-probability preservation cannot be assessed.
If A(s) is empty, return V_search=null with NO_FEASIBLE_SUPPORTED_ACTION;
do not evaluate max(empty) or use zero as an unavailable-probability sentinel.
Keep separately computable theoretical HOLD and remediation information visible.
Use the source's V_star terminology only with the visible qualification "best
among searched supported feasible actions". It is not a universal global optimum.

Represent joint correlated regions, potentially disjoint/nonconvex. Each branch
must have at least one valid exact representative construction. Independent min/
max Delta/Gamma/Vega/Theta projections are descriptive bounds, not a Cartesian
box authorizing any combination. Full exact proposal checks remain mandatory.

17.4 Search budget and ranking

Generate bounded candidates from current positions/available expiries and relevant
strike neighborhoods, permitted action grammar and payoff-risk directions.
Prune invalid data, contracts, risk, BP and horizon before batched model inference.
Engineering starting ceiling: 2,000 candidates per interactive envelope query,
configurable within a measured CPU budget. Record candidate counts by family,
pruned reasons, evaluated set, capped/truncated status, and unsupported coverage.
Do not enumerate every possible multi-expiry combination or imply that pruning
proved no better action exists outside the search.

Rank success probability on its declared event; among statistically/numerically
indistinguishable candidates prefer lower costs/tail risk and simpler exposure,
then report expected net profit/time-in-trade/capital use as secondary quantities
only when computed under the same validated model. Do not optimize high P50 by
silently changing H0, tolerating unbounded losses, adding free rolls or ignoring fees.

17.5 Exact proposal verification

Convert the selected trade/roll into exact proposed fills, ledger and inventory,
including temporary exposures and reservations. Recompute target gap, full payoff,
Greeks, fees, margin, risk certification, dates and domain. Return PASS/FAIL/
INDETERMINATE with reasons; projected HOLD and managed success values when defined;
differences vs current/best feasible; cost/BP/loss impact and uncertainty.
Matching selected Greeks is never enough. A price-changing fill invalidates the
preview and triggers post-fill verification with actual amounts.

If no feasible supported action restores p0, report the best found probability
and deficit, HOLD acceptability, and close/stop consequences. Do not present
"NO FEASIBLE ACTION CAN RESTORE INITIAL P50" as a proof over unsearched or
unsupported actions; qualify it by the validated action/domain scope.

18. RISK LIMITS, CERTIFICATION, SEARCH COVERAGE, AND UNSUPPORTED STATES

18.1 Separate gates

Gate A: contract/data/session/execution eligibility.
Gate B: broker permission, actual supported account BP and working-order capital.
Gate C: campaign/account economic downside and operational risk certification.
Gate D: model domain, surrogate error and confidence for probability guidance.
Passing one does not imply passing the others. Existing violations remain visible
and accounted for; they do not make a position disappear from state.

18.2 Three different loss statements

terminal_payoff_bound: exact same-expiry economic payoff bound under its stated
terminal settlement/fee assumptions.
simulated_execution_stress_bound: worst loss over declared lifecycle, execution,
financing, assignment and cost scenarios, with their bounded assumptions.
pathwise_risk_certification: VERIFIED_WITHIN_DECLARED_MODEL, FAILED, or UNKNOWN,
with proof/check scope and omitted risks. Real-world guaranteed bound is never
implied by a simulation/model certificate.

A stop at -1,000 is an instruction, not a pathwise guarantee. A vertical's width
does not alone bound financing, pin risk, assignment timing, failed liquidation,
unbounded slippage or costs. Strict SRC-E envelope actions require a conservative
verified bound through every permitted intermediate state, including accumulated
losses and reserved future costs. Unknown certification cannot be coerced to true.

18.3 Default strict behavior and research inspection

Default campaign mode is STRICT_RISK_ENVELOPE: block new risk-increasing orders
and admissibility claims if the required loss bound or BP is unsupported. Risk-
reducing/closing actions need their own executable-cost and transient-exposure
checks; do not assume a close always fills. HOLD analysis may still be provided
with its assignment-free theoretical definition and explicit limitation.

The app may allow the user to create a separate RESEARCH_UNCERTIFIED branch for
manual simulation of unsupported/assignment-exposed trades. This is a deliberate
relaxation recorded in the branch/objective, never the default workaround to get
a green risk verdict. Such results remain uncertified and do not satisfy the
original hard-loss requirement. Standard long-premium bounded-risk cases may
provide certifiable model domains while some short ETF structures cannot.

The implementation is not complete until the supported strict domain contains
useful tested cases and limitations are explicit. If broad managed short-spread
certification remains impossible with available data/execution assumptions,
report that engineering result; do not advertise a universal production optimizer.

18.3.1 Breach remediation

Already-breached/imported positions must not be trapped by a rule demanding that
a close undo their historical losses. Permit an explicit BREACH_REMEDIATION
exposure-reducing action after cost, permissions, liquidity and transient-exposure
checks even if final loss exceeds 1,000. Log the breach and realized outcome; do
not label that campaign compliant. Closing a protective hedge is not necessarily
risk reducing. The source caps still block new risk-taking/reentry that worsens
an infeasible campaign. A missing required quote remains missing, not a free exit.

18.4 Unsupported selections

Reasons include unlimited max profit, unlimited loss, missing prior cashflow,
unknown deliverable, mixed expiry/fixing, unmodeled stock assignment, insufficient
quotes/rates/carry/IV, outdated/no pack, extrapolation, absent future-action support,
excess error, unverified temporal semantics, unsupported fee/margin aggregation,
and insufficient data through required lifecycle horizon. Every unavailable
number has a specific reason and an actionable evidence/configuration remedy.
No fallback to delta-as-probability, expiry POP as P50, zero rates, synthetic prices,
or slow live recomputation.

corresponding full tests and the above completion gate are evidenced.

19. APPLICATION APIS AND WORKER/PROCESS INTERFACES

19.1 Required service interfaces

These are contracts to implement, not assertions that functions already exist.
Use typed inputs/results and explicit schema versions. No service reads GUI state
or a mutable global clock to complete a request.

CatalogService.scan(roots, previous_manifest) -> CatalogReport
CatalogService.bind_revision(session_config) -> DatasetRevision
HistoricalData.load_partition(partition_key, revision) -> ImmutablePartition
HistoricalData.underlying_asof(instant, visibility_profile) -> UnderlyingObservation
HistoricalData.chain_asof(expiry, instant, visibility_profile) -> ChainSnapshot
HistoricalData.events_between(start_key, end_key, required_contracts) -> EventStream
ParameterRegistry.resolve(symbol, instant, horizon, profile) -> ParameterSet
ProductRegistry.resolve(contract_identity, effective_date) -> ContractSpec
CalendarService.intervals(product, start, end, version) -> SessionIntervals

ReplayService.create(config) -> SessionHandle
ReplayService.advance(session_id, expected_version, target, command_id) -> StateBundle
ReplayService.submit(session_id, expected_version, draft, command_id) -> OrderResult
ReplayService.cancel(order_id, expected_version, command_id) -> CancelResult
ReplayService.branch(checkpoint_id, new_config, reason) -> BranchHandle
ReplayService.restore(session_id, branch_id) -> StateBundle

ExecutionEngine.on_event(state, event, fill_profile) -> ProposedFillsAndOrderUpdates
FeeEngine.assess(event, accumulated_groups, rule_profile) -> FeePostings
Ledger.apply(previous_state, economic_events) -> NewLedgerProjection
MarginEngine.evaluate(account, proposed_events, snapshot, rules) -> MarginAssessment
LifecycleEngine.events_at(instant, positions, references, policy) -> LifecycleEvents
RiskGate.check(exact_poststates, campaign, account, assumptions) -> RiskCertificate

ModelPack.load(path, required_profiles) -> VerifiedPack
evaluate_hold(snapshot, exact_position, entry_or_campaign, deadlines, pack)
    -> HoldP50Result
optimize_envelope(snapshot, campaign, account_limits, candidate_universe, pack)
    -> EnvelopeResult
verify_adjustment(snapshot, campaign, proposed_trades, account, pack)
    -> CandidateCheck
build_model(build_config) -> ModelBuildReport  [offline command only]
ExportService.record(session, bundle, analysis, destination) -> SavedRecord

19.2 Request and result envelopes

Every asynchronous request includes request_id, requested_at_wall, session_id,
branch_id, expected_state_version, snapshot_id, objective/selection hashes,
data_revision, rule_profile_ids, pack_id, compute_budget and cancellation token.
Every response repeats those identities plus status, typed payload, reason codes,
sources, elapsed CPU/wall time, and measured work counters. Failure is structured,
not a traceback dumped into the GUI. Internal diagnostics may retain sanitized
stack traces and repro IDs.

Required statuses: OK, PENDING, STALE, PARTIAL, UNSUPPORTED, INDETERMINATE,
OUTSIDE_DOMAIN, CANCELED, FAILED. Field validity is independent where appropriate:
valid chart/quotes do not require a valid managed model; valid HOLD does not
prove valid margin or risk certification. Never turn PARTIAL into OK by replacing
unavailable numbers with zeros.

19.3 Core pseudocode

  def submit_simulated_order(command, current_state):
      assert command.expected_version == current_state.version
      assert not already_processed(command.id)
      draft = normalize_exact_legs(command)
      require_contracts_known_and_data_eligible(draft, current_state.snapshot)
      proposal = preview_fill_costs_and_intermediate_states(draft, current_state)
      capital = margin_engine.evaluate(current_state.account, proposal, rules)
      certificate = risk_gate.check(proposal, current_state.campaign, assumptions)
      enforce_selected_account_and_campaign_mode(capital, certificate)
      order = accept_and_reserve(draft, capital, submission_event_key)
      commit_events_atomically(order.events)
      publish_new_state_bundle()
      return accepted_order_with_fill_assumptions()

  def advance(command, state):
      frontier = resolve_exact_target_without_neighbor_substitution(command)
      for event_batch in causal_batches_until(frontier):
          validate_data_revision_and_coverage(event_batch)
          tentative = apply_market_and_lifecycle_events(state, event_batch)
          fills = execution_engine.evaluate_preexisting_orders(tentative)
          tentative = apply_fills_fees_settlements_and_margin(tentative, fills)
          assert_accounting_and_quantity_invariants(tentative)
          state = commit_complete_batch_and_increment_version(tentative)
          if decision_or_unknown_boundary(state): break
      bundle = construct_asof_bundle(state)
      publish(bundle)
      request_fast_analysis(bundle)
      return bundle

  def run_fast_analysis(request):
      pack = loaded_verified_pack_matching(request)
      exact_state = freeze_explicit_inputs(request)
      support = validate_data_model_and_risk_capabilities(exact_state, pack)
      hold = infer_hold_if_supported(exact_state, pack, support)
      feasible = bounded_exact_candidate_generation_and_filter(exact_state)
      policy_values = infer_value_batch(feasible, pack)
      envelope = form_joint_envelope_with_uncertainty(feasible, policy_values)
      return immutable_versioned_result(hold, envelope, support)

No function above can submit real broker orders. An unavailable pack returns a
typed status; it does not call build_model or the legacy Monte Carlo function.

20. PERSISTENCE, CHECKPOINTS, BRANCHES, REPLAY, AND EXPORTS

20.1 SQLite schema and transactions

Use a single writer, foreign keys, explicit migrations, transaction boundaries
and appropriate WAL/checkpoint policy for the local environment. Tables:
  sessions: id, schema/config versions, created_at, initial balance, active branch.
  branches: id, parent branch/checkpoint, fork key, mode, objective/config hash.
  events: (branch_id, sequence) PK, event_id UNIQUE, timestamp/phase/type/payload/hash.
  orders/order_legs: current projection and original event references.
  fills: unique fill_id, order/contract, quantities/prices/source references.
  cash_postings: event_id, account/campaign, category, amount, bucket, settlement.
  position_lots: contract/campaign/open fill, remaining quantity/basis allocation.
  campaigns: original immutable objective and current derived status.
  settlements/accruals: obligation identity, due date, outstanding/booked amounts.
  rule_bindings: effective profiles, source evidence, assumptions and overrides.
  checkpoints: sequence, state hash, dataset/pack/rule hashes and stored state.
  analysis_records: request/result hash, state/selection IDs, status and payload path.
  manifests: data revisions, package/build/model identities.

Index events by branch/sequence and branch/time; fills by order/contract;
lots by contract/campaign; settlements by due date/status; analyses by input hash.
Store large arrays/parquet/model bytes outside SQLite with strong hashes and atomic
publish. Keep an event schema version separate from app/package version.
Use explicit Decimal serialization or integer scaled values, not SQLite REAL
for authoritative cash. Never deserialize code from an event payload.

20.2 Dataset and rule revision freezing

Bind each session/branch to a frozen dataset-revision manifest. Catalog refresh
can discover data for new runs, but changed fingerprints may not silently replace
already bound data. Pause with SOURCE_CHANGED_DURING_RUN; reuse a retained original
revision when available or explicitly branch/restart on the new revision. Do not
replay old fills against revised prices while retaining their original results.
Pin rule/calendar/model versions similarly. A software update cannot rewrite
historical session assumptions automatically.

20.3 Save, crash recovery, and migration

Persist each accepted command and complete economic event batch; checkpoint on
session boundaries, before branches, and periodically by a configurable event
count. Recovery loads the last valid checkpoint and replays later committed events.
Validate hashes and balance/quantity invariants. An incomplete SQLite transaction
is not an executed fill. Corrupt state opens read-only diagnostics; do not repair
balances by arbitrary balancing entries.

Write model packs, mirrors, snapshots and exports to temporary files, flush/close,
then atomic replace within the same filesystem. Write failure preserves the last
good state and clearly reports what was not saved. Back up before migrations;
migrations are deterministic, versioned, tested on older session fixtures, and
must preserve original event/provenance records. No silent destructive reset.

20.4 Rewind and branching

Backward navigation has two explicit behaviors: read-only historical inspection,
or a new branch from a prior checkpoint/event key. A branch reconstructs its cash,
positions, orders, targets and information state at the fork. Descendant future
fills/profits do not remain in its account. Original branch remains unchanged.
Compare branches using the same dataset/rules/model identities or label differences.
Human exposure to the future is marked in research reports; a rewind does not
restore a human's ignorance or validate an unbiased strategy test.

20.5 Exports

Export human-readable TXT, structured JSON and optional CSV order/fill/ledger
tables. Avoid requiring a spreadsheet dependency. JSON includes:
  session/branch/command/state/selection IDs;
  bar_label, decision time, t0, wall runtime, t_star, t_last, T, T0/T_hard;
  exact contracts, source timestamps/precision, all snapshot inputs and hashes;
  rates/carry/IV raw/normalized sources, known/effective dates;
  original cash/M0/H0/p0, all realized/open exposure and cost components;
  mark/execution/terminal bases, calendar/rule/assignment assumptions;
  all P50 event names and probabilities or null+reason;
  policy/envelope/search candidates/constraints/certification;
  uncertainty types, domain, model training cutoff, validation and pack identity;
  stage timings, dependency versions, deterministic seed scheme where relevant;
  data gaps, unverified broker parity, counterfactual/research flags;
  real_orders_sent=false.

The old source's full reproducibility groups remain required where relevant.
No account names/numbers, tokens, authorization headers, or unrelated filesystem
contents enter exports. Export a small reproducible selected snapshot, not the
entire raw archive by accident. Retention may evict regenerable caches, never
session ledgers or source revisions needed by retained reproducibility records
without an explicit archival/removal policy.

21. PERFORMANCE BUDGETS AND SAFE CPU/RAM USE

21.1 Target hardware and resource ownership

Design for the user's Windows desktop: i7-12700F, 12 cores/20 threads, 32 GiB RAM,
NVMe/SSD, RTX 3080 optional. These are supplied project context, not a benchmark.
Independent offline work can use multiple CPU processes; UI/inference must retain
responsive capacity. Initial engineering setting: 6-8 offline workers, bounded
by measured memory per worker, with configurable expansion after benchmarking.
Do not blindly launch 20 workers each running a multithreaded BLAS library.

Set and record BLAS/OpenMP thread limits per worker before importing numerical
libraries. A measured pool/thread configuration is part of reproducibility.
Use shared memory/memory-mapped immutable bases to avoid one huge copy per Windows
spawned process. Pass IDs/small descriptors instead of repeatedly pickling chains.

Initial memory budgets: interactive app plus loaded pack/caches target <=8 GiB;
explicit offline build target <=20 GiB combined peak, leaving room for Windows,
browser and filesystem cache. These are adjustable design budgets, not achieved
figures. Measure PSS/RSS/total child memory and peak process count. Chunk work and
spill regenerateable intermediates to NVMe rather than swapping or OOM.

21.2 Interactive performance objectives

Provisional development targets on validated representative data:
  UI input acknowledgment <100 ms;
  cached chart/chain state publication p95 <300 ms for a simple minute step;
  a supported single-position cached HOLD query p95 <=1 second;
  bounded proposal check p95 <=2 seconds;
  bounded envelope query p95 <=5 seconds;
  model load/cold data parse reported separately from warm query latency.
These targets never justify weaker P50 definitions, omitted events, float32
downgrades, stale quotes, truncated region topology, or unreported search limits.
A large chronological time jump may take longer; report progress and actual
reached cursor while preserving full event processing. Do not imply every jump
costs a constant amount regardless of data volume.

21.3 Safe optimization order

Measure by stage: catalog, read/decompress, canonicalize/as-of, chart projection,
marks/accounting, margin, features, HOLD, candidate generation, batched value,
serialization. Optimize repeated I/O and object creation first. Use binary-search
time indexes, cumulative causal aggregates, vectorized exact payoff calculations,
read-only array caches, batched model inference and candidate pruning.
Keep one loaded pack per inference worker. Cache only parameter-complete keys;
ordinary new IV/spot/time requires evaluating the new coordinates.

Offline parallelism uses independent stable work-unit IDs and SeedSequence streams
whose mapping does not depend on worker completion order. Record reduction order
and deterministic/non-bitwise tolerance where numerical backends differ. Changing
RNG batches or precision cannot be dismissed as an equivalent optimization without
the source's event and numerical parity tests.

Use GPU/Numba/compiled kernels only after profiling a bottleneck and proving
accuracy/behavioral parity on meaningful fixtures. GPU is optional; no claim that
the RTX 3080 automatically accelerates Python loops or small interactive inference.
Do not allocate more RAM just to say it is used; spend it on measured caching,
vectorization or reduced I/O. Avoid unbounded full-day/all-expiry concatenation.

21.4 Performance evidence

Benchmark at least 100 distinct queries on one pack, changing spot, IV, expiry,
strikes, targets, campaign P&L and candidate sets within the domain. Record warm/
cold p50/p95/p99, CPU and peak memory, candidate count, cache hits, and quality
status. Assert zero offline MC/PDE/target-region/training calls/imports on the
ordinary query path. Compare with the SRC-D/P50-R reference engine under matching
numerical assumptions and precision. No invented speedup factor.

22. FAILURE STATES, USER RECOVERY, AND DEGRADED OPERATION

22.1 Data and replay codes

DATA_ROOT_UNAVAILABLE, SOURCE_PARTITION_MISSING, DUPLICATE_SESSION,
DUPLICATE_LOGICAL_PARTITION, SCHEMA_REQUIRED_FIELD_MISSING,
PARTITION_IDENTITY_MISMATCH, AMBIGUOUS_STRIKE_SCALE, AMBIGUOUS_IV_UNITS,
TEMPORAL_SEMANTICS_UNVERIFIED, INVALID_CANDLE, DUPLICATE_UNDERLYING_TIMESTAMP,
INVALID_QUOTE, AMBIGUOUS_EVENT_ORDER, MISSING_AUTHORITATIVE_SPOT,
STALE_ASOF_INPUT, INCOHERENT_SNAPSHOT, OI_NOT_YET_KNOWN,
CONTRACT_NOT_YET_OBSERVED, DATA_GAP_BEFORE_TARGET, DATASET_END_REACHED,
SOURCE_CHANGED_DURING_RUN, CALENDAR_UNVERIFIED, SETTLEMENT_REFERENCE_MISSING.

22.2 Order/account codes

STALE_ORDER_PREVIEW, INVALID_QUANTITY_OR_TICK, CONTRACT_EXPIRED,
SESSION_NOT_EXECUTABLE, INSUFFICIENT_QUOTE_DEPTH, FILL_EVIDENCE_UNAVAILABLE,
INSUFFICIENT_BUYING_POWER, ORDER_RESERVATION_CONFLICT,
UNSUPPORTED_MARGIN_STRUCTURE, BROKER_RULE_HISTORY_MISSING,
BROKER_PARITY_UNVERIFIED, MARGIN_DEFICIT, CASH_FUNDS_UNSETTLED,
LOSS_CAP_EXCEEDED, UNVERIFIED_LOSS_BOUND, UNSUPPORTED_EXPOSURE, BREACH_REMEDIATION,
ROLL_HORIZON_EXCEEDED, TARGET_INITIALIZATION_INCOMPLETE,
LEDGER_RECONCILIATION_FAILED, STATE_VERSION_CONFLICT.

22.3 Analytics/storage codes

PACK_MISSING, PACK_CORRUPT, PACK_PROFILE_MISMATCH, OUTSIDE_MODEL_DOMAIN,
MODEL_COVERAGE_UNAVAILABLE, QUERY_HEAVY_CALL_FORBIDDEN,
NEEDS_MODEL_INPUT, MISSING_RATE, AMBIGUOUS_RATE_UNITS,
MISSING_DIVIDEND_OR_CARRY_INPUT, MISSING_PATH_IV, LEG_IV_NOT_IDENTIFIABLE,
MODEL_BASIS_TOO_LARGE, MODEL_MARKS_INVALID, UNBOUNDED_MAXIMUM_PROFIT,
UNBOUNDED_RISK_NOT_ALLOWED, NONPOSITIVE_TARGET, DEADLINE_NOT_FUTURE,
DEADLINE_AFTER_FIXING, INITIAL_BENCHMARK_UNKNOWN,
DECISION_UNCERTAINTY_TOO_LARGE, SEARCH_BUDGET_REACHED,
NO_FEASIBLE_SUPPORTED_ACTION, MISSING_MANAGED_ANCHOR,
PRICER_NOT_CONVERGED, NUMERICAL_CONVERGENCE_UNRESOLVED,
CACHE_CORRUPT, CHECKPOINT_MISMATCH, MIGRATION_REQUIRED, SAVE_FAILED,
WORKER_CRASHED, REQUEST_SUPERSEDED, USER_CANCELED.

Retain all SRC-D/SRC-E diagnostic identifiers in compatible adapters. Broker auth,
entitlement, malformed-response, pagination, forward-fit and previous-snapshot
errors remain conditional to an implemented live/legacy adapter, not invented
failures of an offline replay app.

22.4 Handling policy

Every error states affected time/contracts/capability, root evidence, valid
recovery, and whether existing accounting is complete. Preserve the user's draft
and last committed state. Data/essential model failure means no probability;
coverage/search limits may permit clearly PARTIAL results, never a confident
global optimum. A worker crash cannot roll back already committed fills or
advance the clock. Restart a worker from immutable request/pack state only.

Out-of-domain requests may be recorded for optional offline expansion. They do
not automatically start computation. Save errors must not falsely advertise an
export path. Canceled oracle builds keep complete verified batches/checkpoints
and mark incomplete work; canceled fast queries never publish stale answers.

23. PACKAGING, INSTALLATION, PRIVACY, AND MAINTENANCE

23.1 Installation deliverables

Provide source repository, tested lockfile, GUI and headless entry points,
environment setup script, data-root configuration example, schema documentation,
model-build instructions, signed/hash-verified pack manifest, and a portable
Windows distribution. Start with a standalone directory build through the tested
Qt deployment workflow; keep pack/data external rather than embedding the QQQ
archive into an executable. Qt's pyside6-deploy is an available official packaging
route; exact packaging versions and compatibility require a clean-machine test.
[APP-C13]

First launch checks Python/Qt/numerical imports in development mode, timezone data,
write permissions in runtime paths, source readability, disk space, schema/rule
versions, and pack availability. Guide only the missing setup fields. It must
not modify system Python, require admin privileges for normal work, or hide an
unrequested dependency build behind an Analyze button.

Initial distribution is Windows 10 x64 for the stated PC, with actual Qt/driver/
display-scaling testing. Linux portability is desirable but cannot delay the
Windows correctness gates. Android is not a desktop deliverable; the three-stage CLI defined inside this
file remains a separate compatible consumer of the implemented mathematical core.
Include dependency notices and review distribution-license obligations before
shipping binaries; do not assume permission to redistribute proprietary datasets.

23.2 Local-only boundary

The simulation package has no broker-trading credential configuration or order
endpoint. Test that manual simulated orders and model builds make no broker
network calls. Optional later read-only imports/adapters require narrow contracts,
redaction and current schema tests; do not leak account identifiers into model
requests. Use fixed trusted formats; never eval vendor data, config strings,
serialized objects or arbitrary code from a model pack.

23.3 Updates and reproducibility

Check new rules/packages separately from an active replay. New models/rules are
installed beside old versions and selected explicitly, then validated. Preserve
old session compatibility or provide an explicit tested migration/export path.
Different app builds retain a reproducibility manifest; no silent reinterpretation
of saved cashflows, timestamp semantics, targets or fees after an update.

24. CONCRETE SCENARIOS AND ACCOUNTING EXAMPLES

All numbers in this section are synthetic unit fixtures, not real QQQ quotes,
currently listed strikes, trading recommendations or measured app results.

EX-01. Five-dollar credit vertical, one unit, multiplier 100, credit 1.00:
  premium cash +100; gross terminal max profit 100; terminal max loss 400;
  H0=50; gross collateral 500; entry BP reduction 400 before fees.
  With initial cash 5,000 and an unchanged -100 signed option mark, cash is
  5,100 but mark equity remains 5,000 before fees. This is not 100 profit.
  Do not deduct 500 collateral from equity and then deduct 400 again.

EX-02. The same spread closes for debit 0.50:
  close premium cash -50; gross realized profit 50; net realized profit
  50 minus actual entry/exit costs. The gross HOLD target is met; the default
  managed NET target of 50 is not met unless net realization actually reaches 50.
  The UI must not equate the two events.

EX-03. Original M0=200 and H0=100. A prior action realizes a 30 loss, then a roll
  changes strikes/expiry and pays more fees. H0 stays 100 total campaign profit;
  remaining required recovery includes the 30 loss plus costs. The new spread's
  max profit does not reset the target or replenish the 1,000 loss budget.

EX-04. Debit vertical width 5, debit 2, one unit:
  max gross profit 300; H0=150; theoretical closing value must reach 350 USD,
  or (5+2)/2=3.50 quoted points, before additional net-cost considerations.
  The source's 767/765 put spread with 0.80 credit separately tests M=80,
  L=120, target=40 and closing debit<=0.40.

EX-05. Eligible one-unit condor with widths 3 and 5, total credit 1.20:
  gross collateral 500; net entry BP reduction 380 before fees, not 800-120.
  Unequal contract ratios or shared hedges must be reclassified before this applies.

EX-06. Six cash-secured puts at K=11, credit 0.75:
  gross security 6,600; premium credit 450; entry BP reduction 6,150 before costs.
  Reject under a 5,000 account cap; premium receipt is not free buying power.

EX-07. Sixteen QQQ contracts in one opening leg under the documented cap:
  opening commission 10, not 16. Four such legs have opening commission 40,
  not 10 for the whole condor. Closing commission zero does not eliminate fees.
  Partial-fill cap grouping and separate-order behavior require their profile tests.

EX-08. A minute sequence touches a midpoint profit target at 10:02 and reverses
  by 10:05. Jumping 10:00 to 10:05 must preserve the historical touch record if
  observable, but creates no closing fill unless an eligible exit instruction
  existed and its execution rule actually filled. A new 10:05 order cannot buy
  the earlier 10:02 price. Unobserved intra-minute touches remain unknown.

EX-09. After a short call assignment, shares decrease by multiplier*quantity and
  strike cash is received; the surviving long call remains. Recompute stock
  funding, margin, borrow and dividends. Do not cash-settle the short's intrinsic
  value as well or erase the remaining hedge.

EX-10. A 15:50 to next-session jump encounters an expiring portfolio. It must
  process available close opportunities and expiry/lifecycle/settlement events.
  If official close or post-16:00 coverage is missing, strict replay stops at the
  unresolved dependency; it must not claim an observed 16:10 forced fill.

EX-11. A new draft changes from a put spread to a butterfly while the first
  analysis is running. The put-spread response cannot replace the butterfly
  result. State/selection/pack identities are checked before UI publication.

EX-12. A new branch starts at 10:00 after the user has seen a profitable 11:00
  close. Its ledger reverts to the 10:00 checkpoint, excluding that future profit;
  the branch report records that the path was previously inspected by the user.

25. ACCEPTANCE TESTS AND NUMERICAL VERIFICATION

25.1 Testing layers

Use small explicit synthetic unit fixtures for deterministic correctness; property
tests for signs/cash/quantity/causality; golden sanitized real-format partitions
for ingestion; frozen reference records for mathematical parity; independent
numerical solvers/rollouts for model accuracy; and end-to-end Qt interaction tests
for state synchronization. Test fixtures must never be an automatic production
data fallback. No actual trade or broker account access is necessary.

25.2 Data and chronology tests

DT-01 Canonical and fallback timestamp columns; naive/aware NY normalization; DST.
DT-02 Filename indexing, duplicate dates/partitions, prefix/copy variations.
DT-03 Candle finite/geometry/volume checks, zero-volume exclusion, real missing slots.
DT-04 390 regular slots, separate 16:00 boundary, early-close shortened sessions.
DT-05 Option row session/expiry/symbol identity; strike and IV scaling ambiguity.
DT-06 All listed Theta aliases, missing optional versus required fields, raw retention.
DT-07 Quote validity, zero bid, crossing, invalid latest update, deterministic sequences.
DT-08 All as-of joins backward only; minute-complete vs raw-event availability.
DT-09 Option OHLCV full-minute volume/high/low unavailable at its opening label.
DT-10 oi_before used intraday; same-day oi never leaks into chain/features/actions.
DT-11 00:00 whole-session legacy selector cannot bypass replay visibility.
DT-12 Vendor underlying radically disagrees; authoritative Parquet still controls.
DT-13 Higher-timeframe partial candles and indicators exclude every future row.
DT-14 Poison future rows with extreme prices/IV/OI; all earlier snapshots/actions/
      model inputs/results remain identical. Include future contract first appearance.
DT-15 Missing minute/expiry/session/holiday distinguished, no neighbor substitution.
DT-16 Cache/mirror invalidation, immutable raw sources, frozen run revision changes.
DT-17 Source timestamp precision uncertainty and TEMPORAL_SEMANTICS_UNVERIFIED.
DT-18 Session-open 09:30 reveals no full new candle; 09:31 reveals 09:30.
DT-19 Same timestamp event-phase ordering for orders, market data and announcements.
DT-20 Large versus repeated small steps produce the same complete state or same
      committed valid prefix at a gap; canceled incomplete batch is not a fill.

25.3 Execution, accounting and broker tests

AT-01 Entry/close signs, multipliers, reversals, integer quantities and exact ticks.
AT-02 Draft edits never affect cash/position/reservations; accepted orders do reserve.
AT-03 Next-event fills, no retrospective candle-high/low fills, no reused quote size.
AT-04 Natural-side combo debit/credit limit inequalities, ratio-unit partial fills.
AT-05 Cancel/replace/day expiry/GTC and duplicate-command idempotency.
AT-06 Gross/net/mark/liquidation P&L reconcile; short sale is cash plus liability.
AT-07 Settlement bucket transfers do not change equity or duplicate receivables.
AT-08 FIFO/specific-lot partial closes, cent remainders, fee attribution, shared lots.
AT-09 Original H0/M0/p0/T0/cutoff persist through losses, fees, rolls and reentry.
AT-10 Partial initial fills do not use unfilled quantity's target; late p0 stays initial.
AT-11 Credit/debit vertical, unequal-wing condor and cash-secured fixtures above.
AT-12 Naked requirement components under dated parameters; no premium double count.
AT-13 Same hedge cannot secure two positions; account-wide regrouping after close.
AT-14 Two pending orders cannot spend identical BP; transient roll capital checked.
AT-15 16-contract/4-leg commission caps; closes still incur applicable noncommission fees.
AT-16 Partial/cross-day/cancel-replace fee group/rounding evidence and explicit assumptions.
AT-17 Sale charges on sold legs of net-debit orders; no NDX proprietary fee on QQQ.
AT-18 TAF waiver boundary 2026-10-01/2026-12-31 and independent Section 31 boundary.
AT-19 Exercise/assignment per-event/leg grouping; no fabricated forced-close surcharge.
AT-20 Four exercise/assignment stock/cash sign cases; no duplicate intrinsic cash.
AT-21 Partial/early assignment leaves hedge; stock, interest, borrow and dividends persist.
AT-22 Official fixing observation precedes consuming expiry event at the same instant.
AT-23 DNE/contrary exercise/pin scenarios, missing close/post-market evidence, failed close.
AT-24 Historic/current PDT transition; account equity/margin/BP still enforced.
AT-25 Stock settlement T+3/T+2/T+1 boundaries and independent option premium settlement.
AT-26 Banking holiday, GFV/funding lot and supported account restrictions.
AT-27 NLV with long option value does not automatically confer marginable equity.
AT-28 QQQ single-leg 4.01 premium passes a penny tick; combo tick separately verified.
AT-29 Session/campaign aggregate loss and BP caps; new tags cannot erase prior losses.
AT-30 Unknown/failed risk certificate cannot become PASS because terminal payoff is bounded.

25.4 P50 and risk-envelope tests

PT-01 Exact payoff extrema at zero/strikes/tails; finite vs unbounded profit/loss.
PT-02 First hit followed by loss, later recovery, disjoint/tangent/narrow target sets.
PT-03 Actual expiry stays fixed when deadline changes; no terminal substitution.
PT-04 No closing hit in closed/data-ineligible periods; separate theoretical endpoints.
PT-05 Terminal settlement increment exactly once; expiry primary label/companion.
PT-06 One QQQ path for all legs; proper GBM moments and ACT/365F/DST elapsed time.
PT-07 American intrinsic/European comparison, PDE/obstacle/domain convergence.
PT-08 Vendor versus inverted IV, flat-vega nonidentifiability, price-basis safeguards.
PT-09 No zero assumed rates/carry, future sidecar data, embedded-spot override or
      American European-parity substitution.
PT-10 Frozen per-leg IV hybrid limits, bounds violations, no artificial mark clipping.
PT-11 All probabilities [0,1]; same-event deadline monotonicity; higher target no easier;
      more eligible observations cannot erase hits. No universal P50>=expiry POP test.
PT-12 Optional targets/day offsets, calendar-day DST, out-of-horizon requests.
PT-13 Coupled pilots/independent production, Wilson intervals, no fake convergence.
PT-14 In-file reference fixtures and analytic/independent solver agreement;
      a failed or unrun implemented reference test cannot count as passed.
PT-15 No MC/PDE/target-region/trainer import or call in online query, including failures.
PT-16 New spot/IV/time/legs within domain reuse identical pack_id and update results.
PT-17 Out-of-domain and missing-pack abstention; no invisible expensive fallback.
PT-18 Matched Greeks/different payoff portfolios are not automatically equivalent.
PT-19 All feasible actions check exact ledger/temporary risk/BP before ranking.
PT-20 Original benchmark unknown/incomparable/changed deadline states handled honestly.
PT-21 Managed realization requires modeled executed exit and net H0; mid touch alone fails.
PT-22 Independent rollout evaluates the RETURNED policy with only causal information.
PT-23 Every envelope branch has an exact feasible representative; joint projections
      do not admit impossible combinations. Proposal checked independently.
PT-24 V_search >= its own feasible HOLD Q within tolerance, never a claim that it
      must exceed the different gross HOLD first-hit event.
PT-25 No greater modeled success from ignoring already-paid fees or free rolls.
PT-26 Finite cutoff/roll limits; quantity/budget exhaustion; flat succeeded/failed/reentry states.
PT-27 Uncertainty ties and search truncation are visible; no false probability ranking.
PT-28 Separate structural/stress/pathwise loss fields, assignment/pin/failed-fill stresses.
PT-29 Held-out payoff shapes, near-expiry gamma, tight butterflies, skew/tails, domain detector.
PT-30 Pack reload/hash validation, as-of training cutoff, feature schema and model reproducibility.

All SRC-E T01-T18 and SRC-D Section 26 cases remain binding in their active scopes;
the above integrates rather than replaces them. Conditional legacy/live/SPX cases
must pass before those adapters are advertised.

25.5 Persistence/UI/operational tests

OT-01 UI never mixes committed state versions; delayed stale analysis is rejected.
OT-02 Play/pause/rapid steps/selection edits do not duplicate orders or freeze GUI.
OT-03 Cancellation leaves a complete committed prefix; recovery reproduces cash/positions.
OT-04 Checkpoint plus event replay matches uninterrupted session bytewise or under
      explicit deterministic serialization/numerical equality rules.
OT-05 Rewind/branch cannot import future profit, open orders or unavailable observations.
OT-06 Source/model/rule change pauses or branches, never silently alters old results.
OT-07 Disk full/corrupt cache/worker crash/migration failure keeps last valid state.
OT-08 Pack/snapshot/config hashes reject tampering/incompatible versions; no pickle/eval.
OT-09 GUI/headless request parity and Windows spawn/packaging/clean-install smoke test.
OT-10 Exports reconstruct the selected calculation, preserve null reasons and contain
      no secrets/PII; no real order-network methods are called.
OT-11 100 changed-state query benchmark, cold/warm latency, total child peak RAM.
OT-12 Display scaling, keyboard actions, missing-data labels, blank future chart and
      no future-driven autoscale. All primary user workflows run end to end.

26. EVALUATION METHODOLOGY AND REPRODUCIBLE EXPERIMENTS

26.1 Mathematical accuracy versus trading outcomes

Measure three different things separately: computational accuracy relative to
the stated model; account/rule reconstruction relative to documented fixtures;
and empirical performance on held-out historical paths. Historical outcomes do
not prove the model's conditional probability is physically correct, and agreement
with a rounded tastytrade display does not prove proprietary engine identity.
The inherited vendor comparison protocol remains applicable when actual matched
reference captures are supplied; do not invent a public P50 endpoint or tune
arbitrary parameters to match a handful of displays.

26.2 Out-of-sample policy evaluation

Freeze training window, supported universe, parameter sources, costs, assignment
scenario, risk caps, campaign horizon, candidate budget and redecision schedule
before evaluating. Group by full campaign/session and embargo overlapping future
label windows. Use common scenarios for comparing candidate policies where valid,
but independent paths for final hold/policy validation. Report accepted/rejected/
unsupported states and the effect of exclusion; missing data cannot vanish from
the denominator merely because it produced an inconvenient loss.

26.3 Metrics to report

Probability accuracy/calibration: hold absolute error distribution, worst cases,
fraction inside the 0.01 target, policy error/rollout intervals, domain abstention,
and policy regret where an independent stronger benchmark exists.
Trading outcomes: net campaign success frequency, realized P&L distribution,
tail loss/stress/CVaR where modeled, drawdown, time to realization, costs, capital
usage, rejected orders, assignment/expiry exposure and completeness of histories.
Operational metrics: snapshot coverage, stale/missing fractions, data/schema errors,
query latency, offline build time, memory/disk use, and event replay determinism.
Do not present high target-hit probability alone as profitability or safety.

26.4 Traceable reports

Every validation/benchmark report names source hashes, exact code version,
dependency lock, parameter/rule/model versions, random streams, test-set definitions,
pass/fail/NOT RUN gates and material exceptions. Include actual observed values
only after running tests. Keep planning targets distinct from results. A failed
gate yields a failed or limited-domain release report, not a polished success
claim. Preserve enough input snapshots to independently reproduce exceptions.

    exact reference algorithms are P50-R, simulation profiles are APP-C.
D31 ACTIVE: in-file requirement integration and explicit compatibility scope;
    Section31 checks internal completeness without needing another file.

U1-U4 model/IV/pricer ->14-16; U5 dated historical parameters ->15; U6 ACT/365F ->07/14;
U7/U8 numerical oracle ->16; U9/U10 sessions/terminal ->07/14; U11/U12 entry/costs
->11/14 with frozen event basis; U13 POP ->14; U14-U16 prior/live fallback are
conditional with as-of/cache principles retained; U17 SPX forward conditional;
U18 no fabricated live fallback conditional; U19 vendor honesty ->26.

28. IMPLEMENTATION MILESTONES AND RELEASE GATES

28.1 M0: Evidence audit and frozen contracts

Read every opening source block and this precedence register. Inspect actual Windows
archive examples read-only: schemas, label timing, scale/IV units, range/coverage,
contract metadata, source sizes and data availability. Freeze SRC-D/P50-R's
event definition, numerical settings, deterministic fixtures and benchmark plan.
Implement the synthetic fixture generator specified here before binding user data.
Record required runtime metadata and dependency compatibility; freeze domains,
units, event schemas and APP-C execution/account profiles.

Outputs: data manifest, canonicalizer fixtures, decision register, in-file
reference-fixture corpus and pinned development environment.
Gate: no guessed units/times. The defined synthetic fixture suite must run
without an earlier script or separate instruction document. Missing real data
is a typed runtime-input limitation, never a missing-algorithm prerequisite.

28.2 M1: Data catalog and causal snapshot layer

Implement catalog/read-only adapters/aliases/validation/mirrors/time availability/
known OI/reference registry, with precise diagnostics and frozen source revisions.
Implement headless as-of snapshot inspection before adding trading complexity.
Gate: DT01-18 pass on synthetic and actual-format samples; future poisoning leaves
earlier outputs unchanged; no source bytes changed. Report real archive gaps.

28.3 M2: Replay state machine and chart

Implement dual time semantics, calendar stepping, event ordering, partial-candle
aggregation, jump/pause, branch skeleton, immutable state bundles and version gates.
Add chart/volume/clock controls. UI render speed is secondary to causal correctness.
Gate: DT19-20, open/close/early-close/DST scenarios, identical step/jump valid prefix,
and responsive cancellation. Chart never reveals a future candle/indicator.

28.4 M3: Orders, ledger, positions, and campaigns

Implement draft/submit/reserve/fill/cancel/reversal/close/roll, exact lot/cash signs,
opening target initialization, fixed H0, gross/net views and persistent events.
Use an explicit synthetic fee profile in tests only; production requires a valid
dated broker profile. Add account/position/history panels.
Gate: AT01-14 and target immutability cases pass; replay crash recovery reconciles.

28.5 M4: Broker rules and QQQ lifecycle

Implement supported Reg T/cash restrictions, fee grouping and dated schedules,
account-wide collateral/margin, dividends/settlement, exercise/assignment/expiry,

only to the explicit simulation profile; no unavailable broker reference is a
prerequisite for implementing or testing the specified behavior.
Gate: AT15-30 plus all supported account-level fixtures; unknown rules/certificates
remain explicit. No final terminal-payoff shortcut replacing physical delivery.

and numerical gates before use.

agreement, convergence/domain report, and no vendor-equivalence claim.

Implement B0-B5/B8-B9, pack schema/load/hash checks, normalized payoff features,

100 changed-state queries without heavy calls, measured latency/RAM.

28.9 M8: Full synchronized desktop workflow

Complete chain/ticket/risk/portfolio/account/history UX and all asynchronous state
gates. Run end-to-end: choose replay date -> step -> select QQQ spread -> inspect
P50/envelope -> simulated order -> later fill -> partial close/roll -> realize
target or fail -> save/reload -> branch/compare/export. Cover failure paths too.
Gate: all APP01-15 behavior in supported scope, all panels coherent, no stale
analysis submission, and small-step/large-jump accounting equivalence.

Run all relevant DT/AT/PT/OT and SRC-E/SRC-D gates on the actual installed pack

Deliver the application/source, tested environment, model pack/build scripts,
data/rules/setup guide, all test/benchmark/validation reports, supported-domain
manifest, example synthetic session and clean reproducible launch instructions.
No gate is passed by a screen merely showing a probability. If any core phase
remains experimental, name it and do not describe the complete app as finished.

Mapping to SRC-E milestones: source M0 ->app M0/M5; source M1 ->app M1-M4;
source M2 ->app M5; source M3 ->app M6; source M4 ->app M7; source M5 ->app M8;
source M6 ->app M9. All source checkpoints remain required in their adapted scope.

28.11 Final definition of done

An independent engineer can install the app; bind the real local QQQ data read-only;
inspect validation/coverage; build/load one qualified pack; step candles and chain
causally; submit/close/roll simulated trades; reconcile tastytrade-profile cash,
P&L and BP; query complete HOLD/managed/envelope/proposal outputs for supported
draft and open positions; see explicit abstentions elsewhere; survive restart/
branch/export reproducibly; and verify zero real broker orders and no hidden
heavy inference. The full evidence report accompanies these actual capabilities.

29. RUNTIME INPUTS, DEFAULTS, AND IMPLEMENTATION DECISION REGISTER

29.1 Decisions already made

DEC-01 Native PySide6/pyqtgraph Python desktop, one codebase, Windows-first.
DEC-02 QQQ Parquet/raw_theta authority, immutable inputs, no automatic acquisition.
DEC-03 Completed-minute reveal plus explicit decision instant; causal next-event fills.
DEC-04 Calendar-slot step size independent from chart aggregation and render cadence.
DEC-05 Append-only ledger, exact money, event ordering, source-version-bound branches.
DEC-06 Separate gross/mid/estimated-liquidation/net P&L and all capital metrics.
DEC-07 Default H0=half initial gross max; managed realization net>=same frozen H0.
DEC-08 Account BP cap 5,000, campaign loss 1,000, explicit conservative session-wide
       aggregate loss default 1,000; starting cash independently configurable.
DEC-09 Explicit finite cutoff/roll allowance, no silent unlimited extension.
DEC-10 One offline-built qualified QQQ pack; fast queries only; domain abstention.
DEC-11 Natural-side next-event simulated fills with disclosed combo/size assumptions.
DEC-12 Strict risk gate by default; unsupported research requires a separate flagged branch.
DEC-13 Effective-dated historical vs fixed-rule counterfactual modes, no fake broker parity.
DEC-14 No ordinary runtime broker login/trading endpoint/cloud dependency.
DEC-15 All five instruction blocks integrated at the beginning; all relevant
       reference algorithms and rules are expanded here with internal mappings.

29.2 Runtime observations and measured implementation results

INPUT-01 No legacy-source input is required. Build SRC-D/P50-R and generate its
       deterministic fixtures and independent solver comparisons in M0/M5.
GAP-02 Actual archive headers/time labels/coverage/units/size. Resolve in M0/M1;
       no assumptions about old partition counts or complete expiry coverage.
GAP-03 Contract metadata, deliverables/adjustments, dated product/holiday/settlement
       calendars and official closes. Build reference registry; unsupported
       metadata blocks affected lifecycle/trading capabilities.
GAP-04 Dated rates/carry/dividends and IV semantics. Resolve from legitimate as-of
       sidecars or visible dated scenario overrides; never current-value leakage.
GAP-05 Historical fees/margin/interest/borrow/day-trading rules. Source versions and
       fixtures; otherwise counterfactual/assumed mode or strict refusal.
GAP-06 Broker fee rounding/grouping and account-margin pairing/SMA details. Reconcile
       against sanitized reference cases; do not guess exact parity.
GAP-07 Quote depth, complex-order executability, latency/queue/assignment outcomes.
       Treat as simulation assumptions with stress variants; archive cannot
       establish the individual's actual counterfactual fills or assignments.
GAP-08 Finite campaign hard cutoff. User chooses visible absolute field once at
       campaign creation within pack support; no hidden infinite horizon.
GAP-09 Defensible managed IV dynamics and validated action domain. Start with stated
       supported scenario; new empirical fit requires causal validation/versioning.
GAP-10 Actual accuracy/latency/memory of arbitrary-shape surrogate/control model.
       This is a substantive modeling research task, not guaranteed by selecting
       a framework. Restrict failing support and report measured limitations.
GAP-11 Genuine bound for short-ETF assignment/funding/failed-exit risk. Require
       verified lifecycle/fill/cost assumptions; terminal spread width alone fails.
GAP-12 Data beyond 16:00 and official exercise reference. Strict replay cannot
       reconstruct it from the RTH archive; qualify reference results accordingly.

Do not ask the user to guess market conventions or mathematical parameters merely
to bypass these gates. Engineering implementation may use conservative labeled
defaults where this plan permits them, or return a precise unsupported state.
No broad claim of all-future-possibilities exact optimization, exact tastytrade
P50 identity, exact historical fills, or guaranteed real-world loss/profit follows
from this plan or from an eventually fast implementation.


30. COMPLETE APPLICATION, DATA AND ACCOUNT IMPLEMENTATION DETAILS

Everything in APP-C01 through APP-C15 is implementation content inside this file.
No named document, website, pre-existing script or broker account is required to
understand or implement these contracts. Software libraries and raw market data
are runtime inputs; their necessary semantics are specified below. Actual unknown
market observations cannot be manufactured by adding more prose. Their absence
has the explicit computational outcome given here.

APP-C01. FROZEN PROFILES, COMPLETE DEFAULTS, AND THE LIMIT OF BROKER IDENTITY

Use one application with independently versioned profiles, not a collection of
incompatible applications. The following initial selections are concrete:
  data profile: canonical underlying and raw_theta rules in this file;
  replay timing: QQQ_BAR_END_REPLAY_V1;
  execution: NEXT_ELIGIBLE_EVENT_NATURAL_V1, atomic ratio-unit combinations;
  account: OPTIONS_COLLATERAL_QQQ_V1, USD 5,000 initial cash;
  fee profile: TT_COMPONENTS_20261028_SIM_V1;
  rule-clock mode: fixed_rules_counterfactual, rule_asof=2026-10-28;
  risk: STRICT_RISK_ENVELOPE, BP cap 5,000, campaign loss cap 1,000,
        session aggregate loss cap 1,000;
  mark: midpoint where a valid coherent quote exists, explicit stale/null otherwise;
  order default: PROTECTED_LIMIT under SIM_CONDITIONAL_RISK_CHECK_V1, DAY,
                 zero added slippage, no automatic exit;
  order latency: strictly later eligible event, with exact rules in APP-C04;
  commission grouping/rounding: APP-C05;
  calendar: explicit exchange/settlement schedule records, or the labeled
            deterministic calendar scenario in APP-C03;
  initial displayed chart aggregation: 1 minute; forward step: 1 trading minute;
  initialization: no open positions, no unsettled obligations, no active campaigns;
  inference: only a qualified loaded pack; absent pack returns PACK_MISSING;
  source repair, live broker access, portfolio margin: disabled.

The initial account profile intentionally has no stock borrowing, short-option
leverage, SMA credit, or cross-expiration collateral relief. It implements the
published component economics for supported long options, cash-secured puts,
verticals and condors using cash collateral. This fully specified conservative
simulation is not a claim of exact account-specific tastytrade margin or every
Reg T privilege. Display that qualification beside its BP result. Do not label
its unavailable SMA or leveraged stock BP as zero: return NOT_APPLICABLE_PROFILE.
The cash-flow, fee and collateral calculation remains operational without a
broker statement, account login, unexplained margin server or undocumented code.

A profile's complete record is:
  id, schema_version, product_scope, account_scope, mode, rule_asof,
  components (numeric rates, units and scopes), grouping, rounding,
  exercise_policy, settlement_policy, borrowing_policy, counterfactual_flag,
  assumption_flags, historical_coverage_intervals, parent_profile_hash, hash.
The application creates this record from this file's stated values. Source URLs
are not required fields. Its numerical contents, not a link, determine behavior.

A strictly historical account rule mode accepts only local records whose entire
required component set is effective at that economic event date. Partial records
return BROKER_RULE_HISTORY_MISSING for the missing calculation. This is a precise
input-validation branch, not an instruction to read another document. The fixed
profile above can operate on historical prices with a visible counterfactual
label; selecting that mode never claims that a current fee applied in the past.

Trading permission is also local data. Initial permission set is standard QQQ
options, whole contracts, supported bounded combinations and cash-secured puts.
Whole-share stock bookkeeping and reductions are mandatory for assignment, but
planned naked calls, naked puts on leveraged margin, short stock, mixed-expiry
margin offsets, adjusted deliverables and portfolio margin are unsupported for
new orders under this profile. The chain can display them and analytics can
return a capability-specific reason. Do not discard an existing unsupported
position from the account merely because new opening permission is absent.

APP-C02. EXACT CANONICALIZATION, AS-OF SELECTION, AND INPUT RECORDS

Catalog and key construction:
  1. Enumerate actual filenames under the two configured read-only roots.
  2. For underlying accept exact QQQ_1m_YYYY-MM-DD.parquet names; parse date with
     an actual calendar parser, not only a regular expression.
  3. For options extract exactly one historical YYYYMMDD and exp=YYYY-MM-DD
     from the filename, combine with the canonical folder data type, and check
     every row's optional identity fields against that partition identity.
  4. A second path with the same logical key is DUPLICATE_LOGICAL_PARTITION.
  5. Fingerprint original bytes with SHA-256 before binding them to a run;
     size/mtime are quick change detectors, not substitutes for the run hash.
  6. Preserve row number, raw fields, exact event time and source sequence.
     Normalize once into immutable typed arrays and optional Parquet mirrors.

Each normalization mapping contains timestamp_column or date/ms_of_day columns,
source_timezone, bar_label_semantics, strike_divisor, IV_unit, Greek units,
contract multiplier/deliverable evidence, mapping_version and confidence. Values
explicitly encoded by a schema are used directly, for example strike_milli means
raw/1000 only when that header's mapping has been bound and validated. Otherwise
try the declared candidate mappings and reject ambiguity. Default candidate
strike divisors are 1 and 1000; add another only as an explicit mapping value.
A plausibility diagnostic is not proof of a unit. Candidate scaling must agree
with a valid known spot, exact contract identity when present, positive strikes,
and consistent mappings across the partition. If both candidates remain possible,
stop the affected normalization with AMBIGUOUS_STRIKE_SCALE.

For IV units the candidates are decimal and percent, giving raw or raw/100. Use
schema metadata first. Without it, reject candidates outside the model's numeric
support and compare their American repricing with contemporaneous bid/ask under
known spot/r/q. Select only a unique candidate satisfying the pricing acceptance
bounds. Two acceptable candidates remain AMBIGUOUS_IV_UNITS. No successful unit
choice can rely on future returns, later quotes or a convenient future trade.
Displaying a quote does not require valid IV; computing its P50 does.

For timestamps, a naive timestamp is New York wall-clock time. If date and
ms_of_day are present, require integer 0 <= ms_of_day < 86,400,000 and construct
local midnight plus that duration. Reject contradictory redundant timestamps.
Timezone-aware inputs are converted to New York before session membership checks.
Keep UTC epoch nanoseconds for ordering and an explicit NY representation for UI.
Round/floor only a derived minute key; never overwrite the original event time.
For DST ambiguous/nonexistent input, require an explicit offset/fold or reject;
ordinary 09:30-16:15 observations are not ambiguous, but user-entered overnight
horizons and legacy day offsets can be.

Underlying validation, zero-volume exclusion and causal aggregation are the
rules already written in the underlying section of this file. A 16:00 timestamp
is retained as a separate boundary observation if useful, never inserted as a
391st RTH bar. If source metadata explicitly establishes end labels, translate
available_at once according to that metadata; do not add a second minute.

Maintain two indexes per contract/session:
  raw_event_keys = sorted (instant, reliable_source_sequence, stable_event_id);
  availability_keys = sorted keys at which the record becomes observable.
Only events known before the request's exclusive cutoff can enter a bar-end
snapshot. Binary-search the cutoff with left insertion for event_time < D and
with right insertion for an explicitly inclusive event-time request. Then apply
validity and exact contract filters. A quote's latest invalid update remains a
quality event; a previous valid quote is merely a stale mark, never a fresh fill.
Conflicting same-time events without a reliable ordering are not resolved by
arbitrary filesystem order. Identical duplicate bytes may collapse with a count.

For ohlcv the whole bar is available only at its interval end. For trades, retain
corrections/cancellations with their own known_at and apply them prospectively;
a correction announced at 11:00 does not rewrite a 10:00 snapshot. Unknown trade
conditions are retained diagnostically and excluded from execution evidence.
Stored option OHLCV is the default volume source. A trade-derived source sums
eligible known sizes once and has its own source ID; the two are not added.

Prior/opening oi_before can be released at the canonical session open as declared
by the data contract, with any later known_at delaying it. Same-session oi stays
unavailable intraday. Never turn one daily OI value into supposedly observed
minute samples. New contracts become selectable only after their first known
listing record or first eligible observed quote. Reading a complete gzip does
not make future-listed contracts known now.

Registry inputs are ordinary typed values, not reading assignments. Define:
  ReferenceRecord(kind, symbol, contract_id_or_null, value, unit,
      observed_at, known_at, effective_from, effective_to, expiry_or_tenor,
      basis, scenario_flag, identity_hash).
Kinds include rate, continuous_carry, dividend, multiplier/deliverable,
calendar_session, official_fixing, borrow_rate and house_margin_override.
Resolve records by exact key, known_at <= decision key, and effective interval.
Conflicting equally authoritative records are an error; no nearest future value.
A user-entered dated scenario record is sufficient to run a model under that
assumption and must remain tagged SCENARIO_INPUT. Missing rate/carry is missing,
not zero. Genuine historical reference inputs are data that cannot be reconstructed
from an instruction file or from option bid/ask alone.

APP-C03. COMPLETE CALENDAR AND TIME-ADVANCE ALGORITHM

A SessionRecord has local_date, exchange_open, underlying_close,
option_close, fixing_at, exercise_instruction_cutoff, settlement_business_day,
status, early_close, profile_id, scenario_flag. Distinguish exchange eligibility
from available source coverage and from the model's monitoring schedule.
The ordinary-day QQQ components are underlying [09:30,16:00) and options
[09:30,16:15). The canonical replay execution window is their supported data
intersection; it ends at 16:00 unless independent compatible data is present.
For a 13:00 early close, use underlying [09:30,13:00); the separate option-close
field must be supplied or explicitly modeled rather than inferred from 16:15.
No option quote after 16:00 can repair missing authoritative underlying data.

Provide a deterministic STANDARD_US_CALENDAR_SCENARIO_V1 so the behavior is
specified even when an exact historical schedule input is absent. Its output is
marked calendar_scenario=true and is not certified historical exchange coverage.
It is a planning/calendar model, not permission to label missing data a holiday.
Its domain is years 2012-2030. Weekend means Saturday/Sunday. Normal close is
16:00 underlying, 16:15 options. Closed dates are:
  New Year's Day (Sunday observed Monday; Saturday adds no Friday closure);
  third Monday of January; third Monday of February;
  Good Friday; last Monday of May;
  Juneteenth from 2022, Independence Day, and Christmas, each observed Friday
    if Saturday and Monday if Sunday;
  first Monday of September; fourth Thursday of November;
  exceptional whole-day closures 2012-10-29, 2012-10-30, 2018-12-05, 2025-01-09.
For an open July 3, an open December 24, and the open Friday after Thanksgiving,
set underlying close=13:00 and modeled option close=13:15. These are explicit
scenario rules, not a complete proof of every historical special session.
An explicit SessionRecord overrides the generated scenario for that date.
If actual files contradict a closure or early-close record, stop for a calendar
conflict instead of silently removing or including the rows.

Good Friday calculation is fully specified by Gregorian Easter. For year y:
  a=y mod 19; b=floor(y/100); c=y mod 100; d=floor(b/4); e=b mod 4;
  f=floor((b+8)/25); g=floor((b-f+1)/3);
  h=(19*a+b-d-g+15) mod 30; i=floor(c/4); k=c mod 4;
  l=(32+2*e+2*i-h-k) mod 7; m=floor((a+11*h+22*l)/451);
  month=floor((h+l-7*m+114)/31);
  day=((h+l-7*m+114) mod 31)+1.
Easter is date(y,month,day); Good Friday is Easter minus two calendar days.
For nth weekday find the first day of the month, advance modulo 7 to that weekday,
then add 7*(n-1). For last weekday start at the month's final date and subtract
its modulo-7 distance to the requested weekday.

A settlement scenario excludes weekends, generated exchange holidays, observed
Veterans Day, and second Monday of October. It deliberately requires both market
and banking availability and is labeled conservative when not replaced by exact
settlement-business-day records. Calculate T+n by starting with the day after
the trade/exercise economic date and counting n eligible settlement days.
Early-close days count as settlement days. Do not count calendar days or assume
next weekday suffices. Frozen current-profile stock and option-premium lag is
one settlement business day. In dated historical mode, stock delivery is T+3
before 2017-09-05, T+2 from that date through 2024-05-27, T+1 thereafter; option
premium lag is independently T+1 in the supported profile. Preserve distinct
trade/economic time, instruction time, notice time and payment date.
