Trading-minute step counts scheduled eligible source slots. Start from current
completed minute M, enumerate the next scheduled slot, and advance D to its
availability time; count missing slots too, stopping on the first necessary
unresolved gap. A next-observed-bar command explicitly skips gaps and cannot
silently act like an ordinary minute step. A clock-duration step adds elapsed
UTC seconds. A New York calendar-day horizon adds calendar days to the local
date while preserving the local clock, then validates DST. These three operations
must be implemented separately.

A long jump merges all scheduled and data events from (last_processed_key,target].
Use a heap of the next event from each sorted source stream. A canonical phase
ordering at one instant is:
  10 known effective rule/calendar/corporate-action publication;
  20 already accepted cancellation/eligibility events;
  30 new source quote/trade and completed-candle availability events;
  35 official fixing publication where supplied;
  40 matching preexisting orders and triggered authorized exits;
  50 fill/fee/cash/inventory postings;
  60 exercise/assignment events that depend on known fixing/instructions;
  70 settlement, accrual posting and account risk review;
  80 committed snapshot publication;
  90 user commands submitted in response to that snapshot.
Within a phase order by reliable source sequence then deterministic stable ID.
Specific dependencies override ordering only by a versioned event-kind rule;
for example an exercise event may not consume an unpublished fixing. Synthetic
market ticks are never inserted to make this ordering convenient.

Required completed-bar override, BAR_END_DECISION_BARRIER_V1: a UI decision at
minute boundary D uses raw quotes strictly before D, so it must occur BEFORE raw
market events stamped D, not after them. At that particular boundary use:
  (D,10) effective already-known boundary rules;
  (D,20) availability of the minute ending at D, plus previously scheduled cancels;
  (D,25) publish the completed-bar snapshot with raw quote cutoff <D;
  (D,26) commands submitted after viewing that snapshot;
  (D,30) raw quote/trade events stamped D, processed on the next advance;
  (D,35..70) fixing/execution/postings/lifecycle/account steps as applicable.
A normal completed-bar advance stops at the target's phase25 barrier. A user's
new command commits at26. The following advance starts after that command and
can consume the still-unseen D market event at30. A seen quote cannot be reused.
For an inclusive true event-time inspection, consume the requested market event
first and publish its later snapshot phase80; a user command then follows at90.
Persist which barrier was used. This explicit distinction avoids future leakage,
backdated order eligibility and contradictions between the exclusive <D visibility
rule and same-timestamp event order.

APP-C04. ORDER MATCHING, QUANTITY, TICKS, AND CAPITAL RESERVATIONS

Normalize a combination into unique ContractKey entries with nonzero signed
integer changes d_i. Combine exact duplicates algebraically before fees/risk.
Let g=gcd(abs(d_i)); a ratio unit consists of d_i/g, with g intended units.
Default quoted unit is one ratio unit. Package cash in dollars is
  -sum(d_i*m_i*side_price_i).
Natural sides are ask on buys and bid on sells. Display both dollars and premium
points only when a common multiplier makes the point convention unambiguous.

QQQ single-leg premium tick is 0.01 in the fixed profile at all premium levels.
For option-only atomic combinations use a separate modeled package tick of 0.01
premium points per ratio unit with multiplier 100; for adjusted/mixed multipliers
require a profile-specific rule. Outward rounding is floor(bid/tick)*tick and
ceil(ask/tick)*tick. User limits must lie on their declared tick. No automatic
rounding of a user's intended limit changes the submitted order silently.

An accepted order records its submission logical key, earliest matching key,
remaining ratio units, cumulative fills, fee groups, last_attempt_event_key and
last_attempt_quote_signature. For event-granular data, every used quote must be
strictly later than the submission snapshot cutoff or an update at that instant
processed after submission under the stable event order. For minute-only quotes,
require a later normalized minute than the latest quote minute seen at submission.
For an atomic combination, require a new eligible update for every leg after
submission, and at least one genuinely new selected-leg quote since the previous
matching attempt. A matching attempt is triggered only by that new eligible
quote event. Later fee, settlement, timer, visibility, risk-review or unrelated
contract events cannot turn an unchanged carried quote into a new fill opportunity.

The quote signature is the canonical tuple of each leg's source quote ID, side,
source event key and residual eligible size. Record it and the trigger key after
every attempt, including attempts failing a price/fee/risk condition. Repeated
processing of one source ID cannot trigger another attempt. Quantity consumed
by successful fills is tracked separately against each quote ID/side and never
restored by a failed attempt or a UI refresh. A genuinely new replacement quote
may replenish size only under the declared quote-replacement assumption.
A selected quote must remain in the current eligible window, its worst-case
age at the trigger must be <=60 seconds, and worst-case inter-leg dispersion
must be <=60 seconds. These are explicit execution-quality parameters, separate
from unfiltered chain display and the more permissive P50 input-age profile.

A debit limit D permits execution only when -package_cash <= D; a credit limit C
permits execution only when package_cash >= C. This profile does not price-improve
beyond natural sides. MARKET, LIMIT and PROTECTED_LIMIT are distinct order types.
An unbounded MARKET order is not silently translated to LIMIT. Under strict risk
it requires an actual full execution-exposure bound for its stated model; without
that bound it is rejected or belongs to an explicitly uncertified research branch.
To use a bounded marketable order, the UI must explicitly create/display a
PROTECTED_LIMIT with its maximum debit/minimum credit, total-fee cap and conditional
fill profile. The stored order type and exported instructions must agree with
that choice. Conversion of an existing MARKET draft is an explicit draft edit.

The strict default PROTECTED_LIMIT uses SIM_CONDITIONAL_RISK_CHECK_V1. Its contract
explicitly makes a modeled fill conditional on the premium limit, total fee cap,
remaining expense budget, account capital and exact post-fill risk checks at the
eligible quote event. Failure posts a CONDITIONAL_FILL_NOT_ELIGIBLE observation,
leaves exposure and reserves in place, and waits for a genuinely new eligible
quote; it does not pretend an adverse fill occurred and was canceled. The UI
labels it 'Conditional simulated limit'. This simulator-only condition is not a
claim that a real accepted market/limit order can always be stopped before an
adverse fill, and no real broker endpoint implements it in this application.

For ordinary LIMIT/MARKET simulation without this conditional profile, acceptance
and reservation must cover every permitted execution state if strict certification
is claimed. Once an accepted eligible fill occurs under those order semantics,
book it and any resulting deficit/breach; do not erase or veto it merely to make
the loss or BP test pass. A later cancellation takes effect only at its processed
logical key, after any earlier fills. Revalidation detects a violation; it is not
an implicit guarantee of cancellation. If the required preacceptance bound cannot
be established, return UNVERIFIED_EXECUTION_BOUND instead of accepting as certified.

When sizes are known, eligible ratio units are
  min over legs floor(unconsumed_side_size_i / abs(ratio_i)).
Track consumption by source quote ID and side, so the same displayed size cannot
be reused. The next independent quote may replenish only under the explicit
quote-replacement assumption. Without sizes, the ordinary profile fills full
remaining ratio units under SIZE_UNVERIFIED_SIMULATION; strict-depth mode returns
INSUFFICIENT_QUOTE_DEPTH. Neither OI nor option volume substitutes for depth.

Cancellation is immediate at its processed command key, and cannot undo a fill
already committed. Cancel/replace closes the original order and creates a new ID;
fee caps follow APP-C05. DAY orders expire when the applicable execution window
ends. GTC persists across supported sessions until a contract cutoff or explicit
cancellation. Expired/unlisted/unsupported contracts cannot become fillable merely
because a stale cached quote exists.

Reserve incremental capital before acceptance. Preview all allowed limit-bound
fills, supported partial ratios and close/open effects. For an atomic defined-risk
package, its allowed extreme premium is the user limit and fees are calculated
for the full intended quantity. Reservation is the maximum nonnegative increase
in required cash commitments over no fill and these admissible filled states.
For multiple simultaneously working orders, do not assume mutually exclusive
fills unless explicit OCO behavior enforces that relationship. Sum independent
conservative reservations or enumerate the valid combined poststates. During
fill, replace the filled portion's reserve with actual capital usage and recompute
the remainder. Reject an unbounded or unsupported reservation with a reason.

APP-C05. FULL FEE ALGORITHM AND NUMERIC FIXED PROFILE

TT_COMPONENTS_20261028_SIM_V1 uses these USD components:
  option opening commission 1.00 per opening contract, cap 10.00 per order leg;
  option closing commission 0;
  option clearing 0.10 per executed contract, either side/open-or-close;
  option regulatory fee 0.02 per executed contract;
  option sale TAF ordinary rate 0.00329 per contract;
  stock commission 0; stock clearing 0.0008 per traded share;
  stock sale TAF ordinary rate 0.000195 per share, cap 9.79 per transaction;
  TAF rate zero in the rule-date interval 2026-10-01 through 2026-12-31 inclusive;
  Section 31 sale component 20.60/1,000,000 of eligible sale proceeds;
  exercise or assignment charge 5.00 per specified leg/batch group;
  separate forced-close surcharge 0 in this simulator profile;
  QQQ proprietary index surcharge 0.
These numeric components are frozen profile values; the grouping, timing and
rounding rules below are explicitly specified simulation conventions wherever
account-level broker behavior has not been established. They must not be promoted
to exact broker-parity facts. A forced close remains an ordinary filled trade
for commissions/clearing/sale fees, and does not automatically incur exercise fees.

At each fill split quantity first into closes of existing exact contracts and
new opens beyond zero. For every component compute cumulative raw Decimal charge
within its group, round the cumulative total, and post only the difference from
that group's prior posted total. Default group keys:
  commission cap: (order_id, ContractKey, opening_effect);
  clearing/ORF/sale charges: (order_id, ContractKey, trade_date, charge_kind);
  exercise/assignment: (economic_date, ContractKey, instruction_or_notice_batch,
                       exercise_or_assignment_kind).
One GTC order's opening cap survives partial fills across days. Cancel/replace has
a new order ID and new cap. Independent orders have independent caps. These are
fully defined assumptions, not an excuse to leave partial fills unspecified.

Commission raw total is min(open_contracts*1.00,10.00) in its cap group.
Clearing and ORF are quantity times rate. TAF uses sold contract/share quantity,
regardless of whether the whole combo is a debit or credit. Section 31 uses sold
premium*m*contracts or stock_price*shares, never option strike notional. For the
fixed-rule mode, the frozen rule date selects the TAF waiver/rates even when
market prices came from an earlier historical year. In historical mode use the
component's declared trade/settlement charge-date basis; the default convention
for this fixed profile is trade date with a fixed rule date for rate selection.

Round commission, clearing and ORF cumulative totals to cents with Decimal
ROUND_HALF_UP. Round TAF and Section 31 cumulative group totals upward to the
next cent with ROUND_CEILING; exactly zero remains zero. This rounding is a
simulation assumption, retained separately from numeric published components.
Never round each fill fragment before accumulating its group's raw amount.
Allocate a posted cent amount to campaign/lot recipients proportionally to their
absolute applicable quantities. Floor allocations in cents and distribute the
remaining cents by largest fractional remainder, tie-breaking stable lot ID.
Signed refunds reverse original allocations; they do not use a new random split.

Exercise/assignment quantity transfers are not ordinary option fills. Charge the
5.00 event group once, remove affected options, and create delivery cash/shares.
The baseline does not additionally charge option clearing/ORF or a stock-sale
charge on the strike-delivery event; this scope is a disclosed fee assumption.
An actual later stock trade gets ordinary stock fees. A revised fee scope requires
a new profile hash and cannot retrospectively change saved results.

Numeric fee fixtures under this exact profile and its waived TAF:
  One option opening buy: commission 1.00 + clearing .10 + ORF .02 = 1.12.
  One option opening sale at premium 1.00, multiplier 100: same 1.12 plus
    ceil_cent(100*20.60/1,000,000)=.01, total 1.13.
  A two-leg spread opened for net credit 1.00 from short premium 2.00 and long
    premium 1.00: fees 1.13+1.12=2.25, cash credit net=97.75.
  Close that spread by buying short for 1.00 and selling long for .50:
    .12+.13=.25 total closing fees; premium debit 50; round-trip net P&L 47.50.
  Sixteen opening buys of one exact leg: commission 10.00, clearing 1.60,
    ORF .32, total 11.92. Four such legs have commission 40.00 before other fees.
  Sixteen one-contract fills belonging to that same leg/order retain the same
    opening commission 10.00, not sixteen independent one-dollar caps.
  Two exercised long contracts in one leg/request group cost 5.00; two separate
    leg/request groups cost 10.00 under the specified grouping convention.

APP-C06. COMPUTABLE ACCOUNT CAPITAL, COLLATERAL, AND STRATEGY GROUPING

This is the exact OPTIONS_COLLATERAL_QQQ_V1 account algorithm, not an unspecified
broker oracle. Economic cash C includes settled cash plus known net unsettled
trade entitlements, less recognized costs exactly once. Let G be allocated gross
cash collateral for option delivery obligations and R working-order reservations.
Within the supported options-only margin simulation:
  free_option_cash = C - G - R - other_unrecognized_reserved_liabilities;
  available_option_BP = max(0,free_option_cash), provided other gates pass;
  deficit = max(0,-free_option_cash);
  equity = C + signed_option_mark_value;
  model_net_equity_requirement = G + signed_option_mark_value;
  maintenance_excess = equity - model_net_equity_requirement - R
                       - other_unrecognized_reserved_liabilities.
The last two are identities of this cash-collateral profile, not general Reg T
or house-margin formulas. Do not deduct option liability twice or subtract
collateral from NLV. Costs already deducted from C do not enter a second reserve.
The $2,000 marginable-equity permission floor applies to privileges requiring
margin; fully funded long-option behavior remains a separately allowed capability.

Entry BPR at a fixed pre/post state is
  (G_after-G_before) - (C_after-C_before)
  + (other_reserved_after-other_reserved_before).
It is capital committed by that transaction, including paid premium and fees.
For current capital reporting keep three distinct values:
  collateral G; historical net premium commitment of remaining lots;
  current model_net_equity_requirement.
They answer different questions. Define project_BP_used as the sum of supported
remaining-lot entry capital commitments plus live reservations, floored at zero
per collateral group. This is the frozen project budget convention, separately
named from current margin and actual cash availability. Recompute capital when
lots reduce, offset, roll or undergo assignment. Acceptance requires both
available_option_BP sufficient and resulting project_BP_used<=5,000. Profit
already realized can increase cash but does not permit simultaneous project
commitments above that cap.

Partition exact net option inventory by (underlying, expiration/fixing,
multiplier, deliverable, currency). Supported component recognition is mechanical:
  - Long-only positions: no short obligation collateral; debit already left C.
  - A matched same-right vertical: one long and one short per ratio unit, identical
    expiry/deliverable/multiplier, distinct strikes. Compute payoff at 0, both
    strikes and the right tail. G_component=max(0,-minimum_terminal_payoff).
    A credit put/call vertical has G=m*units*width; a debit vertical has G=0.
  - A conventional iron condor: equal units in put vertical and call vertical,
    ordered strikes long_put < short_put <= short_call < long_call.
    G=m*units*max(put_width,call_width). Do not sum both wings.
  - A standard butterfly or iron butterfly: same expiry and exact supported
    quantity pattern; use exact strike-knot/tail payoff minimum for G. The
    butterfly's strike equality, ratios and orientation must be checked.
  - Residual short puts may be cash-secured with G=m*abs(q)*K.
  - Any residual short call without an eligible protective long or actual
    allocated shares is unsupported under this account profile.

One long contract can be consumed by only one recognized component. Net identical
opposite lots first with explicit campaign ownership allocation. Then enumerate
legal component groupings deterministically within each partition; preserve the
chosen grouping in output. Initial solver support is at most 16 distinct contract
keys and 20 absolute contracts per key, with explicit operation budgets as well
as these dimension limits. Use integer variables for component units with
inventory-capacity constraints. Recognized single-template verticals, condors,
butterflies and long-only/cash-secured positions use direct checked formulas.
Only ambiguous overlapping allocations enter deterministic branch-and-bound,
minimizing total collateral among the supported recognized components, then
breaking ties by canonical component tuples. Cash-secured residual puts and
unused longs complete a decomposition.

Enumerate component types and branch quantities in stable lexicographic order.
One expanded partial allocation is one grouping node, including infeasible nodes.
The default hard budgets are 10,000 expanded nodes per account state and 100,000
expanded grouping nodes for the ENTIRE interactive request, shared across all
candidate previews rather than restarted for each candidate. Memoize exact
inventory/profile subproblems with complete keys; cache hits count as one lookup
operation and preserve their verified solution/certificate identity. A direct
single-template recognition consumes one grouping operation. Record per-state
and per-request work counters. A wall-clock deadline may cancel work for UI
responsiveness, but it never declares an approximate search complete.

If the solver exhausts its operation budget before proving the selected optimum,
return MARGIN_GROUPING_BUDGET_EXCEEDED with INDETERMINATE capital/admissibility.
The best encountered grouping may be shown as an inspection result with its
proven capacity use, but is not promoted to a completed minimum or a strict order
approval. If the global request budget is exhausted, remaining previews receive
that same explicit unsupported-work status in stable candidate order. A fully
explored infeasible problem returns UNSUPPORTED_MARGIN_STRUCTURE. No hidden
unbounded search, expanded budget or background exhaustive retry occurs during
ordinary inference. Offline qualification may use a separately declared larger
budget and save reusable verified templates.

This bounded allocation is the simulator's own pairing rule, not a claim about
tastytrade's internal strategy-grouping algorithm. Its dimension and operation
limits are part of the fast-query contract, even though it is capital bookkeeping
rather than the forbidden expensive P50 numerical oracle.

For a credit spread width 5, credit 100 and fees 2.25, C rises 97.75 and G rises
500: BPR=402.25 and free cash from 5,000 becomes 4,597.75. Equity marked at -100
becomes 4,997.75. Closing for 50 plus .25 fees restores G to zero, leaves C=5,047.50,
no remaining project commitment and net economic profit 47.50. For a debit
vertical with debit 200, G=0 and C falls by 200+fees; never lock an additional
200 after subtracting the debit. For six cash-secured puts at strike 11 and total
credit 450, G=6,600 and pre-fee BPR=6,150, which fails the 5,000 budget.

Naked margin component inspection uses the formulas in Section 12.5 with explicit
parameters a=.20, b=.10 and standard-contract floor 250. It is an inspection
output only; no available-BP calculation for an unsupported naked account is
invented from it. Likewise the 50%/25% long-stock and 50%/30% short-stock component
percentages describe a separate stock-margin scenario, not new permissions in
the default account. Mixed-expiry/diagonal offsets, portfolio margin and undefined
SMA transitions remain unsupported calculations with exact reasons, rather than
instructions to find a missing broker implementation.

APP-C07. LEDGER, SETTLEMENT, LOT BASIS, AND INTEREST

Cash postings store integer cents plus raw Decimal calculation records. Prices
and strikes are exact decimal strings; signed position quantities are integers.
Opening a long at price p posts -q*m*p; opening a short posts positive premium.
Track a signed gross option basis B=q*m*p for each remaining lot, excluding fees.
Fees are expense events with independent lot/campaign allocations. Gross realized
P&L for closing delta dq against an existing signed lot is closing cash minus
that closed portion's signed basis; implement sign-aware FIFO and prove with
both long and short examples. Net realized P&L subtracts its allocated expenses.
A short opening premium is not realized profit while its liability remains open.

Define economic_cash = settled_cash + outstanding_net_cash_entitlements.
A trade creates an entitlement/payable immediately. Settlement removes it and
adds the same signed amount to settled cash, leaving economic cash unchanged.
Each obligation has an immutable ID and one settlement event; a retry cannot
settle it twice. Receivables/payables already included in economic cash may not
be added again when computing equity. Dividends/interest use the same accrual-
then-payment identity; payment changes buckets, not the income/expense twice.

The conservative cash-account variant uses settled funding lots only. Before a
buy/open debit, assign oldest unreserved settled cash lots sufficient for debit
plus fees; unsettled sale proceeds cannot fund it. After a sale, proceeds remain
unavailable until their settlement event. This stronger rule is fully specified
and avoids needing an incomplete good-faith-violation reconstruction. It is named
SETTLED_CASH_ONLY_V1 and never described as every broker-permitted cash transaction.

Physical delivery transfers gross option basis into resulting stock economics.
For the affected signed option lot basis B_removed and strike cash flow dC:
  signed_stock_basis_delta = -dC + B_removed.
Examples per share, excluding separately expensed fees:
  long call exercised: acquire stock basis K+paid_call_premium;
  short put assigned: acquire stock basis K-received_put_premium;
  long put exercised: stock sale net gross proceeds K-paid_put_premium;
  short call assigned: stock sale net gross proceeds K+received_call_premium.
If delivered shares close an existing opposite stock lot, perform FIFO realization
using that adjusted stock proceeds/cost; only the residual opens a new stock lot.
Do not realize option intrinsic value separately. Keep campaign cashflow unchanged
except for actual strike cash and fees. These are economic gross-basis conventions,
not tax reporting rules.

Stock received or owed by assignment remains on the ledger even when account
permissions prohibit voluntarily opening it. Compute known NLV, delivery cash,
and exposures, suspend new risk, and offer supported exposure reductions. If
required stock quote/borrow/financing data is missing, known subtotals remain
visible while net liquidation P&L and the relevant risk certificate are incomplete.
No missing borrow rate is converted to a verified zero charge.

Financing scenario specification for an explicitly selected scenario branch:
  Standard annual debit-rate table for balances [0,25000), [25000,50000),
  [50000,100000), [100000,250000), [250000,500000), [500000,1000000),
  [1000000,infinity) is .11,.105,.10,.095,.09,.085,.08.
  This simulation uses the selected bracket's rate on the whole daily debit,
  ACT/360, negative settled-cash balance at each New York day's end, and books
  cumulative monthly interest on the month's last settlement business day.
  Whole-balance bracket application, day basis and posting date are declared
  scenario conventions, not assumed verified broker billing internals.
  The optional .0675 flat program is off unless eligibility and its effective
  date are supplied in the profile; it is never selected because it is cheaper.
Daily interest accrual=max(0,-eligible_settled_cash)*annual_rate/360, accumulated
at full precision and posted by cumulative cent rounding. A fixed known unpaid
charge is already an expense when accrued. Stock-borrow accrual uses a supplied
annual borrow rate and its declared collateral/day-count base; the scenario
base is abs(short_shares)*known_stock_mark, ACT/360, and is separately labeled.
These financing scenarios cannot rescue an otherwise uncertified loss bound.

APP-C08. EXERCISE, ASSIGNMENT, DIVIDENDS, AND RISK-REVIEW SCENARIOS

Contract identity must specify standard 100-share QQQ deliverables or an explicit
adjusted deliverable record. The default qualifies only the standard product.
Long call exercise: +m shares, -m*K cash per contract.
Long put exercise: -m shares, +m*K cash.
Short call assignment: -m shares, +m*K cash.
Short put assignment: +m shares, -m*K cash.
Remove only affected option units, preserve other hedges, transfer basis using
APP-C07, post event-group fees, and recompute account capital and exposure.

Three deterministic scenario choices are implemented, and their meaning is exact:
  EXPIRY_BASELINE_V1: no modeled early short assignment; at economic fixing use
    supplied official QQQ close, exercise each eligible long and assign each
    short with intrinsic per share >=.01, except recorded long DNE/exercise
    instructions. This is one simulated outcome, not observed assignment or
    a real-world risk certificate.
  EXPLICIT_ASSIGNMENT_EVENTS_V1: consume specified (contract,quantity,
    economic_at,known_at,batch_id) events causally, including partial and early
    assignments, and consume explicitly supplied contrary exercise outcomes.
  ADVERSE_ASSIGNMENT_STRESS_V1: at scheduled exercise opportunities enumerate
    assignment counts 0..abs(short_quantity) for each supported short contract,
    including contrary outcomes at expiration, preserve protective longs until
    an independently permitted action removes them, and test every intermediate
    funding/exposure state required by the selected risk model. A finite scenario
    envelope is labeled as such; it does not prove all real price/execution paths.

Default exercise-instruction cutoff in a pure scenario is 15:00 ET on a normal
expiry day, or one hour before an early underlying close. This is a conservative
SIMULATED_CUTOFF parameter, not a claim about a broker deadline. A typed exact
contract/broker cutoff record can replace it. Requests arriving after cutoff are
rejected for automatic scenario processing; a late best-efforts request can only
be represented with explicit uncertain outcomes. A short position cannot submit
DNE to stop another holder's exercise. Automatic long exercise requires the same
funding/risk review as the resulting stock exposure; an instruction cannot erase
its economic consequences.

The ordinary RTH archive alone does not establish an official fixing or every
possible after-hours contrary instruction. Strict observed lifecycle replay
stops with SETTLEMENT_REFERENCE_MISSING. A deliberately selected CLOSE_PROXY_V1
scenario may use the last valid completed candle close as its modeled fixing,
with fixing_basis=RTH_PROXY and assignment outcomes labeled simulated. It must
not claim to have reconstructed an actual closing auction or actual assignment.
No expiry simulation uses stale post-expiry option marks as a settlement price.

For a standard announced cash dividend, store declaration known_at, ex-date,
pay-date, per-share amount and currency. At the ex-date economic entitlement
boundary record signed entitled shares and accrue shares*amount, including a
negative obligation for short stock. Post on pay-date and clear the accrual.
An assignment with an earlier economic time can affect entitlement even if its
notice arrives later; process information/accounting revisions explicitly and
never leak the future notice into an earlier trading decision. Special dividends,
due bills and adjusted deliverables require explicit event records; do not apply
an ordinary-dividend approximation silently. Historical candles already contain
the actual ex-date price behavior and must not be shifted down again.

A deterministic optional simulated risk-desk rule is EXPIRY_REVIEW_15M_V1:
  review at underlying_close-15 minutes;
  compute every supported expiration delivery scenario and current funding;
  if a relevant scenario exceeds supported capital or risk, propose closing the
  affected whole exposure through the existing order engine;
  act automatically only when that simulated intervention policy was selected
  in advance; otherwise pause for a user instruction;
  use next eligible quotes, ordinary fees and configured bounded limits;
  keep failed/partial exits as exposure and continue relevant expiry scenarios.
This profile does not assert that a real desk closes at that time or guarantees
an exit. It never sends a real order and never counts a desired close as filled.

For the current frozen account regime, no legacy day-trade counter or 25,000 PDT
minimum is applied; BP and account permissions still apply. New risk is blocked
whenever current capital or maintenance deficit is positive. Deficit records
contain first economic date, amount, cause, age in business days, remedies and
closure. Age checkpoints at five and fifteen business days are audit states,
not an invented permission to maintain a dangerous exposure until those dates.
The simulation's conservative repeated-deficit policy blocks new credit as soon
as any deficit is unresolved, continuing until it is cured; it can additionally
apply an explicit 90-calendar-day restriction scenario. Those choices are labeled
conservative controls rather than exact discretionary broker enforcement. A
strict historical pre-adoption mode without a complete dated trade-counting
profile returns BROKER_RULE_HISTORY_MISSING; the fixed-rule scenario is available
without introducing a false historic PDT reconstruction.

APP-C09. QT TABLE MODEL AND SYNCHRONIZED UPDATE IMPLEMENTATION

Create QApplication and all QWidget/QAbstractItemModel objects on the main GUI
thread. Construct one MainWindow with chart, chain, ticket, portfolio, account,
risk and history docks; all receive one StateBundle identity. Economic state
belongs to the serialized simulation worker and never to a widget.

ChainTableModel subclasses QAbstractTableModel. Implement rowCount(parent): return
0 for a valid parent, otherwise len(rows); columnCount(parent): 0 for a valid
parent, otherwise the fixed column count. data(index,role) checks bounds. For
DisplayRole return formatted numeric/text values or an em dash plus reason for
missing values; for UserRole return the raw typed sort value; for a dedicated
role return ContractKey; for ToolTipRole return exact source time, age and quality.
headerData returns fixed names and units for Horizontal/DisplayRole. flags returns
ItemIsEnabled|ItemIsSelectable; actual orders are commands from a ticket, not cell
edits. A QSortFilterProxyModel sorts raw numeric values, keeps nulls consistently
last, and never sorts numeric strings lexicographically.

Apply changes only on the GUI thread. If only row values change and stable keys
remain, replace the immutable snapshot data and emit dataChanged on contiguous
changed rectangles with explicit roles. For inserted/deleted rows call the proper
begin/end insert/remove functions around the actual row mutation. For a wholesale
universe/expiry change use beginResetModel/endResetModel, then restore selected
ContractKeys from the saved key set. Do not pass QModelIndex objects to a worker
or assume a sorted row number identifies the same contract after refresh.

The simulation worker emits a signal carrying a frozen StateBundle. Connect it
to a main-thread slot using a queued connection. In that slot block recursive
widget signals, apply the bundle to all projections, update the shared visible
state version, then re-enable actions. A draft references the exact snapshot
version it used; submission with a different committed version must revalidate.
No worker calls model methods, touches a widget or runs a nested GUI event loop.

Analysis uses a separate request/result signal or process queue. Check session,
branch, state, selection, campaign, objective, pack and rule hashes before showing
a result. A superseded result may enter a complete-key cache but cannot replace
current text. A 200-ms single-shot QTimer debounces draft edits; pause never
advances economic time. Play is a timer that queues at most one unresolved step
command, so slow I/O cannot accumulate hundreds of unintended advance commands.

APP-C10. CANDLE RENDERING AND BOUNDED COLUMNAR READS

Implement CandlestickItem as a pyqtgraph GraphicsObject with immutable visible
arrays of x/open/high/low/close/volume. x is a deterministic plotting coordinate
mapped to an NY timestamp table; never use the host timezone for labels. Cache
QPicture chunks of 256 source/aggregate candles. For each candle draw the low-high
wick, and a rectangle between open/close with width .7 of the current bar spacing;
for an exact doji draw a horizontal body line. Color is presentation only.
paint(painter,...) replays cached pictures; boundingRect returns the true cached
visible geometry. Call prepareGeometryChange before updating that bounding box,
then update(). Rebuild only changed/new chunks. The final partially completed
higher-timeframe candle is recomputed from revealed underlying rows and uses a
separate cache entry. Volume is a second aligned plot with linked x range.

Hit testing maps the mouse x to the closest actually revealed candle; tooltips
show its real source/aggregate timestamps and partial status. No hidden future
rows may influence pictures, bounds, autoscale, hover, indicators or volume.
Visual downsampling combines only visible geometry for rendering; computation,
orders and account values continue using the original canonical arrays.

For Parquet, select the exact needed filenames, required columns and as-of filter
before materialization. An Arrow dataset scanner or a parameterized DuckDB query
is acceptable. Do not interpolate untrusted paths into SQL. Schema-normalized
mirrors expose available_at, contract key, session and source hash explicitly.
Use predicate/projection pushdown when available, but independently validate the
returned row cutoff; pushdown is performance, not the no-lookahead guarantee.
CSV gzip must be decompressed sequentially. Read with a bounded streaming parser,
validate blocks, append sorted immutable canonical chunks, and publish a mirror
only after complete validation. Source bytes remain untouched. Never announce a
partially built mirror as complete just because its first row exists.

RAM caches use LRU eviction over immutable partitions and have explicit byte
budgets. A cache key includes raw hash, normalization version, mapping version,
selected columns and any logical view policy. Snapshot caches additionally
include cutoff/clock and exact contract universe. Loading ahead is permitted only
inside the private data cache. User-facing services must reapply visibility for
every request, including a cache hit.

APP-C11. PROCESS OWNERSHIP, CANCELLATION, AND NUMERICAL ISOLATION

Windows entry point has an if __name__ == '__main__' guard. Call
multiprocessing.freeze_support() there before starting the application; select
spawn context explicitly for process pools. A module import defines functions
and types only: it does not start a pool, GUI, timer, data download or model build.
Worker functions are module-level importable functions. Pass small immutable
request objects or paths/hashes to read-only arrays, not live Qt objects,
SQLite connections, lambdas or a mutable account object.

The simulation writer is a long-lived serialized worker. I/O may use a small
thread pool, default two independent reads, returning immutable partitions to
that writer. CPU-heavy offline tasks use a separate process/command, default
six workers with BLAS/OpenMP threads=1 set before importing NumPy/SciPy. The fast
inference worker loads the pack once and never imports the offline oracle/trainer.
Its interface accepts only validated query requests and returns data/status.

cancel_requested is checked between small event batches or candidate batches.
A queued future may be canceled before it starts; running work exits at the next
cooperative checkpoint. Cancellation of an advance leaves a committed valid
prefix and reports its actual achieved cursor. It does not undo earlier events
or publish half a fill. Cancellation of analytics has no cash/clock side effect.
If an inference worker crashes, retain the committed application state and return
WORKER_CRASHED; restart the worker and reload the same validated pack before an
explicit retry. Never recover by launching a different slow algorithm.

Pack bytes use JSON plus safe array/tensor formats with fixed dtypes/shapes and
strong hashes; do not use arbitrary object pickle or eval. Memory-map read-only
large arrays where supported. Worker memory budgets are enforced globally, not
per worker multiplied without limit. Reject incompatible dtype/endianness/schema
rather than reinterpreting raw bytes. Float64 remains the mathematical reference.

APP-C12. MINIMUM TRANSACTIONAL STORAGE CONTRACT

Use SQLite with foreign_keys=ON, journal_mode=WAL and synchronous=FULL for session
ledger durability. A single writer owns the connection. Every command has a
unique (session_id,branch_id,command_id) idempotency key and expected_state_version.
The following logical schema is sufficient and must be implemented explicitly:
  sessions(id PK, schema_version, config_json, config_hash, initial_cash_cents,
           active_branch, created_at);
  branches(id PK, session_id FK, parent_id, fork_sequence, fork_state_hash,
           config_hash, mode);
  commands(branch_id, command_id, expected_version, payload_json, status,
           result_json, PRIMARY KEY(branch_id,command_id));
  events(branch_id, sequence, event_id UNIQUE, utc_ns, phase, event_type,
         schema_version, payload_json, previous_hash, event_hash,
         PRIMARY KEY(branch_id,sequence));
  cash_postings(event_id FK, posting_index, campaign_id, bucket, category,
                amount_cents, obligation_id, due_date,
                PRIMARY KEY(event_id,posting_index));
  checkpoints(branch_id, sequence, state_json_or_path, state_hash,
              dataset_hash, pack_hash, rules_hash, PRIMARY KEY(branch_id,sequence));
  analyses(request_hash PK, branch_id, state_version, selection_hash,
           pack_hash, status, payload_json_or_path);
  projections(name, branch_id, sequence, schema_version, payload_json,
              PRIMARY KEY(name,branch_id)).
Orders, fills, lots, campaigns, obligations and fee groups may be indexed
projection tables with their own typed columns, but must be reconstructable from
events. Projection correctness is checked against replay, never assumed because
an SQL row exists.

For each accepted command: BEGIN IMMEDIATE; look up the command idempotency key;
if already completed return its stored result; otherwise compare state_version,
validate, append a complete event batch and postings, update projections/version,
store the command result, COMMIT. On exception ROLLBACK that batch and preserve
the preceding committed state. Only after successful commit emit the StateBundle.
A long advance repeats complete batches and records the achieved prefix.

Canonical serialization for hashes is UTF-8 JSON with sorted object keys, compact
separators, no NaN/Infinity, explicit decimal strings, UTC nanoseconds as integers,
and schema version. Sort unordered sets before serialization. Hash each event
from branch ID, sequence, canonical payload and previous event hash. A branch
starts from its parent's immutable prefix hash and reconstructed state, then has
its own sequences and future. No old future events leak into the new branch.

Checkpoint every 1,000 committed events, at each session boundary, before branch
creation and on orderly close. Write external state/array files to a sibling
temporary path, flush and fsync, atomically replace, then reference their hash in
a committed SQLite row. Recovery validates the latest usable checkpoint, replays
all subsequent complete events, and rechecks cash/quantity/target invariants.
An orphan temporary file is disposable; an uncommitted SQL fill is not executed.

Before a schema migration, use SQLite's backup API to create a consistent backup;
do not copy only an active .db while ignoring WAL. Apply versioned migrations
inside transactions where possible. Preserve original events and all old hashes.
A failed migration opens diagnostics and the retained old version; it cannot
silently reset an account. Exports serialize exact frozen inputs, events/rules,
null reasons and model identity so the selected calculation is reproducible.

APP-C13. INSTALLATION, PACKAGING, AND RUN CONTRACT

Implement command-line entry points in the SAME codebase:
  python -m qqq_sim                 launch GUI;
  python -m qqq_sim audit-data      read-only schema/coverage audit;
  python -m qqq_sim snapshot        save a causal selected as-of snapshot;
  python -m qqq_sim replay          run a deterministic headless replay;
  python -m qqq_sim build-model     explicit resumable offline build;
  python -m qqq_sim validate-pack   validate the actual loaded pack bytes;
  python -m qqq_sim export          export an existing frozen session/result.
Each command parses typed arguments, loads the complete relevant profile, and
prints/returns structured errors; audit/snapshot/replay never trigger build-model.
Help text describes every input and default already specified in this document.

Use a dedicated 64-bit Python 3.12 virtual environment as the initial build target.
Dependency set is PySide6, pyqtgraph, NumPy, SciPy, PyArrow, DuckDB, tzdata and the
chosen explicitly specified model runtime; development adds pytest and the
selected Qt-test helper. The implementer resolves installable binary versions,
runs the real tests, then freezes exact versions/hashes in the generated lockfile.
This is build validation work, not reliance on a missing algorithm document.
Do not change the user's system Python or invent a tested version combination.

Package initially as a standalone Windows directory using pyside6-deploy:
  create a tiny importable main entry file with guarded QApplication startup;
  run pyside6-deploy --init against that entry to generate deployment settings;
  set standalone mode and a dedicated output directory in those settings;
  include Qt platform plugin, required image-format plugins, tzdata and the
  explicitly needed numeric/runtime native libraries; exclude unused Qt modules;
  build using the frozen environment and preserve the deployment configuration;
  keep raw archives, model packs and writable runtime directories separate from
  the executable; resolve their user-configured absolute paths at startup.
The command spelling/options must pass the installed tool's own argument parser
in the build environment; no reader needs a separate design specification.
Record actual resolved versions and executed command in the release manifest.

On a clean Windows 10 x64 test account without the build environment, launch,
load a small synthetic fixture, render chart/chain, submit a simulated spread,
advance/fill/close, load a tiny validation pack, save/restart/export and test at
100%,150%,200% display scaling. Check no compiler, broker login, network service
or elevated permission is needed for ordinary runtime. Generated dependency
notices accompany distribution. The real user archives are never bundled or
written by the application. A missing pack offers the explicit offline-build
workflow and does not hide a build inside an Analyze click.

APP-C14. LOCAL ACCEPTANCE FIXTURES AND REFERENCE-TOPIC COVERAGE

These tests supplement the existing DT/AT/PT/OT cases and have exact expected
outcomes; they need no script or document outside this file:
  A. At D=10:01, a 10:00-labeled start-stamped underlying candle and a
     10:00:59.900 option event are visible; 10:01:00.000 is not. After submission
     at D, that past 10:00:59.900 quote cannot fill the new order.
  B. A normal canonical session has 390 scheduled labels 09:30..15:59. An extra
     source row labeled 16:00 is a separate boundary record, not candle 391.
  C. Replacing every future row's prices with 10^9, IVs with 4.0 and OI with 10^9
     leaves the earlier serialized snapshot, order decision and model inputs
     byte-identical, including its known contract universe and unit inference.
  D. One five-minute advance and five one-minute advances with identical submitted
     commands produce identical ledger hashes and final account values. An
     unresolved third-minute gap leaves both at the identical valid prefix.
  E. The APP-C05 two-leg spread fee fixture yields 47.50 net realized profit;
     H0=50 is not net-realized success even though gross profit=50.
  F. From 5,000 initial cash, credit 100/width500/fees2.25 gives cash5097.75,
     collateral500, available4597.75 and NLV4997.75 at mark liability100.
  G. A long option debit200 consumes cash200 plus fees; its collateral is zero.
     Marking the acquired asset at200 leaves equity lower only by fees.
  H. A short-put assignment for one standard K=100 contract creates +100 shares
     and -10000 strike cash, removes one short put and retains unrelated hedges.
     Its received premium remains in campaign cash; no intrinsic cash is added.
  I. Paying a dividend already accrued changes settled cash and receivable by
     opposite equal amounts, leaving P&L unchanged at payment.
  J. Two six-contract opening partial fills of one leg charge opening commission
     6 then4 under one order ID. A new cancel/replace order starts its own cap.
  K. A duplicated command or settlement ID produces no second posting. Crash
     after SQL COMMIT and before UI publication recovers the committed fill once.
  L. Stale worker results with a different selection/state/pack hash are not
     displayed as current and cannot submit a new order.
  M. The configured calendar's 2026-11-27 early-close scenario contains 210
     scheduled underlying labels 09:30..12:59; its final candle is available13:00.
  N. An account cannot bypass a prior realized loss by assigning the next trade a
     new campaign ID; the session aggregate net loss bound still includes it.
  O. Missing official fixing produces a typed strict lifecycle stop; choosing a
     proxy scenario marks the result RTH_PROXY and never marks actual assignment
     observed. A close instruction without a matching quote remains unfilled.

Internal coverage of the former reference topics, now fully specified here:
  Opening/closing commissions and fee schedules -> APP-C05.
  Regulatory charge rates/waiver/date clocks -> APP-C01 and APP-C05.
  QQQ hours, exchange/settlement calendars -> APP-C03.
  Exercise/request grouping -> APP-C05 and APP-C08.
  Account margin concepts, component parameters and broker-dependent limits
    -> APP-C01 and APP-C06.
  Wider-wing condors, cash-secured puts and hedge allocation -> APP-C06.
  Current intraday-margin/PDT distinctions and deficit controls -> APP-C08.
  Cash-account funding and historical settlement changes -> APP-C03 and APP-C07.
  ETF physical delivery, exercise by exception and assignment -> APP-C08.
  Risk-desk intervention and DNE/exercise cutoffs -> APP-C08.
  Gross/midpoint/net account P&L -> APP-C06 and APP-C07 plus Section 11.
  Dividends, borrow costs, account permissions and mixed-expiry limitations
    -> APP-C01, APP-C06, APP-C07 and APP-C08.
  QQQ and option-only combo tick behavior -> APP-C04.
  Qt table-model/thread ownership -> APP-C09.
  pyqtgraph candlestick item and Parquet/CSV reads -> APP-C10.
  Python spawned workers and process lifetime -> APP-C11.
  Qt/Windows deployment -> APP-C13.

The applicable P50 formulas, numerical reference algorithms, model-pack building,
and managed-control instructions remain together in their P50 sections of THIS
file. The account/UI/storage rules here do not authorize substituting an endpoint
probability for first hit, changing a campaign target, hiding missing data, or
running the expensive reference engine during a normal fast query.

APP-C15. A CONCRETE LIMITED OPERATIONAL CERTIFICATE FOR SHORT SPREADS

To keep the strict supported domain useful without inventing a real-world
guarantee, implement SIMULTANEOUS_DELIVERY_HEDGE_V1 as an explicitly selected
operational model. Its certificate must visibly include this model name. It is
not equivalent to the adverse delayed-notice scenarios in APP-C08. The following
assumptions are part of this model, not claims about a broker's actual handling:
  - Every short contract is allocated one still-owned same-expiry protective
    long of the same right, multiplier and standard share deliverable.
  - An assignment becomes known while the protective exercise can still be
    processed, including joint expiration handling. There is no hidden notice lag.
  - Protection is a pre-authorized standing simulated lifecycle instruction,
    recorded at entry before its applicable instruction cutoff; it does not
    depend on inventing a timely later user request.
  - Assigned short quantity and the corresponding protective long exercise are
    one atomic delivery batch. The model nets shares and strike cash within that
    batch before checking financing; it has no intrabatch full-strike funding call.
  - Long protective exercise at its strike succeeds for the assigned quantity,
    even if that long is out of the money. No stock remains after that paired batch.
  - The supported contract does not have a special deliverable or corporate-action
    change; event fees are bounded by the complete fee profile.
  - New orders cannot remove needed protection unless all resulting states pass
    the same exact certificate; partial fills are in whole protected ratio units.
These assumptions are deliberately stronger than actual operational guarantees.
A user may simulate within them, and their certificate is
VERIFIED_WITHIN_DECLARED_MODEL only. Delayed notice, unavailable protective
exercise, unnetted funding, broken protection or unknown stock exposure changes
the certificate to UNKNOWN/FAILED; it never silently uses the easier profile.

For one protected credit vertical with short and long strikes Ks and Kl,
physical paired delivery has net strike cash no worse than
  -m*abs(Ks-Kl) per assigned ratio unit
and leaves zero shares. Its worst collateral consumption is therefore the
width times multiplier and assigned units plus finite exercise/assignment fees.
For a condor, adverse contrary exercises can affect both wings: operational
width reserve is the SUM of both wing widths, even though its same-expiry
terminal payoff collateral uses only the larger width. Keep those distinct.
This difference prevents an apparently safe wider-wing payoff calculation from
proving a stronger assignment statement it does not support.

Let Cc_net be campaign trade/income cash net of all expenses recognized so far,
with no fees counted twice. For each allocated pair let eta=+1 for a call and
eta=-1 for a put. Its adverse delivery-cash bound is
  m*paired_quantity*max(eta*(K_long-K_short),0).
W_delivery is the sum of these bounds, including both sides of a condor.
A credit vertical gives its absolute width; a favorable debit-vertical pairing
can give zero adverse delivery cash. Let F_delivery be
  5.00*(number of remaining short contracts + protective long contracts that
        could be exercised to neutralize them).
This per-contract fragmentation bound is conservative relative to the profile's
per-leg/batch charge and remains valid if separate notices split the groups.
Use the managed model's remaining total future-expense budget E_remaining,
default 100.00 USD, inclusive of delivery fees and all other future costs. Require
E_remaining >= F_delivery. The optional-action reserve is
  R_optional = E_remaining - F_delivery;
F_delivery is a part of E_remaining, never an extra second reserve. The current
protected poststate has the supported operational lower bound
  final_campaign_net >= Cc_net - W_delivery - E_remaining.
After an actual future expense f, decrement E_remaining by f and include f once
in recognized campaign expenses. Accept no action that spends past this finite
budget or leaves too little for the new poststate's mandatory delivery fees.
Unused expense budget is not a booked expense or actual trading loss.
Unrelated long options have nonnegative terminal value and may contribute zero
to this conservative lower bound; they are not subtracted again after their
purchase debit already entered Cc_net. Already completed campaign cash and all
previous rolls remain in Cc_net. Add independently known liabilities exactly once.

At each allowed future action, check this lower bound and all eligible partial/
no-fill states again, with the same original H0 and cumulative history. Every
accepted transition preserves the cap; it is not enough to check only the first
spread. A limit order cannot guarantee a fill, so no-fill keeps the current
protected exposure and its bound. Success may fail even while the modeled loss
bound survives. Real-world trading uncertainty is not converted into a fictitious
probability-one fill.

Make fee bounds enforceable in the explicitly conditional protected-order model
of APP-C04: each PROTECTED_LIMIT ticket has max_total_fees_usd, default 50.00.
A quote opportunity whose complete fees exceed that cap does not satisfy that
simulated order's declared fill conditions. This is not an implicit cancellation
power over an ordinary accepted MARKET/LIMIT order. Per-action costs must fit
E_remaining while retaining the required mandatory-delivery sub-reserve. The
all-in close cost constraint tests premium debit plus complete fees, not merely net option premium. Thus large individual call-sale proceeds
and their sales fee cannot invalidate a previously claimed fixed fee reserve.
A cap prevents an admissible action; it is not permission to truncate the fee
actually owed on a committed or externally supplied event. Assignment fees have
the separate F_delivery bound and are not suppressed by an order fee cap.

Account support also checks available cash against every net delivery batch and
all other held collateral/reservations. The paired model has no residual borrow
interest or dividend obligation because zero shares survive the atomic batch.
If an event leaves shares, use APP-C07/08 accounting and withdraw this certificate.
The observation-limited trading archive still cannot prove these assumptions.

Explicit fixture: initial one-unit width5 credit100, actual fees2.25 and no
previous loss. Cc_net=97.75, W_delivery=500, F_delivery=10. With the default
E_remaining=100 the conservative modeled loss bound is 502.25. With an explicitly
restricted E_remaining=10 and no optional fee-consuming future action allowance,
it is 412.25. Both pass the 1,000 modeled loss cap and 5,000 account cap with
initial cash5,000. The terminal payoff loss bound before future delivery charges
remains 402.25; it is a different number. The same case with delayed assignment
notice has no such automatic certificate. This provides useful explicit strict-model short-spread cases
without pretending that every short ETF structure is universally guaranteed.

31. INTERNAL COMPLETENESS AND TRACEABILITY CHECKS

31.1 One-file implementation checklist

The implementation engineer must be able to perform all of the following using
the normative instructions in this file:

  P50 definitions and cash accounting:
    SRC-D 13-15; SRC-E 02/03/06; APP 11/14; P50-R and P50-M.
    Signed quantities, premium cash, finite/infinite payoff extrema, original
    dollar target, gross versus net basis, expiry versus deadline and actual
    managed realization are all explicitly defined.

  P50 reference computation:
    SRC-D 10-21 plus P50-R.
    Normalize inputs, resolve IV, solve American prices, locate every target
    region, propagate common-underlying paths, monitor eligible times, count
    terminal-only events, quantify error, report and checkpoint reproducibly.

  P50 model construction and normal query:
    SRC-E 04-09/12-18; APP 15-18; P50-M.
    Generate designs and labels, train/validate/save the pack, apply domain and
    uncertainty gates, evaluate a new exact state, generate feasible actions,
    evaluate the managed policy, and return a realizable joint risk envelope.

  Historical QQQ inputs:
    SRC-B and SRC-C in full; APP 05-08; APP-C.
    Scan actual local data, normalize units/timestamps, respect availability,
    maintain canonical OHLCV and option identities, quarantine ambiguity, keep
    OI and every future observation from leaking into earlier decisions.

  Replay, trading and accounting:
    APP 07-13/18-20/24; APP-C.
    Step a single clock, execute only eligible later events, conserve positions
    and money, group and round costs deterministically, reserve capital, handle
    assignment/stock/settlement/dividend obligations, and preserve campaign limits.

  Desktop and persistence:
    APP 03-05/08-10/19-23; APP-C.
    Build the Qt models/chart/ticket/panels, apply version-gated results on the
    GUI thread, run bounded workers, transact and checkpoint, restore branches,
    package the Windows application and reproduce an exported calculation.

  Acceptance:
    SRC-D 26; SRC-E 16; P50-R/P50-M/APP-C fixtures; APP 24-28.
    A complete implementation passes the supported-scope tests, reports measured
    numerical/performance results, and abstains explicitly outside that scope.

31.2 How to interpret inputs and generated artifacts

Named Python modules, configuration records, model packs and test directories
describe artifacts TO CREATE. Their contents and behavior are specified here;
they are not existing files that the reader must find to recover instructions.
The two D:\QQQ archive roots contain market observations, not missing formulas.
Real market values cannot be reconstructed by an implementation plan. The
synthetic/scenario fixtures permit implementation testing without falsely
presenting generated values as historical observations.

Live transport and other-product compatibility requirements remain separately
scoped. QQQ historical support is not contingent on creating an authenticated
broker connector, obtaining an earlier terminal script, or comparing a private
broker probability display. Vendor parity is not a release prerequisite or an
automatic consequence of passing this file's numerical tests.

31.3 Specification consistency gates

Before releasing code or revising this file, check:
  - Every instructional citation is an in-file section/ID.
  - Every algorithm referenced by the supported app has a definition here.
  - No benchmark or milestone requires unavailable legacy source code.
  - No rate, quote, IV, historical rule or official close is silently invented.
  - All five work parts and their M0-M9 gates remain present.
  - All SRC-E success/tests, SRC-D mathematical/reporting invariants, and QQQ
    data-contract constraints retain an active or explicitly conditional mapping.
  - P50 reference, fast HOLD and managed results have compatible declared inputs
    and separately named events; one model's probability cannot label another.
  - Risk assumptions are visible and do not become claims of unconditional
    real-world loss guarantees. Costs and campaign cashflows are counted once.
  - The final artifact is this one complete file; it does not delegate required
    technical instructions to a companion document.
